waiting (recorded in `FEATURES.md` as "decided by Claude, unconfirmed"), or a step only the author can
do. Review, then confirm, change, or delete the entry.

## 2026-10-15 · Notes redesign · Small fixes (2026-10-15) (plan `docs/research/notes-redesign-2026-10-15/fixes-1.md`)
- Question: the calls the plan marked "decided by Claude" in the three small fixes, and the ones met while building them.
- 1, Ms Scribe's prose suggestions as tracked changes in the scene. Chosen (from the plan): each suggestion she makes in Write or Vibe now also shows **inline in the scene** as a tracked change (struck words, the new ones, ✓ and ✗), through the edit flow's own machinery; one hidden run row per chat turn that proposed prose, never listed among edit passes or edit-flow results. The chat card stays: Accept / Reject on the card or in the text settle both; a card settled in the text reads as applied, or "Rejected: …"; a passage edited since reads "Could not apply: … (the text changed since she suggested it)". Nothing is applied on its own, in any mode. Also decided while building: (a) the hidden row is an ordinary `edit_pass` row marked in its `flow` column (no migration, no new pass type that the Edits list would show); its rows are kept apart from the edit passes' cap of 50 and have their own (the newest 200 turns; an older turn's suggestions leave the text with its row). (b) The card's buttons read **Accept** / **Reject** for these (Apply / Skip stay for every other chat edit), with a line "Also marked in the scene: accept or reject it there or here." (c) An accepted suggestion keeps an Undo on the card for the session, whether accepted on the card or in the text (the new words found again and the old put back, as the chat's own Apply undoes); a cut has none (the editor's Undo takes it back, as for the edit flow's fixes). (d) A turn you stopped or left before it answered rejects its suggestions in the text. (e) Her turn's proposal is settled by the chat when no card waits (as before), not by the tracked rows, so a turn with a note edit and a rejected suggestion still counts as partly accepted. (f) The edit passes' "could not be applied" warning now says "since it was suggested" (it said "since the pass", wrong for her and for the edit flow). Alternatives: draw them in the scene only, no card; a new pass type `scribe`; no cap on her rows; no card Undo at all (the editor's Undo only).
- To change it (1): `src/main/scribe2/tracked.ts`, `src/main/editPass/editPassStore.ts` (`createScribePass`, `SCRIBE_PASS_RETENTION_MAX`, `evictPasses`), `src/main/ipc/handlers.ts` (`ai:scribe`, `editPass:settle`), `src/renderer/features/ai/assistantStore.ts` (`acceptTracked`, `rejectTracked`, `onTrackedSettled`), `src/renderer/features/ai/AgentTurn.tsx`. Contract (additive): `AiScribeResult` changes and `AgentChange` carry `trackedId`; event `scribe:trackedSettled`.
- 2, templates known before she writes. Chosen (from the plan): the notes block she always gets ends, once, with the folders that have a template and their headings (`Characters → template: Role, Appearance, Wants, …`), in the cached part; a new prompt version **`scribe.v4`** (v3 kept as shipped) adds one rule line: "A new page in a folder listed under Folder templates is written under its headings, in order; leave a heading empty rather than invent." `create_note`'s local layout stays the backstop. Also decided while building: (a) a rule line was needed after all (without it the list is data she may ignore, and "leave it empty rather than invent" is an instruction), so v4 rather than a header that instructs; (b) a subfolder that only inherits its folder's template is not listed (Characters › Minor follows Characters); a subfolder with its own template is; (c) the list is capped at 20 folders and 20 headings each (an author's long template ends with "…"); (d) the list goes along even when Notes has no pages yet; (e) the edit flow's Update notes does not get the list (its prompt is unchanged; its new pages still take the local layout). Alternatives: the list in the tool result only (as phase 6); a header line that instructs with no new prompt version; every subfolder listed; the edit flow's notes block too (an `editNotes` prompt version).
- To change it (2): `src/shared/notebookTemplates.ts` (`templatedFolders`), `src/main/scribe2/source.ts` (`folderTemplatesOf`), `src/main/scribe2/context.ts` (`folderTemplatesBlock`), `src/shared/scribe2.ts` (the caps), `src/main/ai/prompts/scribe.v4.ts`. Live fidelity for `scribe.v4` is not run; `npm run eval:ai -- -u` regenerates the token report and the cost registry.
- 3, Rewrite in Vibe. Chosen (from the plan): Rewrite in my voice is bolder in **Vibe**: it may tighten hard, cut, reorder sentences, sharpen the dialogue and add a missing beat, within the selection, keeping its point of view, tense, names and facts and your voice; in Write and Brainstorm it is as before. New prompt version **`rewrite.v6`** (v5 and v4 kept as shipped) with the Vibe line last in the user turn, after the cached part; the voice check and Accept / Reject unchanged. Vibe offers the bolder version only, not both (decided by Claude: you picked Vibe for bolder). Also decided while building: (a) v6 is v4's request plus the line (v4 is what a story memory v1 project sends; v5 is v2's, dormant), and Write and Brainstorm keep sending v4 itself, so their requests and the local cache are byte for byte as before; (b) the mode comes from the app with each rewrite (an optional `mode` on `ai:rewrite`, filled from the chat mode), not read in main from the saved settings, so a mode switched a moment before is never missed; (c) both regenerates stay as they were in Vibe: the voice check's regenerate (a draft off your voice is asked again with the problem named) and your Regenerate… with a note send `rewriteRegen.v4`, the close version, since both correct a draft; (d) the length cap is unchanged (half again the passage plus a little), so "a missing beat" stays short; (e) the Vibe tooltip under the chat box is unchanged (it does not mention Rewrite). Alternatives: v6 sent in every mode with a line per mode; both versions shown in Vibe; a bolder regenerate too (`rewriteRegen.v6`); a longer cap in Vibe; the tooltip and the website's docs page saying Vibe's Rewrite is bolder.
- To change it (3): `src/main/ai/prompts/rewrite.v6.ts` (`REWRITE_V6_VIBE_LINE`), `src/main/ai/rewrite.ts` (`vibe`), `src/renderer/features/editor/rewriteStore.ts` (`send`), `src/shared/ipc/contract.ts` (`ai:rewrite` `mode`, additive). Live fidelity for `rewrite.v6` is not run; `npm run eval:ai -- -u` regenerates the token report and the cost registry.

## 2026-10-15 · Notes redesign · Note templates (2026-10-15) (plan `docs/research/notes-redesign-2026-10-15/templates.md`)
- Question: how templates work for authors who do not like free-form pages (your "later some templates"; you said "keep going").
- Chosen (from the plan, decided by Claude): **New from template** in the Notes toolbar and **New from template ›** in a folder's menu, with nine built-ins (Character, Place, Group / faction, Item, Magic or power system, Event, Timeline, Chapter plan, Style guide); each makes an ordinary page, fully editable, with a title to type over ("New character") and its headings. **Your own templates** are the pages in a Notes folder named Templates, listed first under "My templates"; a page's menu has **Save as template** (a copy into a top-level Templates folder, made the first time). **An empty Notes** offers *Start with folders* (Characters, Places, World, Plot, Timeline and a Style guide page) or *Start blank*, beside the file drop. **Ms Scribe** does not read the Templates folder with your notes; when she makes a page in a folder with a template (Characters → Character, …, or one of yours of that name), the page follows its headings, and the tool's answer tells her which she filled and which are empty. No prompt change (it rides in the tool result), no migration.
- Also decided while building: (1) Each built-in is headings only (level 2, an empty line under each); no hint text to delete. Character: Role, Appearance, Wants, Fears, Voice and speech, Powers, Relationships, Arc, First appears. Place: Where it is, What it looks like, Sounds and smells, Who lives there, History, What happens here, First appears. Group: Purpose, Leader, Members, Beliefs, Allies and enemies, Resources, History, First appears. Item: What it is, Appearance, What it does, Who has it, Where it came from, Rules and limits, First appears. Magic or power system: What it is, Who can use it, How it works, Costs and limits, What it cannot do, How it looks and feels, Known users, Open questions. Event: When, Where, Who was there, What happened, Why it matters, Who knows, Shown in. Timeline: Before the story, During the story, After the story. Chapter plan: Goal, Point of view, Setting, Beats, Conflict, Turn, Ends on, Notes. Style guide: Spelling choices, Capitalised terms, Numbers, Dialogue punctuation, Swearing; its page is titled exactly "Style guide", which Copyediting reads (one shared constant now). (2) Folder names that take a built-in: Characters / Character / Cast / People; Places / Locations; Groups / Factions / Organisations; Items / Objects / Artifacts; Magic / Powers / Magic system(s) / Power system(s); Events; Timeline(s); Chapter plans / Chapters / Outline. World and Plot take none (they hold mixed pages). A subfolder takes its nearest folder's template (Characters › Minor → Character). (3) A Templates folder counts at any depth and in any case; Save as template uses (or makes) the top-level one and is not offered for a page already inside Templates. (4) A page from one of your templates takes its title and whole text; a built-in page's title is "New <kind>" (Timeline and Style guide keep their own names). (5) Her page is laid out **locally**: a line naming a section ("## Role", "Role", "Role:") starts it, "Role: a smuggler" starts it with the rest of the line, any other line goes to the section above it (or above the first section); a section she wrote replaces the template's lines under it, the rest stay as the template has them; nothing she wrote is dropped. A label line in your own template ("Age:") filled with one line reads "Age: 34". She fills an empty section later with `edit_note` and `section` (each counts toward the message's 8 writes). The edit flow's Update notes makes its new pages the same way. (6) She sees your templates only on asking: `list_notes` ends with one line naming them, `open_note` reads one by its title; they are never in the notes block, the names she matches, or the note pages the six v1 prose features send. (7) The starter set makes each folder only when the top level has none of that name, and the Style guide page only when there is none at the top; nothing is opened. (8) The template menu reuses the shared context menu, which gained group headings ("My templates", "Built-in"; shown only when you have templates of your own).
- Alternatives: hint text under each heading; label lines ("Role: ") instead of headings; templates stored apart from notes; one Templates folder at the top level only; a refusal that asks her to resend the page on the template (an extra call, and it would lose the edit flow's batch writes); a `scribe.v4` rule line telling her the folder templates before she writes (cheaper fills, a prompt version); World → Magic or power system, Plot → Chapter plan; opening the Style guide after Start with folders.
- To change it: `src/shared/notebookTemplates.ts` (templates, folder names, `layoutOnTemplate`, `templateForFolder`), `src/renderer/features/notebook/notebookStore.ts` (`createFromTemplate`, `saveAsTemplate`, `startWithFolders`), `templateMenu.ts`, `NotebookTemplateMenu.tsx`, `NotebookTree.tsx`, `NotesTab.tsx`, `src/main/scribe2/tools.ts` (`create_note`, `list_notes`, `open_note`), `src/main/scribe2/source.ts`, `src/main/ai/context/storyBible.ts` (`namedNotePages`).

## 2026-10-15 · Notes redesign · Vibe and Notes in prompts (2026-10-15) (plan `docs/research/notes-redesign-2026-10-15/vibe.md`)
- Question: what the v1 prompts carry in the story bible's place while the old story bible is archived, and how bold Ms Scribe gets in Vibe.
- Chosen (from the plan, decided by Claude): **Notes in the bible's slot.** Ghost text, Rewrite, the old chat, editor's notes, What next and the suggested notes send, while the old story bible is archived, no tag bank, scene tags or sheets, but the scene's place, its neighbours (titles, details, summaries) and the **note pages the scene names**: a page whose title, or a capitalised word of its title of 4+ letters ("Tash" in "Tash — powers"), stands in the scene's text, or in its synopsis or scene notes (any case there), most often named first; titles only, no AI; Ms Scribe's own matcher. Each page is cut to 600 characters and then to the room left within the block's old budget (400 tokens; 150 for ghost text, where a page gives its first lines). Same heading, no new prompt versions; a changed page misses the local cache. **Vibe (`scribe.v3`):** she proposes prose changes herself, unasked, and may go big (a whole paragraph, a cut, a reordered beat, a missing line of dialogue), at most **3** an answer, each with its reason in a line; each is a tracked change in the chat that only your Apply puts in the book. Write proposes only when you ask; Brainstorm unchanged. Ghost text in Vibe keeps its length cap (a longer continuation is Draft with AI's job).
- Also decided while building: (1) the **scene steer** (the scene's tone, content, thread and theme tags in ghost text, Rewrite, What next and the old chat's Author mode) is left out while archived too, since tags are hidden data. (2) A capitalised title word is not a name when it is a common word ("What", "When", "With", "From", "That", … in a small fixed list), so "What the river knows" is matched only by its full title. (3) In `scribe.v3` **every tool is described in every mode** and the mode's line closes the `# Now` block, so switching Brainstorm/Write/Vibe never changes the cached prefix (about 2¢ saved per switch on your book); Brainstorm's line says it changes nothing, and main still refuses a write tool the mode may not use (as before). Brainstorm's rules grow about 200 tokens (cached), Write's 15, Vibe's 80. (4) The cap counts prose suggestions only; note edits still count toward the message's 8 writes. (5) The data-sharing rows of the six features add "while the old story bible is switched off, the note pages the scene names instead of the tags and sheets", and the Write and Vibe mode meanings now say Write suggests prose when you ask and Vibe proposes bolder changes on its own (up to three); both mirrored on the website's docs page. (6) The old chat's `#name` references (the scene notes of documents carrying that tag) still resolve while archived; you write them yourself, and v1 chat messages go to Ms Scribe now.
- Alternatives: pages chosen by meaning search or an AI pick (costs a call); pages named in the open scene only (not synopsis and notes); a per-page share of the budget instead of most-named first; keep the scene steer; keep write tools out of Brainstorm's rules (no cache across a mode switch, as `scribe.v2`); no cap, or 2 or 5 in Vibe; a longer ghost text in Vibe; the proposal shown inline in the editor rather than as the chat's tracked change.
- To change it: `src/main/ai/context/storyBible.ts` (`namedNotePages`), `src/shared/storyBible.ts` (`STORY_BIBLE_NOTE_CHARS`, `noteLine`), `src/main/scribe2/context.ts` (`pageNames`, `pagesNamedIn`), `src/main/ai/context/sceneSteer.ts`, `src/main/ai/prompts/scribe.v3.ts` (`SCRIBE_V3_MODE_RULES`), `src/shared/scribe2.ts` (`SCRIBE_VIBE_PROSE_MAX`), `src/shared/aiSettings.ts` (`STORY_BIBLE_SENDS`, `ASSISTANT_MODE_MEANING`). Live fidelity for `scribe.v3` is not run; `npm run eval:ai -- -u` regenerates the token report and the cost registry.

## 2026-10-15 · Notes redesign · Notes phase 3 (2026-10-15): notes updates, the step, the chat, automatic after a chapter (plan `docs/research/notes-redesign-2026-10-15/phase3.md`)
- Question: the calls the plan marked "decided by Claude", and the ones met while building it.
- Chosen (from the plan): **the step** is *Update notes from this scene / this chapter / the whole book* at the end of the Manuscript tree's right-click menu, and an **Update notes…** button in the Notes tab's toolbar (the open scene's chapter; the scene itself when it sits in no chapter; the whole book when no scene is open); both open the edit flow pre-filled (Update notes, Quick; Deep for the whole book) and show the price before Run; hidden while Use AI (or the edit flow) is off and in story memory v2. **The chat**: in Brainstorm, a request to update the notes opens the flow the same way; Write and Vibe unchanged. **Automatic**: Settings › AI › "Update my notes after I finish a chapter", off by default, per project in the settings (the book decides), with the price of one chapter under it and a daily cap (10 by default, 1–50, counted by runs started, reset at local midnight). It runs when you open a scene in another chapter (or a scene in no chapter) after a scene of the chapter you left was saved from the editor; a note page, the Outline, a folder or closing the project never triggers it. Main skips (not queues) while an edit run is going, and when the chapter's text is the same as at its last automatic update. An automatic run is kept apart from your own run of that chapter (marked "automatic" in Earlier runs) and never opens the panel.
- Also decided while building: the chat request is recognised on this machine by its wording, matched whole ("update my notes", "update the notes from this chapter / the whole book", "can you refresh my notes with this scene?"), so no request is sent and no prompt changed; "update my notes on Mara with …" stays a chat turn. "Written in" means the editor's own save (your typing, accepting or rejecting a tracked change); Ms Scribe's edits do not count. The text compared is the chapter's scenes' words, so a reorder or a formatting change alone does not run it again. The count and the text are recorded as a run **starts**, so a failed run (no key, no balance, rate limit) is not retried until the chapter changes again; it leaves one line with the cause and no Retry. The activity line counts distinct pages ("Notes updated from Chapter 3: 2 pages"); Open shows the run in the edit flow with each note edit's Undo; a run that changed nothing, or was stopped, leaves no line. A skipped leave is written to the app's log (not when the switch is off). The price per chapter is the whole book's Quick Update notes price divided by its requests (one a chapter) on your fast model. Every edit-flow run now tells the windows as it starts (so the activity bar follows an automatic one). The cost registry note for `editFlow` mentions the automatic trigger (needs `npm run eval:ai -- -u`).
- Alternatives: the step only in the Notes tab, or only in the tree; the chat route in every mode, or taught to the router (a prompt change); the automatic update on leaving a scene, or on closing the project; queue a leave while a run goes; keep the hash only after a run succeeds (a failure would retry on the next leave and could spend each time); one kept run per chapter shared by your runs and the automatic ones; store the switch app-wide.
- To change it: `src/main/editFlow/autoNotes.ts` (the gate), `src/renderer/features/editFlow/chapterTriggers.ts` (the trigger), `src/shared/editFlow.ts` (`notesUpdateAsk`, the cap, `scopeKey`), `src/renderer/features/editFlow/editFlowStore.ts` (`openNotesUpdate`, `notesUpdateTarget`), `src/renderer/features/manuscript/contextMenuItems.ts`, `src/renderer/features/notebook/NotesTab.tsx`, `src/renderer/features/editFlow/NotesAutoSection.tsx`, `src/renderer/features/activity/activityJobs.ts`.

## 2026-10-15 · Notes redesign · Fresh start: the old story bible put away (plan `docs/research/notes-redesign-2026-10-15/fresh-start.md`)
- Question: how far "hidden and no longer read" reaches, and where your uploaded docs land.
- Chosen: one switch, Settings › Archived features › **Old story bible** (archived by default, in every project, the existing one included), stored with the other archive switches in the project's editor settings; main reads the same row. While archived: the sidebar lists Manuscript, Notes, Outline, Timeline and Edits (no categories, Index, Threads, Library, To do, Changes, no New category…); the header's Tags and References toggles, their columns, focus mode's References, Insert › Character/Setting/World-building note, View › References/Tags and Tools › Tags are gone; the editor has no `#` suggestion or tag menus (asked at typing and click time, so the switch never rebuilds the editor); Outline has no Threads view; the Manuscript tag filter is gone (a filter left on is lifted); search has no sheet chips or tag filter; Check consistency and the continuity notes are gone; the summary.v4 conversion dialog does not show. Main: Ms Scribe reads no sheets or uploads; the summary job stores the summary (and its card) but no tags, facts, sheets, threads or Changes rows, and clears none; no background consistency check, To do sync, sheet sync, mention scan, or the v1 conversions on open, so the old rows stay exactly as they were. Switching it back on brings everything back and the mention scan and To do list catch up at once. Docs: on open, while archived, a project whose Library has files gets a Notes folder **Docs**, one page per file (read again from the original, so Word and Markdown keep their headings; the stored text when the original is gone; images left out), recorded so it never runs twice; a project with an empty Library is left alone. A new project's wizard files go straight to Notes › Docs (no Library, no AI sort), and files dropped on the window go to Notes.
- Also decided: the switch is story memory v1's only (v2 is dormant and keeps its own records). The summary prompt is unchanged (no new version, so no re-summarising of the book): it still names the story-bible names the scene contains; only what it answers about them is no longer stored. Timeline stays (it was not in the list to hide). Not hidden: Help › Switch to the new story memory…, the v1 chat agent's sheet and tag tools (recap, Write this), the scene details' Location/POV suggestions from tag names. Contract: `notebook:import` takes optional `paths` (the wizard's files).
- Alternatives: convert the sheets to pages (rejected by you); hide Timeline too; copy the Library into Docs even when the story bible is switched on.
- To change it: `src/shared/archivedFeatures.ts` (`isStoryBibleArchived`), `src/renderer/features/shell/sectionList.ts` (`buildFreshStartSections`), `src/main/notebook/libraryDocs.ts`, the `bibleArchived()` gates in `src/main/ipc/handlers.ts`.

## 2026-10-15 · F-16.17 · Sixth live run; concrete-only suggestions (plan Suggestions.md)
- Question: the sixth live run (`reader.v7`, reports 2026-10-15T14:40 and 14:48 UTC) failed: suggestion precision 42.1 % and 56.3 % (41 and 24 unjudged), one planted negative suggested (the Captain "owed Mr. Varrow money" as `detail:wealth`). The concrete slots stayed about 90 % right; traits and history were about half noise. Your decision (confirmed): concrete-only suggestions, then the copy trial.
- Chosen: the reader goes back to `reader.v6` (prompt and slot set: traits and history in `NOT_READ_SLOTS` again, one set); the pipeline sends `reader.v6`; *Rebuild memory*, the backlog and the switch-over price from the `reader.v6:*` rows, and the cost registry's reader row is pinned to `reader.v6` (`costRegistry.ts`, beside `agent.v8`). `reader.v7` stays in the catalogue with its file, golden and eval cases, but nothing in the app sends it. Everything from the Suggestions build stays (every fact a suggestion, Accept/Dismiss, the deck, the panel). The replays are back to v6's: the planted traits and histories are refused as `notRead`, and the Captain's two fathers are no findable C7 pair. The sixth run's unjudged facts are not judged (no new verdicts).
- Not run here: `npm run eval:ai -- -u` (the token report and cost registry: the reader row back to `reader.v6`; the worktree guard refuses "eval").
- Owed by you: no more test-book live runs for now; try suggestions on a copy of the real book.
- To change it: `src/shared/memory/slots.ts` (`NOT_READ_SLOTS`), `src/main/memory/reading/pipeline.ts`, `backlog.ts`, `src/main/memory/switchover/plan.ts`, `src/shared/aiCostNotes.ts`.

## 2026-10-14 · F-16.17, F-16.12, F-16.18, F-16.19 · Suggestions you approve (plan Suggestions.md)
- Question: five live runs never reached 95 % stored-fact precision, because a fact filed with nobody looking must be near perfect. Your decision and answers (confirmed): every fact a reading proves is a suggestion you accept or dismiss; traits and history read again, relation/role/clothing/objects off; nothing auto-applied; no limit per scene, best first. Everything below is decided by Claude, unconfirmed.
- Chosen:
  1. **State**: a claim state `suggested` (`src/shared/memory/claims.ts`, no migration). The pipeline stores what passes the gates and the checker as `suggested`; one rule, `isAccepted` (live or stale), is what every reader of memory uses: True now, sheets, ages, the Timeline, who knows what, threads, the contradiction rules and Check the book, Ms Scribe's instant cards and evidence. **Accepted = `live`**: lines stored live before this build count as accepted (no rewrite of your test copies).
  2. **Lifecycle**: a suggestion whose sentence changes, or whose scene stops being read (an Idea), is **dropped**, not kept stale: the next reading of its paragraph suggests it again if the text still says it. An accepted line goes stale and comes back live as before. A fact waiting on a new name becomes a suggestion on Yes (dropped when its sentence changed). A **Rebuild memory keeps the lines you accepted** while their sentences stand, whether or not it states them again; it replaces only its own suggestions.
  3. **Accept / Dismiss**: `memory:accept` (with `undo` for the toast's Undo), `memory:dismiss` (Wrong value's Dismissed row keyed by the sentence; Undo in the toast or in Dismissed brings it back as a suggestion), `memory:suggestions` (by scene, record or claim ids). **Undo is the toast and Dismissed, not the Changes log**: v2 does not show the Changes log (P1-D8). An accepted line is taken back later with its *Not right*.
  4. **Where you see them**: the status bar's "4 suggestions" (side work) opens their review in the assistant column on the review deck, grouped by character, *Accept all for Mara* on the rail, Accept / Later / Dismiss per card, applied at the end; Needs a look's "Suggestions from this scene" (after My to-dos, not in the badge) with ⓘ, Accept, Dismiss, Accept all; Rebuild memory's summary is the same deck for the rebuild's suggestions (its old *Not right* for a sheet's new lines is gone from the contract).
  5. **Who knows what and threads**: their knows lines and steps are suggestions too. Secrets and threads a reading makes are still records (the sidebar lists them; the grid shows no knower until you accept one). Scene cards stay automatic.
  6. **`reader.v7`**: v6 plus traits and history with v5's value rules (a lasting quality; their own past act told as fact; who does it, not who it is done to; where someone died is history); rules about 90 tokens more than v6. The spot checks now check suggestions (they measure the reader; a strong refusal deletes only a suggestion, never your line). The reader's near facts and who-knows lines are your accepted lines only.
  7. **The bar**: suggestion precision at least **85 %** (the plan's figure), safety bars unchanged. **The fifth run re-scored** with the main session's verdicts: run 1 **91.2 %** (31 of 34; 88.2 % with the calls turned), run 2 **82.8 %** (24 of 29; 86.2 % with the calls turned): run 2 misses 85 % as judged strictly. Attribution 100 %, literal 100 %, support 100 %, no negative, nothing from a never-read scene, in both.
  8. **Replays**: the knows, Ms Scribe, contradictions and switch-over replays accept every suggestion after reading (an author who accepts everything); the planted replay's two traits no gate refuses (Mrs. Harrow "quite disconcerted", "violent against him") are now suggestions you would dismiss; the Captain's two fathers are a findable C7 pair again.
- **Judgment calls** in the fifth run's verdicts: Ned Ashby location "the room" judged right; Ned Ashby detail:build "good-looking and gentlemanlike" judged wrong (wrong slot).
- Not run here: `npm run eval:ai -- -u` (token report and cost registry; the worktree guard refuses "eval") and the live run (no key).
- Owed by you: one live run, `MYTHSCRIBE_EVAL_LIVE=1 MYTHSCRIBE_EVAL_RUNS=2 OPENROUTER_API_KEY=sk-or-… npm run eval:ai -- memory`; then the suggestions in a development build (Read now on a scene, the status bar's count, accept and dismiss in the panel and the review, Rebuild memory's deck).
- To change it: `src/shared/memory/claims.ts` (`isAccepted`), `src/main/memory/reading/suggestions.ts`, `anchors.ts`, `waiting.ts`, `src/shared/memory/reconcile.ts`, `src/main/ai/prompts/reader.v7.ts`, `src/main/ai/eval/plantedScore.ts` (`PLANTED_PASS_BARS`), `src/renderer/features/memory/suggestions/`.

## 2026-10-14 · F-16.17, F-16.3 · Reader narrowed to concrete facts (plan ReaderNarrow.md)

## 2026-10-14 · F-16.17, F-16.3 · Reader precision: the third live run re-scored, and what changed (plan ReaderPrecision.md)
- Question: the third live run (21:35) passed the safety bars but about 1 in 10 of the facts stored on unlabelled sentences looked wrong. How is the reader made precise, measured over every stored fact, without patching instances?
- Confirmed by you: the main bar is **stored-fact precision ≥ 95 % over every stored fact**. Everything below is decided by Claude, unconfirmed.
- Chosen:
  1. **Labels (step 1).** Every one of the 90 facts the third run stored on an unlabelled sentence is judged by hand from the text alone (`storedVerdicts` in `src/main/memory/testBook/labels.ts`; a new list beside `claims`, which stay the expected readings, so no eval case or recall changes). Strict: the quote must state the fact of that named person; a trait lasts (never one act or one evening's mood); a report or someone's opinion stored as narration is wrong. **No existing label was changed.** Only the third run's report was on disk (each run overwrote the day's file; the report is now stamped to the minute), so the first two runs' unreviewed facts are judged only where the third run stored the same ones.
  2. **The third run re-scored: 63.8 % (74 of 116 stored facts right)**; 74.1 % (86 of 116) if every judgment call below went the other way. 41 wrong: by my reading of the new code gates about 14 of them would now be refused in code (not run against the live replies), the rest (acts as traits, rumours and plans as narration, acts as roles) left to the strong checker and `reader.v5`'s rules.
  3. **The bar in the live report**: right stored facts over every stored fact, a fact not judged yet counts wrong and is listed to judge (the run fails until it is judged; the JSON now carries every stored fact, so `scoreStored` re-scores it with new verdicts and no second run). "Quotes found" counts only quotes really missing from the text (bar stays 90 %; the third run's would be 94.6 %); refusals for an invented slot or a quote's length are reported as a cost, no bar. **Verifier pass is reported with no bar.** `MYTHSCRIBE_EVAL_RUNS=2` reads the book twice on fresh copies (each with its own 50¢ stop) and reports both.
  4. **`reader.v5`** (golden, catalogue, 8 eval cases): the field `voice` is now `src` (the parser reads either); what each open-ended slot holds (kinship is what they are to a named person, never the relative; a role is a position, never an act; a relation is how they stand to one person in at most 6 words; history is their own act told as fact, never a plan or a report); for history, role, relation and traits "r" is who does it; a he/she two people nearby could be is no one's; a place or thing has no location. Rules 1,480 tokens (v4 1,303; cap 1,550); same output cap as v4.
  5. **`verify.v4`** (golden, catalogue, 2 eval cases): the same open-ended rules for the checker, and a report or what others said is rumour (rules 697 tokens, v3 547). **The open-ended slots' facts (history, traits, relation, kinship, role) are checked on the strong tier** (DeepSeek V4 Pro) in their own batch per page, through the request path's tier (routing overrides still apply); everything else stays fast. Spot checks and *Not right* stay on `verify.v3`.
  6. **Code gates** (`ladder.ts`, before the checker, counted by reason): `pronounRivals` (a he, she or part's they with two or more people who fit in the quote's sentences and the nearest sentence before that names one: `ambiguous`; one who is not the subject: `subject`), `pronounOfOtherGender` (a he for a woman: `subject`), `actsOnSubject` (someone else does it: `actor`), `ageIsSpan` ("for forty years": `valueKind`), `relativeValue` ("his second daughter", "The Captain's father" as kinship: `possessed`), `relationValue` (a relation with no other record, over 6 words, or only people: `object`), and `slotFitsKind` (no location on a place; no location, role, kinship, clothing or objects on a thing). A green-eyes-style pronoun is now refused before the checker instead of waiting (it used to wait and be dropped at the next full read).
  7. **Offline**: every wrong fact the plan names is planted behind a gullible checker and refused with its reason (`memory.eval.ts`); the oracle replay asserts 100 % precision with nothing unjudged, the adversarial and planted replays ≥ 95 %; pipeline unit tests plant the same shapes.
  8. **Prices**: `reader.v5:first` 2,560 / 650, `:window` and `:card` 4,730 / 400, `verify.v4` 900 / 50 and `verify.v4:strong` 950 / 75 per page (`READING_V3_ROWS`); Rebuild memory and the backlog add the strong batch on the strong model: a 90-scene book of 800-token scenes stays under 50¢ (about 16¢ on the defaults by the rows: 8¢ reading and fast checks, 8¢ strong checks).
- **Your judgment calls** (each in `storedVerdicts` with `call: true`; change `right` to turn one): wrong as stored: Mrs. Harrow "quite disconcerted" (a passing state as a trait), Ashby "brought only six" (a report the girls heard), Varrow "ten thousand a year" (a report in circulation), Varrow "fine figure of a man" (the gentlemen's opinion), Varrow home "Elsdale" (an estate there, not said he lives there), Mrs. Harrow "most violent against him" and "dislike … resentment" (a feeling towards Varrow, not a trait), Johanna "admired" (one evening), Lise "does not lose much…" (her mother's opinion), Varrow "critical" (one look), Pruett "exultation" (one moment), Mr. Harrow "would ride over to Thornmere" (a plan, not history), Lottie "all smiles", Hobb role "opened the lodge gate", Ashby location "the hall" (she is handed in), the Captain clothing "boots in his hand" (carried, not worn), Mr. Harrow "noticed nothing at all", Lise "noticed a great deal" and "put it together" (acts as traits), the Captain trait "owed Mr. Varrow money" (a debt); right as stored: Mr. Lowther relation "brother-in-law", Mr. Lowther "merely looked the gentleman", Varrow "above his company", Lise location "Wexley" (Clara found her there), Mrs. Harrow history "laughed at a ball" (stored as now in Lise's memory), Lise role "guest", Mr. Dunmore "a married man now and very pleased with himself", Lottie "very happy now on most days".
- Also decided: a thing keeps a creature's eyes, hair, scars, age and alive (only the person slots above are refused); gender comes only from a title (Mr., Mrs., Miss, Lady, Sir…), so a name with none ("Lottie", "Hobb", "the Captain") may be meant by a he or a she, which refuses more pronoun facts (recall, not precision); the green-eyes label's reason still says the claim waits, its expectation (never stored) is unchanged.
- Not run here: `npm run eval:ai -- -u` (the worktree guard refuses commands containing "eval"; the main session runs it and commits the token report and the cost registry), and the live run (no key). The full `npm run test` runs the eval project's replays.
- Owed by you: the final live run, `MYTHSCRIBE_EVAL_LIVE=1 MYTHSCRIBE_EVAL_RUNS=2 OPENROUTER_API_KEY=sk-or-… npm run eval:ai -- memory`; judge any fact it lists as not judged yet, then the bar is final. Pass: switch the real project after the copy trial; fail: stop, no fourth round without you.
- To change it: `src/main/memory/testBook/labels.ts` (`storedVerdicts`), `src/main/ai/eval/plantedScore.ts` (`PLANTED_PASS_BARS`, `scoreStored`), `src/main/ai/prompts/reader.v5.ts`, `verify.v4.ts`, `src/main/memory/reading/pipeline.ts` (`STRONG_CHECK_SLOTS`), `src/shared/memory/ladder.ts`, `src/shared/memory/slots.ts`, `src/shared/aiCostNotes.ts`.

## 2026-10-14 · F-16.17, F-16.16 · Second live planted-error run of 2026-10-14: what failed and what changed
- Question: your second run (19:20, after `reader.v3`) passed attribution, literal and support precision and never-read, but failed quotes found (75.5 %), verifier pass (79.9 %) and one negative (h07 Johanna "the road"); "cut off at the cap" stayed at exactly 83.3 %; six wrong facts stood among the unreviewed. What is wrong, and how is it fixed without loosening a bar or changing a label?
- Root causes found:
  1. **The 83.3 % was the metric, and the cap still too small.** The report counted a reading as cut off when *any* reply in it was cut, recovered or not: a cut first page later read whole in halves, a retry half, a checker batch. With 400-token pages a reading made about six requests (109 reading requests for 18 scenes), so the share could not fall as pages shrank; it equals the old figure by coincidence of saturation. The 400-token pages, the caps and the half re-read *were* in effect on the live path: `runLivePlanted` reads through `readBook` → `runReading`, the app's pipeline, with the app's request path (`liveRequestDeps`: Auto routing, reasoning off for reader and verify, max_tokens clamped by the feature budget); nothing builds requests differently. But v3's own estimate for a full page with its card is 160 + 1.2 × 400 + 240 = 880 tokens, clipped to the 720 budget, so most full pages (the card is asked on every page of a full read) were cut by design and read again in two halves (two more requests, each resending the ~1,500-token prefix).
  2. **Quotes found 75.5 %** is mostly invented slots: `voice` ×21 and `dialogue` ×5 (v3's compact form moved voice out of every fact and listed its bullet right after the slot list, so the model wrote the field's name as a slot), plus singular/plural spellings (`trait`, `roles`, `detail:scar`) and a few others.
  3. **Verifier pass 79.9 %**: the code-gate refusals (subject, value, wish, kind) are **not** counted as checker failures: they are refused before the checker and never enter `verifySent`; only items the checker answered count. The 28 misses are 24 `unsupported` plus subject picks the checker made against the reader. No accounting fix was due; the lever is the reader (fewer unsupported proposals) and the new gates.
  4. **The negative and the six wrong facts** each passed the checker: a place described beside Johanna ("The road was hard with frost…"), another person's fact through "my father", a count of daughters as an age, an absence as a location, a death place as a location, "attentions" as an object, one claim in two slots.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **The cut-off metric tells the truth.** A reading's `finish_reason` is now `length` only when a cut was not recovered (a retry cut again or unreadable, nothing left to re-read, a checker batch cut twice); `reading.cut` still counts every cut reply. The report shows "Cut off at the cap and not recovered" (the old line's place) and, apart, "recovered by the re-read" with the count of cut replies; JSON adds `recoveredReadings` and `cutReplies`. Settings › AI › Story memory's "Readings cut off at their limit" now means the same unrecovered cut-offs. The knows report's row is relabelled the same way.
  2. **`reader.v4`** (new file, golden, catalogue, 9 eval cases incl. the second run's scenes): the slot list first under "SLOTS." as the only values of "s", naming voice, dialogue, speech, thought, memory, dream, secret, action, event, belief, emotion and appearance as never slots; voice, mode, sp and chg under "FIELDS, never slots"; what each value must be (an age is an age, an object a thing, a location a place the quote puts them at or going to and never where they died, an absence no fact, a relative's fact theirs only as kinship, relation or history, each fact in one slot). Rules 1,303 tokens (v3 1,151; cap 1,400). `verify.v3` is unchanged (the gates suffice; no `verify.v4`).
  3. **The reader's budget is raised from 720 to 1,000** (`src/shared/ai.ts`; the pin in `ai.test.ts` changed): v4 asks min(1,000, 160 + 1.4 × prose tokens + 240 with the card), 960 for a full page with its card. Justification: v3's formula needed 880 and got 720, so most pages were re-read in halves; max_tokens is not billed, only what is written, and a page written once costs less than a cut page plus two halves. v1, v2 and v3 keep their 720 (their token rows are unchanged).
  4. **Slot spelling read exactly** (`readSlot`): a slot's other number only ("trait"→traits, "roles"→role, "scar"/"detail:scar"→scars, "injury"→injuries, "eye"→eyes, "object"→objects, a detail label plus "s"). Voice, dialogue, memory, secret, speech, event, action, belief, breathing, return stay refused (their meaning differs).
  5. **Code gates before the checker** (`src/shared/memory/ladder.ts`, refusals counted by reason): `valueKind` (an age is a count with age words only, never "four grown-up daughters"; an object's head word is no feeling or attention), `absence` (a negated value outside traits, history, relations, alive, kinship and age), `possessed` (with every "my father", "his wife", "Mr. Harrow's daughters" taken out no pointer at the subject is left; not for kinship, relation or history, so "My father was a clergyman" stays Mr. Dunmore's kinship and "the Captain's father was a merchant" his history), `where` (a location's words stand in a sentence of the quote that names or points at the subject, after at/in/to/up/reached… or as a one-word place), `past` (location, clothing, objects or injuries from a sentence where the subject died), `duplicate` (the same record, value and paragraph in another slot: the second is refused).
  6. **Offline replay**: `memory.eval.ts` plants each of the second run's failures (the road, my father, the daughters, not there, the death place, attentions, the duplicate trait, a `voice` slot) behind the gullible checker and expects each refused with its reason, keeps "trait" read as traits and the relation that came first, and every honest label is still stored. A pipeline unit test does the same on a small scene.
  7. **Prices**: `reader.v4:first` (2,380 in / 650 out), `:window` and `:card` (4,550 / 400) in `READING_V3_ROWS` feed the rebuild and the backlog; the reader note's typical out is 500.
- Alternatives: asking the card only on a full read's first page (halves the card output, but Here would miss characters who appear later); smaller pages (more requests and more repeated prefix); a `verify.v4` for the six wrong facts (code is exact and costs nothing); counting post-verdict subject refusals outside the verifier bar (that would be loosening it).
- Not loosened: every bar and every label as they were.
- Owed by you: run it again: `MYTHSCRIBE_EVAL_LIVE=1 OPENROUTER_API_KEY=sk-or-… npm run eval:ai -- memory`; then live fidelity for `reader.v4`.
- To change it: `src/main/ai/prompts/reader.v4.ts`, `src/shared/ai.ts` (`reader`), `src/shared/memory/ladder.ts` (the gates), `src/shared/memory/slots.ts` (`OTHER_NUMBER`), `src/main/memory/reading/pipeline.ts` (`lost`), `src/main/ai/eval/plantedScore.ts` (`cutLines`).

## 2026-10-14 · F-16.17, F-16.16, F-16.3 · Live planted-error run of 2026-10-14: what failed and what changed
- Question: your live run (`test-results/eval-memory/planted-2026-10-14.md`) failed four bars. How should the reader pass them without loosening any bar?
- Root causes found:
  1. **Cut off at the cap (83 %).** `reader.v2` read whole scenes as one page of up to 2,500 prose tokens with 720 output tokens, every fact in its long form (about 50 tokens with `voice`, `mode`, `sp`, `o`, `chg` written out) and a card of about 230 tokens. The test book's 300–560-token scenes already asked more than 720.
  2. **Quotes found 76 %** was mostly not the truncation: 14 items used invented slots ("action", "emotion", "dialogue", bare "occupation"…), and 11 quoted more than 25 words; both count as quotes not found.
  3. **2 claims from never-read scenes** was a scorer bug, not the reader: h09 (read) and its earlier draft h10 (never read) share the sentence "Lise came back to Thornmere", and the scorer counted any quote standing in h10's text. Nothing from h10 was read.
  4. **6 negatives and the junk** came from facts the quote does not prove: a subject the quote does not name ("the sisters walked to Callow Cross" → Johanna; "talked of everything except Thornmere"; "How can you be so unfeeling" → Mrs. Harrow's accent; "Harrow's temper", the shared surname, which the checker settled on Mr. Harrow), a value not in the quote ("the road" from "Johanna walked quickly", "at the table"), a want read as a fact ("Johanna wanted thread", "I hope Mr. Ashby will like it" as an occupation), and a place given a person's slot (Thornmere's "home").
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **Code gates before the checker** (`src/shared/memory/ladder.ts`, `slots.ts`; refused facts counted by reason): the value's words must stand in the quote (`value`; alive and kinship exempt, an age in digits matches its words); the quote itself must name the subject, or say he, she, his, her, a description of one person ("a single man"), or a first person for the speaker, or (eyes and hair only) a they/it for the part (`subject`); a state read as now from a want, wish, hope or plan word is refused (`wish`; traits, history and relations exempt); a place takes only location, role, traits, history, relation and a few details (`kind`); a detail label written bare ("occupation") or a base slot behind the prefix ("detail:location") is read as its slot, anything else invented is still refused.
  2. **`reader.v3` and `verify.v3`** (new prompt files, goldens, eval cases incl. the live failures; token report: reader.v3 rules 1,151 tokens, +162 on v2; verify.v3 rules 547, +131): compact facts (defaults left out), the slot list with what each slot holds, the subject-in-the-quote and value-from-the-quote rules; the checker refuses one person for "they"/"the sisters", "you" as the speaker, a want as a thing, and a value of the wrong kind.
  3. **Pages of 400 prose tokens** (was 2,500) and `max_tokens` = min(720, 160 + 1.2 × prose tokens, + 240 with the card), so a page's facts and card fit the existing 720 cap. A reply still cut off is read again from its last quoted paragraph (its facts from the cut reply dropped, since that paragraph's list may stop midway), in two halves.
  4. **Prices follow** (`READING_V3_ROWS` in `src/shared/aiCostNotes.ts`): Rebuild memory and the backlog price per 400-token page (about 2,230 in / 600 out, verify 1,000 / 120), so a rebuild prices about 1.5–2× the old figure (still cents on DeepSeek V4 Flash).
  5. **The scorer**: a claim counts as the inactive draft's only when its quote stands in h10 and in no readable scene; the report now lists any stray claims. **One label added**: `johanna-muff` (h07, "with her hands in her muff", Johanna's clothing): it is true, the sentence names her, and the h07 negative is about the hypothetical "If Mr. Ashby came back tomorrow"; without the label any true state of Johanna's on that walk counted as a false alarm. Please confirm this one: it is the only label change.
  6. **Offline replay** (`memory.eval.ts`, "the live run's failures of 2026-10-14, planted"): each live failure planted into the fake reader behind a checker that supports everything; the code gates alone refuse each with its reason, no negative or stray is stored, and every honest label is still kept.
- Alternatives: raise the reader's output budget to about 1,300 and keep 800-token pages (fewer calls, less repeated prefix; blocked today because `src/shared/ai.test.ts` pins `reader: 720` and another session is editing that file); ask the card only on a full read's first page (cheaper, but the panel's Here would miss characters who appear later in a long scene).
- Not loosened: every pass bar is as it was. The rosters' format version is still `reader.v2` (its lines did not change), so nothing is rebuilt.
- Owed by you: run it again: `MYTHSCRIBE_EVAL_LIVE=1 OPENROUTER_API_KEY=sk-or-… npm run eval:ai -- memory` (report in `test-results/eval-memory/`); then live fidelity for `reader.v3` and `verify.v3` (`… npm run eval:ai -- prompts`).
- To change it: `src/main/ai/prompts/reader.v3.ts` (`READER_V3_PAGE_TOKENS`, the cap), `src/shared/memory/ladder.ts` (the gates), `src/main/memory/reading/pipeline.ts` (the cut retry), `src/shared/aiCostNotes.ts` (`READING_V3_ROWS`).
