## 2026-10-14 · F-16.31, F-16.3 · Switch-over slice 5 review: the merge list offers twins, not families; the replay checks your converted pages
- Question: The review of slice 5 found that the first dry run on a realistic book (the test book built on v1) offered 13 merges of which only one was right: Mr. and Mrs. Harrow, each daughter with Mr. Harrow, Ned and Celia Ashby, Mr. and Mrs. Lowther, Sir Walter, Lady and Clara Pell, Mr. Fenwick with Anne, and even the Bell (a place) with Bel Harrow and Pell House (a place) with Lady Pell, one wrong tick merging two people's names, pages and facts; that the replay's "your pages are byte-identical" check compared only records made in the new memory, so none of a switched book's; that the v1 test book held none of your sheet text but the Captain's Plan line, so the comparison with the v2-built book never covered your sheet text becoming canon Pinned lines, and the Filed-by-AI block was not checked Not canon; that the invariants after the rebuild left the findings out; that the AI rows the switch leaves in the old tables (the sheet the AI made, the old summaries) were not on the "never sent" list; that the live switch-over run stopped at the first rate limit, which the app's queue retries, and wrote no report when it stopped at its own time limit; and that `docs/OPERATOR-SETUP.md` did not list `-- switchover`.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **The merge list offers twins, not families (decision SW-D15).** (a) A nickname the conversion marks shared on each holder ("Harrow" on Mr. and Mrs. Harrow) is left out of the twin test; a record's own name stays in, since two records of one name are the likeliest twins. (b) A character and a place, or any two kinds, are never offered (this changes P1 slice 6's item 3, which offered the two Harrows, a character and a place). (c) Two records that each have a name part of their own, a word or a title the other's names lack, matched only by folding titles out or by a word subset (the surname they share), are left out: Mr. and Mrs. Harrow, Johanna Harrow and Mr. Harrow, Ned and Celia Ashby. Still offered: a name alone beside the full name ("Varrow" beside Callum Varrow), a misspelling ("Liesl" beside Liesel Fenwick), one name spelled twice ("Mrs Tull" and "Mrs. Tull"), a surname alone beside a title ("Brand" beside Captain Brand). A pair left out is not looked past (the name's first hit decides, as before), so the list errs toward fewer offers. **What it costs:** a real twin of the family shape is no longer offered, for example a "Mr. Varrow" tag beside a "Callum Varrow" sheet, or "Miss Liesel" beside "Liesel Harrow"; *Same as…* merges them after the switch. On the test book the list is now only the Varrow pair, which the replay pins exactly; `planSwitchover.test.ts` holds each rule. `sameAs` itself is unchanged: Convert merges only pairs the dry run offered, and your own *Same as…* may merge any two named records on purpose.
  2. **The pages the replay and the live run compare are every named record's Pinned and Ideas** (yours and the converted ones; never a record or secret the reading made) **and every Research page**, and the replay checks that Ned Ashby's Pinned, the Captain's Ideas, the Bell's and Anne Fenwick's Pinned and the Research page are among them, so the check cannot be empty again. Item 4 of "Switch-over part A built" said this; now the code does it.
  3. **The v1 test book carries your kind of sheet text:** the labels' Pinned pages and P4's notes as sheet text (the Bell, Anne Fenwick and her father), the Captain's and Lottie's Ideas lines as Plan sheets, and P4's Research page as v1's Project notes. The v2-built book it is compared with gets the same pages and notes (its Research page titled "Project notes", as the switch titles v1's). The claims and findings still match with the note rules in: 67 claims, 12 findings (9 before, without notes), notes-against-book findings (C6) among them. The replay checks each converted line canon, and Ned Ashby's AI-written personality under "Filed by AI earlier: please check", Not canon.
  4. **The invariants after the rebuild include the findings** (open findings judged both ways, none on the Dismissed list, every side a live claim), as the contradictions replays check them.
  5. **"Never sent" now includes the AI rows the switch leaves in the old tables until P9** (the sheet the AI made and the old scene summaries), in the replay and as the live run's `notCanonSent` bar.
  6. **The live run retries what the app retries** (this replaces item 6's "a 429 stops it" in "Switch-over part A built"): a rate limit, a network error or a provider's unreadable answer is the reader queue's to retry (after 5, 15 and 45 seconds) and is counted in the report; only an error that pauses the app's queue (no key, a bad key, no credit, over a cap) stops the run. If retries still leave scenes unread, the report fails with "only N of 18 scenes were read". The 50¢ stop is unchanged.
  7. **A report every time:** the rebuild stops at its own 25-minute limit with the queue's Stop and the report says so; the live test's limit is 40 minutes, above that and the switch before it; a run that fails before its rebuild (the conversion, the key) writes a short report naming the failure.
  8. **The final verification ran again on the reviewed code:** typecheck 0 errors, lint clean on the touched folders (src/shared/memory, src/main/ai/eval, src/main/memory/switchover, src/main/memory/testBook), `npm run test` 763 files and 9006 tests passed (the eval project and its token report included, unchanged: no prompt changed), `npm run test:e2e` 22 passed, `memory.spec.ts`'s two switch-over tests beside the unedited v1 smoke. `docs/OPERATOR-SETUP.md` lists `-- switchover` (six live runs now), its report path, and the `PLANTED_PASS_BARS` pointers in older entries name `plantedScore.ts`.
- Alternatives: showing the family pairs under a "probably different people" heading, never ticked (the dialog would need a second list, and the pairs are noise for most books); making `sameAs` refuse two kinds (your own *Same as…* allows it on purpose); keeping a shared nickname in the test; leaving the test book without your sheet text; stopping the live run at any provider error.
- To change it: `src/shared/memory/twinGate.ts` (`twinPairs`'s `allowed`, `eachHasOwnName`), `src/main/memory/switchover/model.ts` (the merge roster and `offered`), `src/main/memory/testBook/projectV1.ts` (`sheetTextOf`, `aiFrozen`), `src/main/ai/eval/switchover.eval.ts` (`authorPages`, `notCanonTexts`, `rebuildMemory`'s `limitMs`, `runLiveSwitchover`'s error sorting, `REBUILD_LIMIT_MS`), `docs/OPERATOR-SETUP.md`, `plans/Switchover.md` §5 "Reviewed" and SW-D15.

## 2026-10-14 · F-16.31, F-16.3, F-5.12 · Switch-over part A built before your run in the installed app
- Question: Part A of the switch-over (plan `docs/research/story-memory-2026-10-11/plans/Switchover.md`, five slices, each but the last reviewed) is built: any v1 project, yours in Google Drive included, can be switched from the installed app, from Help › *Switch to the new story memory…* or Settings › AI › Story memory, with *Try it on a copy first* recommended. Slice 5 adds the test book built the way a v1 author has it, an offline replay of its switch-over in `npm run test`, and the live switch-over run for you, then verifies the whole on the code: typecheck 0 errors, lint per folder clean (src/shared, src/preload, src/main, src/renderer, cloud, e2e, scripts), `npm run test` 763 files and 9001 tests passed (a first run had one timing miss in `findings.perf.test.ts`, 18.3 ms against a 16.7 ms bar, which passed alone and on the second full run), `npm run eval:ai` 125 passed (7 live skipped; token report unchanged, no prompt changed), `npm run test:e2e` 22 passed, `memory.spec.ts`'s two switch-over tests beside the unedited v1 smoke. Nothing is forced on open; new projects stay v1; every v1 path still works. F-16.31 is not ticked: it needs your run in the installed app, and part B. These are the calls slice 5 made:
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **The split pair the replay merges is Callum Varrow's** (his sheet, and a character tag `varrow` made for the surname alone; the twin gate offers "Merge Varrow into Callum Varrow", rule `subset`), not the plan's "the Captain as Harrow and Captain Harrow": the test book's Captain is never named Harrow, and "Harrow" is the Harrows' shared surname, so that pair could not leave the records equal to the labels. The tag's one inline token stands where "Varrow" is alone in the prose (3.1's "Come, Varrow").
  2. **Mrs. Harrow's v1 sheet has no tag.** v1 keeps a name on one tag only (F-4.14), so a surname two people share cannot be an alias of both tags; the book puts "Harrow" on Mr. Harrow's tag and on her sheet's own names, and the switch-over marks it shared on both, as the labels have it. In your project, a surname on one tag ends up on one record; the dry run lists each record's other names, and in the new memory you can give it to the other one too, marked shared.
  3. **The replay compares everything the memory holds, not only the labelled claims:** every claim and every finding of the switched book equals the same *Rebuild memory* on the v2-built book, ids named by what they stand for (67 claims, 9 findings; 12 findings since the slice 5 review, which added your sheet text and notes to the v1 book, see "Switch-over slice 5 review" item 3). It holds although the switch types the 1811 year only on the first scene of its run (one timeline event over s01–h07), where the v2 book types it on each scene: the year carries.
  4. ***Rebuild memory* runs through the reader service**, as the app runs it (the open, the waiting notice priced as the dry run said, `rebuild()`, the reading, the spot checks and the contradiction sweep on the reader's queue), answered by the oracle reader, checker and judge behind a fake OpenRouter; the author's pages (every record's Pinned and Ideas, every Research page) are compared byte for byte before and after *(true since the slice 5 review, item 2: before it only records you made in v2 were compared, so the converted ones were not)*.
  5. **The live run's bars** (`SWITCHOVER_PASS_BARS`, decision SW-D13): the reported cost within 0.5–1.5× of the price the dry run asked; the time reported against the dry run's minutes, with no bar; the planted-error run's bars (`-- memory`'s, now shared through `plantedScore.ts`) met on the converted book; the conversion's own checks clean (prose, snapshot, inline tags, records); no author page changed by the rebuild; no request carrying the sheet text the AI wrote or an Ideas line. It runs at the app's own pace (one request per 500 ms), so its time is the one you would see.
  6. **The 50¢ stop:** past the cap the run's requests are refused as the request path refuses one past the daily cap (`BUDGET`, nothing sent), the reader's queue pauses, and the run stops it as the app's Stop does; what was read stays and the report says it stopped. (Refusing with a cancel before the Stop left the job queued and ran it again at once, so nothing else could run.) A provider error (a 402, a 429) and a cost that cannot be known stop it the same way and are reported. *(Changed by the slice 5 review, item 6: a 429 or a network error is retried as the app's queue retries it; only an error that pauses the app's queue stops the run.)*
  7. **Offline, the fake OpenRouter reports the tokens each reply would take** (estimated as the app estimates them) and no cost, so the ledger prices them from the price table as a live run's would; the switched test book then costs about 0.75× the dry run's price (3.2¢ against 4.3¢), inside the bar. The real ratio is for your run to measure.
  8. **Shared code, no change in behaviour:** `project.ts`'s scene layout became `layOutTestBook` (both builders use it); the planted-error run's bar measures moved from `memory.eval.ts` to `plantedScore.ts` (its report reads the same). No migration, prompt, channel or app code changed; the token report and the cost registry are unchanged.
- **Owed by you:** in the installed app, the trial on a copy of your real Drive project (Help › *Switch to the new story memory…* › *Try it on a copy first*, then its *Rebuild memory* and the grouped summary); after the whole build, the live runs of CLAUDE.md 1c, now with the live switch-over run (`MYTHSCRIBE_EVAL_LIVE=1 OPENROUTER_API_KEY=sk-or-… npm run eval:ai -- switchover`; the `OPENAI_API_KEY` form works too; it stops at 50¢, about 3–5¢ expected; report in `test-results/eval-switchover/`); then the real switch of your project. **Part B** (deleting v1, about −25k lines; retiring `tags`, `summary`, `continuity`, `organise`, `todo`, `sheetSync`, `query`, `route`; the switch-over offered on open; new projects on v2; closing every feature marked "replaced by F-16") waits for your switch and your word that it works (the gate in `Switchover.md` §7), and gets its own plan.
- Alternatives: a Harrow/"Captain Harrow" pair with a label for it (a record the book never names); giving Mrs. Harrow the surname after the switch as an author step in the replay; comparing only the labelled claims and contradictions; driving the pipeline directly (`readBook`) instead of the reader service; a price bar on the replay's fake cost; running the live run with no pause between requests (faster, but not the time you would see).
- To change it: `src/main/memory/testBook/projectV1.ts` (1, 2), `src/main/ai/eval/switchover.eval.ts` (3–7; the bars in `SWITCHOVER_PASS_BARS`), `src/main/ai/eval/plantedScore.ts`, `src/main/memory/testBook/project.ts` (8), `FEATURES.md` F-16.31.

## 2026-10-14 · F-16.31, F-16.19 · Switch-over slice 4 review: one price rule on every way into Rebuild memory, and the same words everywhere
- Question: The review of slice 4 found that on MythScribe Cloud a rebuild with no price could still start from Needs a look's *Rebuild…* or Settings' *Rebuild memory…* (only the final screen held it back, and its line promised Needs a look would ask with the price); that with today's AI spending cap reached the final screen still offered *Rebuild now*, which then sat at "Reading 0 of N scenes" with no reason; that the rebuild dialog never said, on Cloud, that the balance pays and which caps apply; that the dry run priced the rebuild in dollars ("about $0.14") and the final screen in cents ("about 14¢"); that a test copy's ask did not say a second rebuild is needed when you switch your project; that *Later* and Escape stayed live while *Rebuild now* was starting, so a paid rebuild could run with its progress and *Stop* out of sight; and that a local model's notice read "about 0¢".
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **On Cloud, Rebuild memory starts only with its price, whichever button asks** (decision SW-D9 kept everywhere): main refuses the start ("MythScribe Cloud has no price for a model story memory uses (<models>), so Rebuild memory does not start: on Cloud it starts only with its price." and "Pick another model in Settings › AI, or reset the models to their defaults."), and the rebuild dialog shows that in place of a price, with *Close* and no *Rebuild*. Cloud prices every model it offers (its table, or the bundled one until it arrives), so a missing price means a model it does not offer; the final screen now says that, replacing "MythScribe Cloud has not sent its prices yet" (item 3 of "Switch-over slice 4"), and ends "Then This scene › Needs a look offers Rebuild memory with its price." An own key with a model missing from the price table still may start.
  2. **Today's AI spending cap reached stops the rebuild from starting**, as it already stops *Check the book*: main refuses with "Today’s AI spending cap is reached, so Rebuild memory does not start." and "Raise the daily cap in Settings or wait until tomorrow." (a local model is held only once the day is over the cap, as its requests are). The final screen, when the reading is paused at the cap, offers no *Rebuild now*: "Today’s AI spending cap is reached, so Rebuild memory waits and nothing more is spent today. When you raise the cap in Settings › AI, or tomorrow, This scene › Needs a look offers Rebuild memory with its price." with *Later*. A rebuild that reaches the cap part-way still pauses as before.
  3. **The rebuild dialog on Cloud** adds, under the price, "Paid from your MythScribe Cloud balance. It is not counted toward your monthly reading cap; today’s AI spending cap still applies, so a long book may finish tomorrow." (the final screen's words), however you reach it (Needs a look, Settings, the Book tab).
  4. **One wording, in cents, for what the rebuild costs and who pays**, shared by the dry run, the final screen and the rebuild dialog: the dry run now says "Rebuild memory: 3 scenes, about 3¢ on your own key, about a minute. It asks before it starts." (was "about $0.03 … about 1 minute").
  5. **A test copy's ask adds** "This reads the test copy only: when you switch your project, it needs its own Rebuild memory, at about the same price." (no price sentence on a local model), and there *Rebuild now* is a plain button; *Open my project* stays the screen's main one. This adds to item 5 of "Switch-over slice 4".
  6. **While Rebuild now is starting, Later, Close and Escape wait** for main's answer; and a rebuild main started opens its progress, *Stop* and summary even if the screen was somehow left meanwhile (it is running and spending).
  7. **A free rebuild's notice reads** "Rebuild memory has not run since the switch-over: it reads 4 scenes at no cost" (a local model, or a free one), never "about 0¢".
- Alternatives: refusing the unpriced Cloud start only in the rebuild dialog (main would still accept it from the draft-never-read notice); wording the cap case as "starts reading tomorrow" (nothing resumes the queue on its own at midnight); a third Cloud sentence just for the dialog; dollars everywhere; making *Later* the main button on a copy; carrying the estimate's source in the notice instead of reading a price of 0 as free.
- To change it: `src/shared/memory/switchover.ts` (`cloudUnpriced`, `cloudUnpricedRefusal`), `src/main/memory/reading/readerService.ts` (`rebuild`, `dayCapped`), `src/renderer/features/memory/memoryView.ts` (`rebuildCost`, `aboutMinutes`, `CLOUD_REBUILD_CAPS`, the `switchoverRebuild` notice line), `RebuildMemory.tsx` (`EstimateLines`), `src/renderer/features/switchover/rebuildAsk.ts` (`dailyCap`, `cloudUnpriced`, `testCopyLine`), `switchoverText.ts` (`rebuildLine`), `switchoverStore.ts` (`rebuildNow`), `SwitchoverDialog.tsx` (`busy`, `RebuildAskBlock`), `plans/Switchover.md` §4 "Reviewed".

## 2026-10-14 · F-16.31, F-16.19, F-16.29 · Switch-over slice 4 (the rebuild ask, the notice row and the record in Settings): calls made while building it
- Question: Slice 4 finishes the end of the switch-over in the renderer (plan `Switchover.md` §4, SW-D8 and SW-D9): the final screen asks once for *Rebuild memory* with its price, or says why it waits; Needs a look's notice offers it later; Settings › AI › Story memory keeps the switch-over's record for good. Building it needed these calls.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **The final screen decides from main's own reading status**, read after the project reopens on the new memory: Use AI off ("Use AI is off, so nothing is read and nothing is spent. When you turn it on in Settings › AI, This scene › Needs a look offers Rebuild memory with its price."), the reading switch off (the same, "Story memory reading is off…"), no provider ("Story memory needs an AI provider: add a key or sign in to MythScribe Cloud in Settings › AI. Needs a look then offers Rebuild memory with its price."); only when reading may run is the price asked for (`memory:rebuildEstimate`, nothing sent). No Rebuild button in those cases, and the way out is *Close*.
  2. **The priced ask:** "Rebuild memory now: the AI reads N scenes once, about X¢ on your own key (about M minutes). Your text, Pinned, Ideas and Dismissed stay as they are." On Cloud: "…about X¢ from your MythScribe Cloud balance (about M minutes). It is not counted toward your monthly reading cap; today’s AI spending cap still applies, so a long book may finish tomorrow. …" On a local model: "…free on your local model…". Buttons *Rebuild now* (in the ask) and *Later* (where *Close* was). The screen's focus is on *Later* (or *Close*), so Enter never spends.
  3. **Cloud without its prices yet** gets no Rebuild button: "MythScribe Cloud has not sent its prices yet. Needs a look will offer Rebuild memory with its price." and *Later*. **An own key with a model missing from the price table** says "a cost not yet known (a model is not in the price table)" and may start (P2a's rule for your own key).
  4. **Rebuild now** starts the rebuild (`memory:rebuild`, priced again by main as it starts), closes the switch-over dialog and opens the *Rebuild memory* dialog on its progress, *Stop* and then the grouped summary. **A refused start** (the health gate, the key gone) shows main's message and next step under the line, keeps *Rebuild now* and *Later*, and leaves the screen open.
  5. **A test copy's final screen asks too** (beside *Open my project*), so the trial on a copy can run its rebuild and grouped summary before you switch your project. A book with nothing to read says "Rebuild memory: nothing to read yet. …" with no button; a price that cannot be read says why and that Needs a look offers it.
  6. **The notice keeps slice 2's wording** ("Rebuild memory has not run since the switch-over: it reads N scenes, about X¢"), not the earlier "Your book has not been read since the switch-over…", because a scene you write in and leave is read meanwhile. *Rebuild…* opens the rebuild dialog at its price (nothing sent until you press *Rebuild*); *Later* hides it until the project opens again.
  7. **Settings › AI › Story memory shows the record first:** "Switched to the new story memory on 14 October 2026.", "Backup: <zip>", "Dated copy: <folder>" and the final screen's way back; while no rebuild has started, "Rebuild memory has not run since the switch." with *Rebuild memory…* (Settings closes, the rebuild dialog asks with its price); afterwards "Rebuild memory first ran after the switch on <date>." A test copy's says "This is a test copy of <your project's folder>, switched to the new story memory on <date>. The project it was made from still uses the old one." To name that folder, the record now keeps it (`source`, written at Convert from the copy's marker; a record written before today reads none). Nothing shows for a project made new-memory another way.
- Alternatives: reading the key and Cloud sign-in in the renderer instead of main's status; one Close button with *Rebuild now* beside it; no ask on a test copy; the plan's "Your book has not been read…" wording for the notice; reading the copy's source from its marker in a v2 channel instead of the record.
- To change it: `src/renderer/features/switchover/rebuildAsk.ts` (the words and the rules), `switchoverStore.ts` (`loadAsk`, `rebuildNow`), `SwitchoverDialog.tsx` (`RebuildAskBlock`, the footer), `src/renderer/features/memory/MemorySettings.tsx` (`SwitchoverRecordBlock`), `src/shared/memory/switchover.ts` (`SwitchoverRecord.source`), `src/main/memory/switchover/run.ts`, `convert.ts`, `plans/Switchover.md` §4.

## 2026-10-14 · F-16.31, F-16.15 · Switch-over slice 3 review: nothing writes behind the dry run, and leaving a test copy takes you home
- Question: The review of slice 3 found that, with Use AI on (your setting), the old memory's summaries, To do list, voice, plan links and sheet sync still wrote the project while you read the dry run, so Convert made a second kept backup and a second dated copy in Google Drive after all (item 8 of "Switch-over slice 3" fixed this only with AI off); that leaving a test copy the flow had made left you working in the copy, with no way back offered, and the next launch opened the copy; that Convert sent the id of whatever project was open, not the one the flow was for; that the intro said what you dismissed stays (old dismissals go) and did not say sheets only the AI wrote go; that a test copy's intro promised a backup and a dated copy it does not get; that the way back named a file Settings › Backups never shows; and that the consent screen had no Cancel. Fixing them needed these calls.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **The old memory's background work waits while the dialog is open:** from the safety step until Convert or until you leave the dialog, no summary, To do sync, voice update, plan-link run or sheet sync starts (what they would do waits and runs when the dialog closes). The safety step first lets a run already under way finish, at most 30 seconds, so its result is in both copies. The tag mention scan keeps running: it writes only the app's own indexes. So Convert reuses the copies made before the dry run unless something you did changed the project. This corrects item 8 of "Switch-over slice 3" and replaces item 3 of "Switch-over slice 1 review" (background work no longer counts as a change, because none runs).
  2. **Leaving a test copy the flow made, before its end, opens your project again** (Cancel or Escape on the dry run or consent screen, or on the intro after the copy's dry run failed), with "Your project is open again and unchanged. The test copy was not switched. It stays at <copy>." **Leaving a test copy you opened yourself** keeps it open and says "You are working in the test copy. It stays at <copy>. Your project at <path> is unchanged." with *Open my project*. The copy's final screen keeps its own *Open my project* and *Close*. This replaces item 6 of "Switch-over slice 3" for copies and settles what item 7 of "Switch-over slice 1 review" left to this slice.
  3. **Convert names the project the flow started for** (the test copy once it is made), so if another project was opened under the dialog (Settings › Backups › Restore, say) Convert is refused as "The open project changed during the switch-over. Nothing was changed." The dialog does not close by itself when the project changes.
  4. **The consent screen has Cancel** beside Back and Convert (greyed while converting); after the safety step it shows the same "Nothing was changed…" toast.
  5. **The intro's list, worded as the dry run counts:** "…story years and what you dismissed that still applies."; "Sheet text the AI added to your sheets goes under “Filed by AI earlier: please check”, marked Not canon."; "What the AI made goes: summaries, AI facts and tags, sheets only the AI wrote, Changes, To do, consistency findings and old dismissals nothing reads any more. …". On a test copy its last line is "Snapshots are not changed. It runs once on this copy; the project it was made from stays as it is." (no backup or dated copy promised).
  6. **The way back names the backup as the list shows it:** "To go back: in Settings › Backups choose Restore on the backup from <date and time> marked (kept: before story memory). …" (the full path stays on the Backup line above). This replaces the go-back sentence of item 7 of "Switch-over slice 3".
- Alternatives: removing the superseded backup and dated copy at Convert instead of holding the jobs; cancelling a run in flight instead of waiting for it; closing the dialog when the project changes; reopening your project also when you leave a copy you opened yourself; keeping the copy open with only a toast after a copy the flow made.
- To change it: `src/main/memory/switchover/run.ts` (`SwitchoverRun.safety`, `leave`, `SWITCHOVER_HOLD_WAIT_MS`), `src/main/jobs/indexQueue.ts` and `src/main/ai/sheetSyncService.ts` (`hold`, `release`), `src/main/ipc/handlers.ts` (`v1Writers`, `switchover:leave`), `src/renderer/features/switchover/` (`switchoverStore.ts` `close`/`convert`/`projectId`/`madeCopy`, `SwitchoverDialog.tsx`, `switchoverText.ts` `whatChanges`/`goBackLine`), `plans/Switchover.md` §3 "Reviewed".

## 2026-10-14 · F-16.31, F-16.15 · Switch-over slice 3 (the entry points and the in-place flow, renderer): calls made while building it
- Question: Slice 3 gives every build the way in and the flow on the project itself (plan `Switchover.md` §3, SW-D1): Help › *Switch to the new story memory…* and Settings › AI › Story memory, an intro that says what changes and recommends a copy first, the safety step's progress and failure block, the dry run and consent worded for your own project, and the final screen with the backup, the dated copy and how to go back. Building it needed these calls. Nothing is offered or forced on open, new projects stay on the old story memory, and the old story memory is unchanged until you press Convert. No live run is involved (no key here).
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **The Help item is absent, not greyed, on the welcome screen** (it is about the open project's story memory), and absent for a project already on the new one. It sits after *Keyboard shortcuts*. The developer-only item and the build check are gone.
  2. **The Settings block** sits where a v2 project has its Story memory settings, above *Summarize all scenes*: "This project uses the old story memory: tags and the story bible. The new one reads your book and keeps your names, notes and labels." and *Switch to the new story memory…*, which closes Settings and opens the intro.
  3. **The intro knows a test copy** through a new read-only channel, `switchover:trial` (the copy's marker or nothing), so opened in a copy it says "This is a test copy." and offers only *Switch this copy…*, and the copy's final screen names the project it was made from even after you closed the dialog in between.
  4. **A failed step stays where it failed:** the safety step's block on the intro, the conversion's on the consent screen, each with *Try again* (the same step again). A failure saying the open project changed has no *Try again*: its next step is to start over from Help. Its message is shown without the "Nothing was changed…" sentence it ends with, which the block shows on its own line.
  5. **While the copies are made the line says where:** "Making a backup and a dated copy in Google Drive…" (the cloud folder's name from the status bar) or "…beside the project".
  6. **Leaving after the safety step** (Cancel on the dry run or consent screen) toasts "Nothing was changed. The backup and the dated copy made a moment ago stay where they are: <backup> · <dated copy>". Leaving earlier, or a copy, says nothing.
  7. **The final screen:** "Your project now uses the new story memory: N records, N labels, N Research documents, N inline tags as words."; "Backup: <zip> (kept until you delete it)" with *Show backups* (the backup folder); "Dated copy (in Google Drive): <folder>"; "To go back: in Settings › Backups choose Restore and pick “<zip name>”. It opens as a new project beside this one, on the old story memory. You can also open the dated copy with File › Open project…. This project stays on the new story memory." A test copy's says "This test copy now uses the new story memory…" and "Your project at <path> is unchanged and still uses the old one." with *Open my project* and *Close*. A reopen that fails says "Your project is switched, but it did not open again: … Open it from File › Open project…." Slice 4 adds the rebuild ask under these lines.
  8. **A write only to the app's own indexes is not a change the copies must follow.** The e2e showed that a tag mention scan landing a second after the last save made Convert copy the project to Google Drive a second time ("… 2.mythscribe") and keep a second backup. The copies now count as current while everything but the search indexes, the mention scans, the job queue and the AI cache reads the same (a digest of the rest, taken only when SQLite's change count has moved). Any other write still makes both again. This narrows item 1 of "Switch-over slice 1 review" ("nothing written since").
- Alternatives: the Help item greyed on the welcome screen like the other project items; the copy detected from the folder name instead of its marker; *Try again* everywhere; no toast on Cancel; waiting for the mention scans before the copies (it still copies twice when a job lands later); removing the earlier pair at Convert.
- To change it: `src/shared/menu.ts` (`switchStoryMemory`, `openProjectOnly`), `src/renderer/features/switchover/` (`switchoverStore.ts`, `SwitchoverDialog.tsx`, `SwitchoverFailure.tsx`, `SwitchoverSettings.tsx`, `switchoverText.ts`), `src/main/memory/switchover/run.ts` (`projectDigest`, `stillCurrent`), `plans/Switchover.md` §3.

## 2026-10-14 · F-16.31, F-16.19 · Switch-over slice 2 review: a scene you only look at waits, and the notice follows your key and license
- Question: The review of slice 2 found that, while the switch-over waits for its rebuild, every scene you opened and then left was read in full, even one you only opened to check the converted text (the likeliest thing to do right after switching), and the rebuild then read it again, so it was paid twice outside the one priced ask; that clearing your key, choosing a provider without a key, buying a license after the trial or the trial ending did not show or hide the notice until some later change, so its *Rebuild…* could fail for want of a key; that the notice said "Your book has not been read since the switch-over: N scenes" when N is what the rebuild reads, not what is unread; and that item 7 of "Switch-over slice 2" claimed more than its test showed. Fixing them needed these calls.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **While the switch-over waits, a scene is read when you leave it or pause only if you have written in it since the project opened** (saved from the editor). A scene you only opened to look at waits for the rebuild. This replaces item 6's "reading the scene you leave or pause in" of "Switch-over slice 2". Written in before you closed the app and not left before it closed: the rebuild reads it. Once any rebuild has started, leaving a scene reads it as before.
  2. **The notice follows the provider at once:** a key saved or cleared, a provider chosen (with a key or without), a license bought after the trial, or the trial ending each look again, so the notice shows or goes straight away (and a reading paused for want of a provider resumes when one comes back).
  3. **The notice says what the rebuild does:** "Rebuild memory has not run since the switch-over: it reads N scenes, about X¢" ("a cost not yet known" when a model's price is missing). This replaces item 8's line of "Switch-over slice 2" and the words of SW-D8's notice.
  4. **The daily cap, said exactly:** a rebuild paused at today's AI spending cap stays paused while the app stays open, until you resume it (the activity bar's Resume, a settings change, or *Rebuild memory* again); the next time the project opens it carries on by itself, and pauses again if today's cap is still reached. Tests pin the pause, the resume and the next open. This replaces item 7 of "Switch-over slice 2", which said it never resumes by itself.
- Alternatives: reading a left scene only when its text changed since the switch (the conversion stamps every converted scene as changed, so this would have needed a fingerprint of every scene stored at the switch); a count of the scenes still unread in the notice ("N of M not read yet"; one more walk of the book on every look); keeping the daily-cap pause across a reopen (a stored pause no other paused rebuild has).
- To change it: `src/main/memory/reading/readerService.ts` (`settle`, `editorSaved`, `editedHere`), `src/main/ipc/handlers.ts` (`documentsWritten`, `ai:clearKey`, `ai:setOwnKeyProvider`, the access subscription), `src/main/account/appAccess.ts` (`subscribe`), `src/renderer/features/memory/memoryView.ts` (the line), `plans/Switchover.md` §2.

## 2026-10-14 · F-16.31, F-16.19 · Switch-over slice 2 (the rebuild after the switch, main side): calls made while building it
- Question: After the switch-over, *Rebuild memory* asks once with its price, and until it starts the book is not read in the background; Needs a look offers it instead (plan `Switchover.md` §2, decision SW-D8). Building the main side needed small calls the plan left open. Nothing new is visible yet in a normal project: the notice appears only in a project switched over (today only a test copy in a development build); slice 3 gives every build the entry point and slice 4 the final screen's ask. No live run is involved (no key here).
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **A book with nothing to read yet gets no notice.** The hold stays (nothing unasked is read); the notice appears once a scene has text, at the next open, write outside the editor or settings change.
  2. **The notice shows only while reading can run** (Use AI on, the reading switch on, a provider). Turning any of them on raises it at once rather than at the next open, and a change of provider or model prices it again. For a project without the record, a settings change does exactly what it did before.
  3. **Its price is the price at the last look** (the open, a write outside the editor, a settings change), not live; *Rebuild…* opens the rebuild dialog, which prices it again when you confirm (P2a's dialog).
  4. ***Later* and closing it do the same:** hidden until the project opens again. The hold stays meanwhile; the Book tab's *Rebuild memory* still ends it.
  5. **Any rebuild that starts ends the wait,** whichever button started it, even one you stop part-way. After that the book is read as any project is: up to 5 changed scenes without asking, more asked about with their price. A rebuild refused before it starts (no provider, Use AI off, the health gate pausing the model) leaves it waiting.
  6. **What the hold does not stop:** reading the scene you leave or pause in, *Read now*, the back-fill of a late name (it only rereads scenes already read) and the contradiction judge (it only judges what was read).
  7. **The daily cap:** a rebuild that reaches today's AI spending cap pauses with its progress shown and stays paused until you resume it (the activity bar's Resume, a settings change, or *Rebuild memory* again); it does not resume by itself the next day. That is how any paused rebuild behaves today; a test now pins it down.
  8. **The panel's row exists already,** so the code compiles: the line "Your book has not been read since the switch-over: N scenes, about X¢" ("a cost not yet known" when a model's price is missing) with *Rebuild…* and *Later*. Slice 4 adds its tests and the final screen's ask.
- Alternatives: a notice for an empty book ("nothing to read yet"); a live price on every look (it walks the whole book, so it would cost time on every reading); *Later* hiding it for good (the record then never shows it again until a rebuild); resuming a rebuild paused by the daily cap automatically the next day.
- To change it: `src/main/memory/reading/readerService.ts` (`checkBacklog`, `computeNotices`, `settingsChanged`, `answerNotice`, `rebuild`), `src/shared/memory/views.ts` (the notice), `src/renderer/features/memory/memoryView.ts` (its words), `plans/Switchover.md` §2.

## 2026-10-14 · F-16.31, F-16.15 · Switch-over slice 1 review: the copies always match the project, and retries make no pile
- Question: The review of slice 1 found that the dated copy could be older than the project it was named as the way back to (the safety step's record of what it made lasted while the project stayed open, so leaving the flow, writing on, and switching later kept the first dated copy); that a record of a project that had closed could come back after a reopen; that each *Try again* after a failed dated copy made another kept backup that is never deleted; that a write landing while the originals were read was in neither copy; that a test copy you had written in was converted with no backup; and that the conversion's own check computed the expected text with the very rewrite it was checking. Fixing them needed these calls.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **What the safety step made is reused only while it is still the project as it is:** the same open session (a project closed and opened again, even the same folder, starts fresh), nothing written since, and the backup and dated copy still where they were. Otherwise the copy to Google Drive, the backup and the dated copy all run again. This replaces item 7 of "Switch-over slice 1".
  2. **At Convert, a project written since the safety step gets a new backup and a new dated copy** (the slice made only a new backup), so the record, the last screen and Settings name copies that hold every word you wrote. The earlier pair stays where it is; nothing the switch-over made is deleted. This changes SW-D3's "the dated copy is made once".
  3. **Old-memory background work counts as a change:** while the dialog is open, the old memory's index queue (and, with AI on, its summaries) may still write, so Convert can make a second backup and dated copy even when you typed nothing. That is the price of "always exactly the project before the conversion".
  4. **A failed dated copy keeps the backup:** *Try again* makes only the dated copy, so a full or offline Drive does not leave a new kept zip per attempt. The message still says "The backup made a moment ago is kept: <file>."
  5. **A conversion that fails and is tried again copies nothing again:** it undid everything, so the project reads as it did when the copies were made.
  6. **Nothing can write between the last copy and the conversion:** Convert reads the uploads' originals first, then checks the project, then makes the copies current and converts, all in one go.
  7. **A test copy you have written in gets a kept backup** of the copy (no dated copy beside it) before it converts: the copy remembers a fingerprint of its prose when it is made, and any difference, or a copy made before today, counts as written in. This changes SW-D4 for that case only. Offering to reopen the original when you close the dialog in a test copy is left to slice 3's dialog.
  8. **The conversion's check reads the expected text from your original documents,** with each inline tag read as the words you chose (one shared rule), instead of from the rewrite it is checking; it also checks that every record's Pinned and Ideas page opens.
- Alternatives: keeping the first dated copy and marking it "made before later changes" on the last screen, offering only the backup as the way back; deleting the superseded copies the same run made; pausing the old memory's background work while the dialog is open; always backing up a test copy; a content fingerprint instead of SQLite's change count for the real project (fewer copies, but a write the fingerprint misses would be lost).
- To change it: `src/main/memory/switchover/run.ts` (1–7), `trial.ts` and `SwitchoverTrial` in `src/shared/memory/switchover.ts` (7), `checkConverted.ts` and `inlineTagWords` in `src/shared/memory/inlineTagReplace.ts` (8), `plans/Switchover.md` §1.2 and "Reviewed".

## 2026-10-14 · F-16.31, F-8.4 · Switch-over slice 1 (the in-place run, main side): calls made while building it
- Question: Building the switch-over's run on the project itself (plan `Switchover.md` §1: the safety step, the kept backup, step-labelled failures, the switch-over record, the conversion's own check) needed small calls the plan left open. Nothing of it is visible yet except the kept backup's row in Settings › Backups; the entry points and screens are slices 3 and 4. No live run is involved (no key here); the switch-over itself calls no model.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **A failed step after the safety step keeps the dated copy and the backup** made before the dry run (no second dated copy is made on *Try again*); only a failed dated copy leaves none (the half-made folder is removed), and a failed backup leaves no dated copy because it never starts.
  2. **Convert on a project already switched** answers the `project` step with "This project already uses the new story memory; the switch-over runs once. Nothing was changed." rather than the usual "…still uses the old story memory", which would be untrue there.
  3. **The conversion's own check looks everywhere:** every node's content and notes (folders and the section roots too) and every other draft, wider than the places the conversion rewrites. The app cannot put an inline tag in a folder's text or a root's notes, so this only refuses a project damaged some other way, and it names the place.
  4. **The check's message names up to three problems** ("its own check found that “Scene 1” would not read as it did; …", then "and N more"), after "The conversion stopped and undid everything:".
  5. **A cause is shown once:** a Google Drive or dated-copy error already starting "Could not …:" is shown from its cause, so the message reads "Could not copy the project to Google Drive first: EACCES…." and not the same words twice.
  6. **Without the safety step,** Convert on the project itself is refused outright ("Make the backup and the dated copy first."); the dialog never allows it. The safety step itself refuses a test copy ("A test copy needs no backup of its own…") and a project already switched.
  7. *(Replaced by item 1 of "Switch-over slice 1 review".)* **The safety step's record of what it made** lasts while the project stays open: a second safety call answers the same backup and dated copy, a project switch forgets it, and a failed Convert keeps it (updated when Convert had to back up again), so *Try again* never makes another dated copy.
  8. **A kept backup is recognised by its name** ("… before story memory <date time>.zip"). A project whose own name ends in "before story memory" would have its ordinary backups kept for ever too: retention can only keep too much, never delete a kept one.
  9. **The dry run counts each scene as it will read once converted** (inline tags as their words), so its rebuild price equals *Rebuild memory*'s on the converted book (tested with and without the contradiction sweep).
  10. **Until slice 3,** the developer copy flow shows a failed step as its error line ("<what failed> Next: <next step>"), its consent screen says only the test copy changes (a test copy has no backup or dated copy of its own now), and its done screen shows the backup and dated copy line only when there are some.
  11. **The check stays inside the transaction:** a 200-scene book with a second draft and a snapshot converts and checks itself in about 0.7 s (the bar is 5 s), so nothing moves out of it.
- Alternatives: removing the dated copy when Convert fails (then *Try again* needs a new safety step); the check limited to the places the conversion writes; a separate "already switched" step; marking kept backups in a side file rather than by name.
- To change it: `src/main/memory/switchover/run.ts`, `checkConverted.ts`, `src/shared/backups.ts`, `plans/Switchover.md` §1.

## 2026-10-14 · F-16.31 · Switch-over plan (part A): the calls the design left open
- Question: Planning part A of the switch-over, your switch of the real project from the installed app (`docs/research/story-memory-2026-10-11/plans/Switchover.md`, five slices, nothing built yet), needed calls the design and F-16.15's copy flow did not make. Details are in the plan's §8 (SW-D1 to SW-D14).
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **Where you start it:** Help › *Switch to the new story memory…* and a button in Settings › AI › Story memory, in every build (installed too), while a project on the old memory is open. It is never offered or forced when a project opens, and new projects stay on the old memory until part B. The developer-only *Try … on a copy…* item goes.
  2. **The first screen** says plainly what changes (tags become names and labels, inline tags become words you choose, everything you wrote stays, the AI's sheet text goes under "Filed by AI earlier: please check", what the AI made goes and is rebuilt with its price shown first, snapshots untouched, a backup and a dated copy first, it runs once) and recommends *Try it on a copy first*, with *Switch this project…* beside it.
  3. **Order on your project, as the design lists it:** copy to Google Drive, the backup and the dated copy first; then the dry run, the consent screen and Convert. If anything in the project changes before you press Convert, it is copied to Drive and backed up again then, so the backup is always the project exactly as it was just before the conversion. (The copy flow built in P1 made its copies at Convert.)
  4. **The backup is kept:** a full zip named "<Name> before story memory <date time>.zip" in the project's backups folder, which the automatic clean-up never deletes and which does not count toward "keep 10"; Settings › Backups shows it as kept and can restore it. The dated copy, "<Name> (before story memory <date time>)", sits beside the project in Google Drive.
  5. **A test copy gets no backup or dated copy of its own:** your original is never written (a test proves its bytes unchanged), so it is the copy's safety, and Drive does not fill with copies of copies.
  6. **The conversion checks itself before it saves:** no inline tag left, snapshots byte for byte the same, every scene's text the same once your chosen words are in, no two records claiming one name, every document readable. Any problem undoes everything and says what it found. Convert also names the project it is for, so a dialog left open can never convert another book.
  7. **If anything fails,** the screen says what failed (copying to Google Drive, the backup, the dated copy, the project, the conversion), that nothing was changed and the project still uses the old memory, and what to do next, with *Try again*.
  8. **The project reopens on the new memory without a restart** (main closes it, Google Drive gets the converted file, the window opens it again).
  9. **The last screen** names the backup (with *Show backups*), the dated copy, and how to go back: restore the backup in Settings › Backups (it opens as a new project beside this one, on the old memory) or open the dated copy. Settings › AI › Story memory keeps saying this afterwards, from a small record the conversion stores in the project.
  10. ***Rebuild memory* asks once:** the last screen shows its price and time (*Rebuild now* / *Later*). Until a rebuild starts, the rest of the book is not read in the background; Needs a look shows one notice, "Your book has not been read since the switch-over", with *Rebuild…* (priced again when you press it) and *Later* (until the project opens again). The scene you are writing is still read when you leave it or pause. The dry run's price includes the contradiction sweep, so it matches the rebuild's own price.
  11. **Use AI off, no AI provider, or MythScribe Cloud without its prices yet:** no Rebuild button; the screen says why and that Needs a look will offer it with its price. On Cloud the ask says it comes from your balance, is not counted toward the monthly reading cap, and that today's spending cap still applies (a long book may finish the next day).
  12. **The live test** (needs your key, after the build): `MYTHSCRIBE_EVAL_LIVE=1 OPENROUTER_API_KEY=sk-or-… npm run eval:ai -- switchover` switches the test book from an old-memory version of it and rebuilds it live (stops at 50¢, about 3–5¢ expected); the bars: the real cost between half and one and a half times the price the app asked, the planted-error run's memory bars on the switched book, and your pages untouched.
  13. **Part B** (deleting the old code, retiring the old feature ids, the switch on open, new projects on the new memory, closing the replaced features) starts only after the live runs and your switch of the real project, when you say so, with its own plan.
- Alternatives: the safety step at Convert, as in P1's copy flow (the backup then always exact, but you read the dry run before any copy exists); the F-8.7 database-only backup (cannot be restored from Settings); a backup and dated copy for test copies too; reading the backlog in the background after *Later*; a Rebuild button with Use AI off that turns it on; keeping the menu item in development builds only until part B.
- To change it: `plans/Switchover.md` §8, before the slice that builds it.

## 2026-10-14 · F-16.11, F-16.23, F-16.30 · Flashback switch review (2026-10-14)
- Question: The review of the Flashback switch found that *Undo* after *Not a flashback?* could overwrite a later change made in the scene details; that *Not a flashback?* could set the switch on the wrong scene (the first flashback side, not the one whose flashback hides the pair); that the switch-over wrote v1's computed reading-order flag into your switch; that "(you set 1.3 as a flashback)" and "a scene looks like a flashback" were not always true; and that some paths had no test (loose ends' deaths, the report's `typed` flag, *Not right* on a card's flashback, a stale card flashback in the details, a save that leaves the switch out). Fixing them needed these calls.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **Undo reverses only its own change:** it puts the switch back only while it still says Not a flashback; if you changed it in the scene details meanwhile, nothing is sent and a note says "1.3’s Flashback switch has changed since, so Undo left it as it is." (`sceneDetailStore.setFlashback`'s `expected`.)
  2. **The scene that hides the pair, by its rule:** one table says which side's flashback hides each rule's pair (C2 dead: the later presence; C2 body: either; C3: the learning line; C4 birth: the stated age; C4 falls: the later age; C5: the advance), and both the rules and *Not a flashback?* read it (`flashbackSides`, `hidingScenes` in `src/shared/memory/findings.ts`). When both sides of a body change are flashbacks, *Check the book* lists the pair once per scene, each with its own *Not a flashback?* (the heading still counts the pair once). `FindingView.actions.notFlashback` became a list of scenes.
  3. **The switch-over sets no switch:** v1's reading-order view flagged a scene whose event comes before one read earlier; that was computed, not something you set, and two storylines that interleave on the timeline would have been switched to Flashback, hiding findings and ignoring the reader's checked card. Years stay on the first scene of each run, as before the switch; the plan's notes name the scenes v1 flagged ("v1’s Timeline showed 4.1 'The flood' as set earlier than a scene before it. The Flashback switch is left to follow the book: turn it on in a scene’s details where it is a flashback, so its story year does not carry into the scenes after it."). Not done: typing the year before a flashback run onto the scene after it (the review's other option), since that writes a guessed year into your typed Story year, which C4's age check treats as yours.
  4. **Words true whoever set it:** the report says "(1.3 is set as a flashback in its scene details)" when the switch is the cause ("(1.3 looks like a flashback)" when the card is), and the dialog says "set aside because a scene is a flashback (set in its scene details, or read so by the AI)". The comments that named only the card now name `sceneFlashback` (no prompt text changed; `judge.v1.ts` only in a doc comment).
  5. **Tests added:** loose ends' deaths follow the switch both ways with no card; the report's `typed` flag from the main side, one line per hiding scene; *Not right* on a card's flashback still unhides a pair (the switch unset); a stale card flashback counts for nothing in the details; `sceneDetail:set` refuses a save without the switch and keeps false as false.
- Alternatives: an origin column on the switch (who set it) to keep "you set"; listing v1's flagged scenes for a per-scene yes/no in the switch-over dialog; a single "Not a flashback?" that sets every hiding scene at once.
- To change it: `src/renderer/features/editor/sceneDetailStore.ts`, `src/renderer/features/memory/checkBook/CheckReport.tsx` (1, 2, 4); `src/shared/memory/findings.ts` (`flashbackSides`), `src/shared/memory/candidates.ts`, `src/main/memory/findings/findingViews.ts`, `checkBook.ts` (2); `src/main/memory/switchover/model.ts`, `convert.ts` (3); `CheckBook.tsx` (4).

## 2026-10-14 · F-16.11, F-16.23 · Flashback switch (2026-10-14)
- Question: You chose "Yes, add the switch" for P2b slice 5 review item 10: a Flashback switch in the v2 scene details beside Story year, your setting winning over the AI's reading in both directions, everywhere. Building it needed these calls.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **Data:** an additive migration, `0040_scene_flashback`: `scene_detail.flashback`, null (follow the book, every older row), true (a flashback) or false (not one). The details' schema requires it, so a save that leaves it out is refused instead of quietly unsetting it.
  2. **One rule everywhere** (`sceneFlashback`): your switch when set; else the card's flashback only when the checker confirmed it and its sentence still stands (as before); else not a flashback. The story years and ages, the Timeline, the sheets, Ms Scribe's computed years, the contradiction rules (what they hide), the judge's "flashback" mark, *Check the book*'s hidden list and loose ends all read it. With the switch on, a year typed or quoted there stays that scene's, ages there are its own, and the findings that need it not to be a flashback are hidden, with no card and with Use AI off. Set to Not a flashback, a card's flashback is ignored.
  3. **Where it shows:** beside Story year in a scene's details only (a manuscript document); chapters and parts keep Story year without it, since only scenes are read in story order.
  4. **What it shows:** the switch shows what counts. Unset with a card that reads a flashback, it is on with "AI read: flashback" dimmed under it. Once you set it, *Follow the book* appears (unsets it), and where the card reads otherwise its reading is shown dimmed ("AI read: flashback" or "AI read: not a flashback"). A disagreement is never asked about in Needs a look: your setting wins.
  5. ***Check the book*'s *Not a flashback?*** now sets the scene's switch to Not a flashback (the scene's other details kept), with *Undo* in the toast putting the switch back as it was, instead of marking the card's flashback not right. One place holds your answer, and it also works when your own switch is what hid the pair. The report says "(you set 1.3 as a flashback)" when the switch is the cause, "(1.3 looks like a flashback)" when the card is. (Changed in the review, 2026-10-14: "(1.3 is set as a flashback in its scene details)"; *Undo* leaves a switch changed since as it is; the switch set is the scene that hides the pair by its rule. See "Flashback switch review (2026-10-14)".) *Not right* on the card's flashback (its ⓘ) is still there. P4's tests keep their intent: the hidden pair is listed, *Not a flashback?* unhides it, and it is judged next (the contradictions replay now sets the switch on h16).
  6. **The Timeline's ⓘ** lists the card's flashback only when the card's reading is what makes the row a flashback, never under your switch.
  7. **The judge** reads a side as "flashback" by the same rule, and a pair is rendered again when you flip a switch on one of its scenes (its stamp includes the switch), so its verdicts are for the words it was shown.
  8. **The switch-over:** v1's Timeline already showed some scenes as flashbacks in its reading-order view (a scene whose event comes before the latest event of a scene read earlier). Those scenes get the switch set to Flashback and their own event's year (before, only the first scene of each run got a year), so the year before the flashback carries on after it in v2. That rule now lives once in `src/shared/timeline.ts` for both. The Scene details line of the switch-over summary adds "N flashbacks" when there are any. (Changed in the review, 2026-10-14: v1's flag was computed from the event links, never typed, so the switch-over sets no switch and keeps years on each run's first scene as before; a note in its plan names those scenes. See "Flashback switch review (2026-10-14)".)
  9. **No prompt changed:** the judge's mark and Ms Scribe's computed years already carried the flashback as data; the Story notes block's scene line shows no flashback (unchanged); no golden output changed.
- Alternatives: keep *Not a flashback?* marking the card not right (it would do nothing when your own switch hid the pair); show the switch on chapters too; map nothing from v1 (the flashback's typed year would then carry into unlinked scenes after it); a three-way select (Follow the book / Flashback / Not a flashback) instead of a switch with *Follow the book*.
- To change it: `src/shared/memory/flashback.ts` (the rule); `src/renderer/features/editor/SceneDetailsFields.tsx` (`FlashbackField`); `src/renderer/features/memory/checkBook/CheckReport.tsx` (*Not a flashback?*); `src/main/memory/switchover/model.ts` (v1's flashbacks); the author's check in a development build (`MYTHSCRIBE_STORY_MEMORY=v2 npm run dev`): the switch on a real flashback scene with Use AI off, its year not carrying on, and *Not a flashback?* with *Undo*.
