## 2026-10-14 · F-16.27, F-16.29, F-16.30 · P4 slice 6 review: calls made while fixing it
- Question: The review found `npm run test` red about one full run in two (`findings.perf.test.ts`'s 50 ms recompute bar at 50–57 ms, and over it in 2 of 9 runs alone too), so the final verification could not be repeated; the replay's thread loose ends and *secrets nobody learns* tested nothing on the test book (no such thread, an empty list); nothing checked that code made no candidate the labels do not name; the live report listed trap findings without their quotes; and a live run whose cost cap passed while the last scene was read reported failed bars instead of the stop. Fixing them needed these calls.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **The timing tests run last and alone:** `*.perf.test.ts` moved to their own vitest project, `perf` (`sequence.groupOrder: 1`, `fileParallelism: false`); `main` excludes them. `npm run test` still runs them, after everything else. The 50 ms budget is unchanged.
  2. **And the recompute got faster** (it sat at 37–55 ms alone on this 4-core machine, so isolation alone would not hold): `draftClaims` reads the claims as plain rows (the ORM's row mapping cost as much as the read: 8.8 → 4.6 ms on the cast book; `reading.test.ts` holds the plain read to the ORM's), `canonNotes` answers the same list while no note moved and `noteIndex` keeps its index for that list and the records' names (C6 6 → 3 ms), and `foldDetail` keeps what it folded. The cast measure is the fastest of ten, not five: the first runs after seeding 900 claims share the machine with collecting that garbage (up to 17 ms). Measured since: 28–33 ms for the cast recompute, 35–39 ms for the job, in six runs alone.
  3. **The test book gained a loose thread and an unlearned secret, P4 only:** *Mrs. Tull's introduction*, opened and advanced in s02's dialogue ("Mrs. Tull has promised to introduce him", "it will be impossible for her to introduce him") and never touched again (Mr. Harrow calls on Mr. Ashby himself), so the replay lists a thread loose end with the characters and the planned card; and *Anne's sold pearls*, a new last paragraph of 19.1 that tells the reader and no one in the book, which *Check the book* lists as the one secret nobody learns (label `secretsNobodyLearns`), scoped. A thread about the Captain's scar was tried first and dropped: P3's question "Does the Captain have a scar?" then found the thread. The knows replay's grid check now reads a secret with no learner as known to readers from the scene that tells them.
  4. **No false alarm goes unseen:** the all-`c` judge's run now also asserts that every open finding is a labelled contradiction or a trap; the sessions write P3's record pages (the Bell's Pinned note, two Ideas pages) before the notes, so an idea is in the project and never compared; two `finding` negatives on the Idea scene h11 (no C1 from Lise's blue eyes there, no C2 from Mr. Harrow's death there). The inactive draft (h10) has no negative of its own (its node is h09's, where the planted grey/brown pair lives); the unlabelled check covers it.
  5. **The live report** lists each trap that became a finding with both quotes; and a run whose cap (or an unknown cost) passed while the memory was read, its last scene included, judges nothing and says it stopped (an offline case prices only the last scene past the cap).
  6. **Not done:** reading the oracle book once and copying it for each replay (the review's other option): with the timing tests isolated it is no longer needed for the gate, and a copied project would share a project id across sessions.
- Alternatives: raising the budget (refused: it is the design's); the median of more runs; an author-made thread for the loose end (it would have needed a new label field and changed the knows replay's topic counts).
- To change it: `vitest.config.ts` (1), `src/main/memory/reading/claimStore.ts`, `src/main/memory/findings/candidateInputs.ts`, `src/shared/memory/noteValues.ts`, `src/shared/memory/typedVsText.ts` (2), `src/main/memory/testBook/{book,labels,labels.schema}.ts` and `src/main/ai/eval/knows.eval.ts` (3), `src/main/ai/eval/findings.eval.ts` (4, 5).

## 2026-10-14 · F-16.27, F-16.28, F-16.29, F-16.30 · P4 slice 6 (offline replays, the live contradictions run, the final verification): calls made while building it
- Question: P4's last slice replays the contradiction check over the test book offline (in `npm run test`), with an oracle judge and six planted ones, builds the live contradictions run for you, and verifies the phase. No prompt, migration, channel or app behaviour changed; nothing in v1 moves. These are the calls made:
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **The oracle judge reads each request as it was sent** (`src/main/ai/eval/judgeOracle.ts`): an item is a labelled contradiction when one label quote sits on each side (either way round), its question is of the label's rule (judge.v1's wording: C1 and C7 share one) and it names the label's record; `c` then, `ok` for everything else, the stronger check likewise. So a pair whose context happens to hold another label's quote (Anne's eyes and hair share a paragraph in 19.1) is never taken for it.
  2. **The replays drive the app's own service**, not IPC: the `findings` job, then `createFindingsService`'s background runs while it wants them (the recompute after each, as `memory:changed` does), then its *Check the book*, over the request path the live runs use (`liveRequestDeps`, `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` alone, the fake OpenRouter). *Not a flashback?* is `cardNotRight` on h16's flashback, as the report's button sends it. Changed 2026-10-14: the replay sets h16's switch to Not a flashback, as the button now does (see "Flashback switch (2026-10-14)"); `findings.test.ts` keeps *Not right* on a card's flashback unhiding a pair.
  3. **"Avoids unread paragraphs" is shown by editing**: after the checks, a sentence is added to the 19.1 paragraph whose quote held an underline, unread; that mark goes and the next open finding takes its place, still three. It runs last, since the edit re-renders the pairs that read 19.1.
  4. **The six planted judges:** orders that never agree (`c` the first time a pair is seen, `ok` the second: nothing opens, nothing is confirmed or underlined); all `u` (unclear only, listed, never open, never in the badge or underlined, never confirmed); all `c` (the open findings are exactly code's candidates; no negative without a candidate gets a row); every second item dropped, and the oracle's reply cut halfway at its cap (both leave pairs unjudged and counted, and the honest judge's next run settles them; a cut reply is never kept); a stronger check that clears everything (every planted finding fine with the stronger verdict `ok`, listed as cleared, nothing underlined).
  5. **The live run** (`runLiveFindings`; `MYTHSCRIBE_EVAL_LIVE=1 OPENROUTER_API_KEY=sk-or-… npm run eval:ai -- findings`, with `MYTHSCRIBE_EVAL_READ=live` to read the memory with `reader.v2`/`verify.v2` first): one pass, background judging then one *Check the book*, the bars P4-D24 as planned (no trap as a finding, at most one unlabelled; planted recall ≥ 70 % over C1–C6, C7 reported; the two orders agree on ≥ 90 % of pairs; underline precision ≥ 70 %; the stronger check confirms ≥ 80 % of the planted it sees and no trap; no reasoning tokens). It also fails on more than three underlines in a scene and on a negative that got any finding at all (a candidate code must not make), as the replays assert. **Underline precision** is measured over the marks the editor would draw in every scene at the end of the run (in the text, the default), not over 20 closed findings as the app's own question counts. **Agreement with the labels:** planted pairs `c` both ways, traps not both `c`. The check's cost is reported against design Part 5 scaled to the pairs it checked (3.3¢ per ~100), with no bar.
  6. **The 50¢ stop is a Stop:** once the reported cost passes 50¢ (or a cost cannot be known) the judge's next request is refused as the app's Stop refuses it, so what was judged stays and the check's report says it was stopped; a live reading of the memory stops at a scene's end instead, and nothing is judged after it. A provider error (a 429 the background takes quietly, a 402) stops the run and is reported with what was judged and paid for.
  7. **One recorder for the live runs:** the Ms Scribe run's request recorder moved to `liveProvider.ts` (`recordingProvider`, `CapturedCall`), shared by the contradictions run (its cut-off rate); slice 1's label matchers moved to `judgeOracle.ts`.
  8. **No box is ticked** (the run's instructions). F-16.27–16.30, the M8 row and CLAUDE.md name the checks you still owe in a development build and the runs owed before the switch-over: the live contradictions run (F-16.27's merge condition) and live fidelity for `judge.v1`.
- Alternatives: an oracle keyed on quotes alone; the replays through IPC and the reader service's queue; precision over a simulated history of answers; the stop as a cap error; a recorder per run.
- To change it: `src/main/ai/eval/judgeOracle.ts` (1, 4), `src/main/ai/eval/findings.eval.ts` (2, 3, 5, 6; the bars in `FINDINGS_PASS_BARS`), `src/main/ai/eval/liveProvider.ts` (7).
- Seen once: `findings.perf.test.ts`'s 50 ms bar measured 50.6 ms under the full suite's parallel load (this eval adds about 20 s of work beside it); it passed alone and on the second full run. Fixed in the slice 6 review (the entry above): the perf tests run last in their own project, and the recompute is faster.
- Not run: no prompt changed (token report unchanged). The live contradictions run and live fidelity for `judge.v1` need your key: `MYTHSCRIBE_EVAL_LIVE=1 OPENROUTER_API_KEY=sk-or-… npm run eval:ai -- findings` (about 1–2¢, stops at 50¢; report in `test-results/eval-findings/`) and `MYTHSCRIBE_EVAL_LIVE=1 OPENROUTER_API_KEY=sk-or-… npm run eval:ai` (judge.v1's 8 cases among the rest).

## 2026-10-14 · F-16.28 · P4 slice 5 review: calls made while fixing it
- Question: The review of slice 5 found every editor of a stacked chapter or manuscript reading its scene's whole memory (seconds of work in the background on every reading, answer and judged pair), a click on underlined words opening the card even where the underline was hidden at the caret and the card staying over the text while you typed, Escape taken by a card the pointer had only passed over, answers taken back with *Undo* still counted for underline precision (and counted twice when answered again), and a line taken away by *Not a secret*, a deleted secret or a spot check counted as a fix in the text. Fixing them needed these calls.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **The editors read only their underlines:** each region of a stacked view asks for its scene's underlines, all of them in one request, over just the findings they show; the This scene panel alone reads a scene's whole memory, as before slice 5. Measured on the 90-scene test book with a cast of 30 and an open finding in every scene: about 12 ms for one scene and 65 ms for the whole stack, where each region used to cost up to 50 ms. They are read again when the findings move, after a reading, when a name in the scene changes and when the roster changes; nothing is read while the Contradiction check is off or contradictions show in the panel only.
  2. **A click opens the card only on an underline you can see.** Once the caret is in the paragraph the underline is hidden, and clicking those words just places the caret. A card a click or a hover opened closes at your first keystroke in the scene, or when its underline goes; a card opened with Alt+Enter holds the focus and stays until Escape.
  3. **Escape:** a hover card closes and the key still does what it would have (leaving focus mode, for one); a card a click pinned takes the first Escape; only a card opened from the keyboard gives the focus back to the text.
  4. **Each underlined pair counts once, as last answered:** answering the same pair again after an *Undo* replaces its first answer, and *Undo* of the Dismissed row an answer wrote (*Not a problem*, or the *Not right* row *This one's right* left) takes its outcome out of the count. The question already asked stays asked.
  5. **A fix in the text needs changed words:** a line that went (or was read again with another value) counts for only when its scene's words changed since the underline was first drawn; *Not a secret* (whose lines are kept to undo), a deleted secret, a spot check or a rebuild that took a line away with the words unchanged adds nothing. The scene's words are kept as a hash for each drawn finding until it closes.
- Alternatives: underline only the focused region of a stacked view (the other regions would show none); keep the underlines in the scene's memory and cap the regions watched; close a pinned card only when its paragraph is edited; carry the first draw's time over to a finding *Undo* brings back instead of taking the outcome back; record the claims each memory correction deletes instead of comparing the words.
- To change it: `src/main/memory/findings/underlines.ts` `underlinesOf`, `candidateInputs.ts` `sideInputs`, `reading/views.ts` `unreadParagraphs`, `src/renderer/features/memory/memoryStore.ts` (`watchUnderlines`, `loadUnderlines`, `useSceneUnderlines`) and `useContradictionMarks.ts` (1); `src/renderer/features/editor/contradictionMarks.ts` (`mousedown`, `handleClick`, the plugin view's close) (2); `ContradictionCard.tsx` (3); `src/shared/memory/underlines.ts` (`logOutcomes`, `forgetOutcomes`) and `underlineLog.ts` `forgetAnswer`, called from `dismissed:undo` (4); `underlineLog.ts` `fixedInText`, `logShownScenes` (5).
- Not run: no prompt changed (token report unchanged); live runs still need your key.

## 2026-10-14 · F-16.28 · P4 slice 5 (contradiction underlines, the hover card and the precision question): calls made while building it
- Question: Building slice 5 (the editor's underlines for possible contradictions, their hover card, Settings' "Show contradictions" and the one question about underline precision) needed calls the plan left to the build.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **Alt+Enter opens the card from the keyboard** (the plan said Enter): plain Enter has to start a new paragraph, and the underline is hidden in the caret's paragraph anyway. With the caret in an underlined paragraph, Alt+Enter opens the card pinned and moves the focus into it; Escape gives the focus back to the text.
  2. **"Never at the caret" holds while the editor has the focus:** the paragraph holding the caret shows no underline while you type there; clicking outside the editor (the panel, the binder) shows it again.
  3. **An edited paragraph loses its underline** at the first keystroke and stays bare until it has been saved and read again; refreshes that land while the scene has unsaved edits keep it bare. Edits in other paragraphs keep their underlines.
  4. **What the precision log counts** (only findings that were drawn as underlines): *Not a problem* counts against; *This one's right*, *Update my note*, or a reading that changed or removed a side's line counts for (a fix in the text). A note conflict whose book line stands, closed because the note changed, counts for. *Not right* on a line (kept aside to undo), a moved or excluded scene, a flashback or typed detail, a character no longer on the page, a draft switch and a deleted record add nothing.
  5. **The question is asked once** ("Of the last 20 underlined contradictions, 8 were not problems. Show contradictions in the panel only?" · *Panel only* · *Keep underlines*), only while underlines are on and the Contradiction check is on; either answer, or closing it, counts as asked. *Panel only* changes the same setting as Settings.
  6. **Picking the marks:** at most three marks a scene; a finding is underlined whole or not at all (a same-scene pair is two marks, so it fits only when two are free); one quote is underlined once even when two findings share it; a side whose paragraph has a finished sentence not read yet is left out (the other side still shows).
  7. **Settings › AI › Story memory** reads "Show contradictions: underlined in the text · in the panel only"; under *Details* "Contradiction check: 40 pairs judged both ways; the two orders agreed on 37 · underlines: 14 of the last 20 were right" (before any: "no pairs judged both ways yet", "no underlined contradiction closed yet"). The pairs are the active draft's findings judged in both orders (the verdict cache keeps each order apart, so it cannot pair them).
  8. **The card reuses the panel's words:** each underline carries the finding as Needs a look shows it, so the card's rule line, "1.1: “Mara's eyes were grey”" or "Your note on Orrin: “Born: 1790”" and its buttons match the panel's rows.
  9. **The card's timing:** it opens on hover at once and closes a quarter second after the pointer leaves the underline and the card; a click on the underline (or Alt+Enter) pins it until Escape or a click elsewhere. *Update my note* opens the This scene panel and puts the proposed edit into the note conflict's row in Needs a look, where you save it.
  10. **The editor watches the scene's memory itself**, so the underlines show with the This scene panel closed; they follow every reading and every answer (in the card, the panel, Check the book or Undo in Dismissed).
  11. **The underline's colour** (`--ms-contradiction`) mixes each theme's warning colour with its quiet text colour: a soft dotted line, quieter than the spelling squiggle, in every theme without a value per theme.
- Alternatives: plain Enter on the underlined text (would break typing); hide the underline only while typing, not while the caret rests there; keep the underline on an edited paragraph until main says otherwise; count *Not right* on a line as an answer for the log; ask the question every time precision falls; a fixed delay before the hover card opens; underlines only while the panel is open.
- To change it: `src/renderer/features/editor/contradictionMarks.ts` (1–3, 9), `src/main/memory/findings/underlineLog.ts` `fixedInText` and `logGone` (4), `underlineNotice` and `src/renderer/features/memory/memoryStore.ts` `answerNotice` (5), `src/shared/memory/underlines.ts` `pickUnderlines` (6), `MemorySettings.tsx` and `memoryView.ts` `contradictionStatsLine`, `contradictionStats` in `underlineLog.ts` (7), `src/renderer/features/editor/ContradictionCard.tsx` (8, 9), `useContradictionMarks.ts` (10), `src/renderer/styles/tokens.css` (11).
- Not run: no prompt changed (token report unchanged). Live fidelity for `judge.v1` and the live contradictions run (slice 6) still need your key. Your check in a development build (`MYTHSCRIBE_STORY_MEMORY=v2 npm run dev`): two scenes that disagree, read and judged, then the underline in each, the hover card and its answers, the underline hiding at the caret and after an edit, Alt+Enter, and Settings' "in the panel only".

## 2026-10-14 · F-16.29, F-16.30, F-16.27 · P4 slice 4 review: calls made while fixing it
- Question: The review of slice 4 found the chat's consistency check sent as a paid Ms Scribe request in Plan, *Ask Ms Scribe* on a loose end using up a passage staged in the composer, *Update my note* undone (and misreported) when you typed on that note while it saved, a *This one's right* line outliving its Dismissed row, a loose end's undone *Not a problem* not coming back, a note sentence on another record's page or a Research page worded as about the page's owner, a kinship line losing the other person, C4's scene year missing, unclear findings without ⓘ, "Nothing changed since your last check" before any check, a stopped or failed check announced as finished, the report's *Go there* opening behind the dialog, and the dialog stuck on "Checking…" when the check was held as it began. Fixing them needed these calls.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **Plan answers the consistency check too** (v2 only): it is local and edits nothing, so Plan runs it as Ask and Auto do; v1's Plan still answers it with the read-only chat.
  2. **A note not on the subject's own Pinned page names the subject:** "Your note on Orrin says of Mara Vell: green eyes; the book says brown (1.2)"; a Research page always does ("Your Research page “Harbour notes” says of Orrin: born 1790; …"). The chat's text carries the same words.
  3. **Kinship keeps the other person:** "Mara Vell’s family (to Orrin): sister (1.1) · cousin (2.1)", a note "says sister to Orrin"; a name inside a slot keeps its capitals.
  4. **C4 shows the year it rests on:** "Lottie is 17 in 19.1 (year 1810, typed in its details); born 1795 (Lottie’s Pinned page)", or "stated in its text"; ⓘ says the same.
  5. **Unclear findings have ⓘ:** "the AI judged it twice and the words did not settle it", or, when both fast orders said contradiction and the stronger check could not settle it, "judged a contradiction both ways by AI; the stronger check could not settle it"; with what judging it cost.
  6. **Update my note saves once:** if you type on that note while it saves, your words win (the note keeps your draft, so the old value), the save says "Your note was being edited; save it again in a moment", and Needs a look reads the findings again (the conflict comes back to try again).
  7. **A check that ended early says so:** the activity bar says "Check the book stopped" (a Stop: "Judged 2 of 6 pairs."; a failure: its cause and next step first); a check held back as it began (the cap reached, the switch turned off or the provider removed since *Check*) ends at once with that reason, which the dialog shows instead of "Checking…". A first check that costs nothing says "Nothing new to judge: free". The dialog's list of what it looks for now names a lasting body change with no change claim and a stated age lower than an earlier one.
- Not changed (rejected with reasons): *Check* answering ok without queueing when the manuscript has no root: a project always has its manuscript root, so that path cannot be reached in an open project.
- Alternatives: keep Plan's chat answer for the check (a paid request for a free answer); name the subject only for Research pages; re-propose the note patch after her draft is saved; a separate failed state for the check instead of `stopped` with a reason.
- To change it: `src/shared/chat.ts` `routeActionFor` (1), `src/renderer/features/memory/needsALook/findingView.ts` `noteConflictLine`, `slotWords`, `yearWords`, `judgedLine` and `FindingView.year` in `src/shared/memory/findings.ts` (2–5), `needsALook/NoteConflictRow.tsx` and `research/pageStore.ts` `writeBehindPage`'s `tries` (6), `CheckProgress` in `src/shared/memory/views.ts`, `src/main/memory/findings/findingsService.ts` `checkBook`, `src/renderer/features/activity/activityJobs.ts` and `memory/checkBook/` (7).
- Not run: no prompt changed (token report unchanged). Live fidelity for `judge.v1` and the live contradictions run (slice 6) still need your key.

## 2026-10-14 · F-16.29, F-16.30, F-16.27 · P4 slice 4 (Needs a look and Check the book in the panel): calls made while building it
- Question: Building slice 4 (Needs a look's contradictions, note conflicts and loose ends; *Check the book* in the Book tab with its report; the chat's consistency answer; the activity bar; Dismissed) needed calls the plan left to the build.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **Needs a look's order** is My to-dos, Conflicts, Questions, Loose ends, Notices (P4-D20); the badge counts filed lines, typed details and open contradictions only (never unclear ones, loose ends, questions or notices). In Conflicts the contradictions touching the open scene come first ("In this scene"), then "Elsewhere in the book", then a collapsed "Unclear (n)".
  2. **The chat's consistency answer keeps absence out of its text:** the turn's text (which later requests carry as history) is only the scene's possible contradictions, or that the check is off, and where *Check the book* is; "No possible contradictions found in this scene so far", the sentences not read yet and the pairs not judged yet are drawn under it from what the turn stored, never sent. (This changes slice 3 review's item 2, whose lines were in the text.) The turn stores the scene's findings as they stood; each side has *Go to* and the turn has *Check the book…*.
  3. **"Check this scene for consistency" needs no request in v2:** the check's own words (that phrase, the chat's suggestion "Check this scene for continuity slips", and "Check consistency") are taken locally as the consistency check, as a typed action name already was, since the answer is free; any other wording still goes to the navigator. v1's router is unchanged.
  4. **Loose ends' words:** a thread "Ashby's return: last moved in 7.3, nothing since (checked up to 18.2)" (main says where it was last touched, not whether it opened or advanced there, so "moved"); a character "Orrin: last named in 6.2"; a planned card "Planned “The duel at dawn” sits before 14.2 and is not drafted". *Ask Ms Scribe* opens the chat and sends the loose end's own question; it is disabled, with the reason, while Ms Scribe is off or the chat is answering.
  5. **This one's right leaves a line** "Marked “brown” (1.2) not right" under Conflicts, with *Go there* (the scene, to fix the prose: nothing was edited), *Undo* (its Dismissed row) and ×; these lines last for the session.
  6. **A note conflict reads** "Your note on Orrin says born 1790; the book says 1792 (1.1)" (eyes, hair and scars read value first: "green eyes"; a Research page "Your Research page “Harbour notes” says …"), with both quotes, *Go there*, *Open my note*, *Update my note* when offered and *Not a problem*, and ⓘ like every finding.
  7. **Update my note saves behind the page store:** your typing on that page is saved first and the page read again after; if you keep typing on it through three tries, your words win and the save says "Your note was being edited; save it again in a moment". The helper is now shared with Ms Scribe's sheet lines (moved into the page store).
  8. **Dismissed** lists a contradiction's *Not a problem* as kind "Contradiction", "Not a problem: Mara Vell, eyes grey (1.1) and brown (1.2)", and a loose end's as kind "Loose end".
  9. **Check the book** sits in the Book tab under a "Contradictions" heading, before *Rebuild memory*, with "Last checked 2 days ago · 2 found" (or the run's progress) and *See the report*. Its dialog is the app's, so the chat's link, the "wait for Check the book" notice (*Check the book…*) and the activity bar's Open reach it. *Check* stays offered when the price is free (it sends nothing and refreshes the report). The activity bar counts pairs judged and confirmations as one bar.
  10. **The rebuild's notice** gains *Review* (the grouped summary, now with "Possible contradictions (n)" and one *Not a problem*).
  11. **Settings › AI** shows the Contradiction check switch since slice 2 (its own data-sharing row); nothing changed there.
- Alternatives: keep the scoped lines in the chat text (they would reach later requests); send the phrase to the navigator (a paid request for a free answer); word a thread's loose end by its step (main would have to say which); keep *This one's right*'s line until closed across sessions; put the check dialog inside the Book tab only.
- To change it: `src/renderer/features/thisScene/thisSceneView.ts` and `ThisScenePanel.tsx` (1, 8), `src/renderer/features/memory/needsALook/findingView.ts`, `src/shared/chat.ts` `ContradictionsTurn`, `src/renderer/features/ai/assistantStore.ts` and `ContradictionsTurn.tsx` (2), `src/shared/assistantRoute.ts` `memoryLocalRoute` and `src/main/scribe/navigate.ts` (3), `needsALook/looseEndView.ts` and `LooseEnds.tsx` (4), `memoryStore.ts` `rightMarks` and `needsALook/FindingRow.tsx` (5), `needsALook/NoteConflictRow.tsx` (6, 7), `src/renderer/features/research/pageStore.ts` `writeBehindPage` (7), `src/renderer/features/memory/checkBook/` and `src/renderer/features/activity/` (9), `RebuildMemory.tsx` and `memoryView.ts` (10).

## 2026-10-14 · F-16.27, F-16.29, F-16.30 · P4 slice 3 review: calls made while fixing it
- Question: The review of slice 3 found *Update my note* flattening the whole note paragraph (line breaks and bold or italic lost, a Pinned "Born: …⏎Eyes: …" merged into one line), the chat's v2 "Check this scene for consistency" saying "none found" with the Contradiction check off or with the scene unread, a held rebuild sweep running days later as asked spend, the sweep priced below what it could send, the rebuild review counting findings opened by later writing, an age card shown while its birth year was disputed, *Undo* not bringing a C7 finding back, and the report counting unread scenes as checked. Fixing them needed these calls.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **Update my note changes only what you changed:** the words both texts share at the start and the end keep their formatting; the new words take the formatting where the change starts; a line break you type stays a line break. The save re-checks that the proposal is still offered for that paragraph. It is not offered for a paragraph longer than 4,000 characters (the save could not take it) or one whose value code cannot place.
  2. **The chat's consistency answer (v2) never claims an absence it did not check:** with the Contradiction check off it says so and where to turn it on; a scene not read yet "has not been checked"; otherwise "No possible contradictions found in this scene so far", followed by how many of its sentences are not read yet and how many pairs in the book are not judged yet (or that the check is running).
  3. **The rebuild sweep is priced and capped alike:** it judges at most 10 pairs per 15 scenes the rebuild read (at most 60), which is what Rebuild memory's price counts. Held back when it would run (the switch off, no provider, the daily cap) or while another rebuild runs, it is dropped and never run later.
  4. **The rebuild review lists what its sweep opened:** the keys of the open findings when the sweep ends are kept with the review; findings later writing opens never join it (a finding you bring back with *Undo* does). Its notice's lines, sheets and names are counted once when the rebuild closes (what it added), so the app does not rebuild the summary on every update; its contradictions count is the ones still open. With the sweep dropped, the review lists none.
  5. **An age question hands over to Ms Scribe** while an open possible contradiction disputes the birth year it is computed from (C1 or C6 on the birth year), as well as C4.
  6. **Undo of Not a problem on a C7 finding** brings it back at the next local recompute (not only at the next Check the book). Its stronger check's verdict is remembered under its words, so the next Check the book confirms it again for free.
  7. **The report's "checked" scenes** count only scenes with words that were read; the unread ones are listed under "not checked".
  8. A loose end whose planned card or last scene was deleted since the list was read answers "no longer there" instead of an error.
  9. The judge's cost note and the data-sharing row (app and site) say C7 pairs go on Check the book, and that Check the book and the sweep may go past the Cloud monthly cap, never past the daily cap. "1 possible contradiction waits for Check the book" is singular now.
- Not changed: P1's filed-line update (`updateFiledLine`) still rewrites a whole line, which only ever touches the single plain line it filed.
- Alternatives: refuse *Update my note* on a formatted paragraph; ask the renderer for the switch state (the main side's view says it now, `FindingsView.on`); keep the sweep for later; price the sweep at its 60-pair cap on every book; keep dismissed C7 rows as tombstones.
- To change it: `src/shared/memory/pageMarks.ts` `replaceBlockText`, `src/main/memory/findings/findingActions.ts` and `findingViews.ts` `noteEdit` (1, 8), `src/renderer/features/memory/needsALook/findingView.ts` and `FindingsView` in `src/shared/memory/views.ts` (2), `src/main/memory/findings/findingsService.ts` `sweepPairs`/`dueSweep` (3), `src/main/memory/reading/rebuild.ts` (4), `src/main/scribe/instant.ts` `openFindingOn` (5), `src/main/memory/findings/recompute.ts` `restore` and the handlers' `restoreFindings` (6), `src/main/memory/findings/checkBook.ts` (7), `src/shared/aiCostNotes.ts`, `src/shared/aiSettings.ts`, `site/public/docs/index.html` (9).
- Not run: no prompt changed (token report unchanged; `docs/AI-COST-REGISTRY.md` regenerated for the cost note). Live fidelity for `judge.v1` still needs your key (`MYTHSCRIBE_EVAL_LIVE=1 OPENROUTER_API_KEY=sk-or-… npm run eval:ai`).

## 2026-10-14 · F-16.27, F-16.29, F-16.30 · P4 slice 3 (Check the book and Needs a look's answers, main side): calls made while building it
- Question: Building slice 3 (Check the book's price, run and report; *Not a problem*, *This one's right* and *Update my note* on a finding; loose ends and their *Not a problem*; the rebuild's sweep and its notices; the instant card's handover; continuity in v2; channels only, the panel is slice 4's) needed calls the plan left to the build.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **The report keeps what it found as keys, read again when you open it:** a finding closed since (*Not a problem*, *Update my note*, a revision) drops out of the report; one brought back by *Undo* shows again. The secrets nobody learns are kept as they were at the check (the secret, the scene readers know it since, "checked up to"), never as an absence.
  2. **The report says when the check did not finish:** it carries "stopped" (your Stop, or a provider failure) and how many pairs were left unjudged, beside the plan's *not checked* list (travel times and distances, where objects are, anything the book never states; scenes not read yet; findings past the 20 confirmations).
  3. **Price:** "about" counts the confirmations due plus a quarter of the new pairs, "at most" every pair that could open, both never past 20 confirmations; "free" only when no fast call and no confirmation would go. A repeat check on an unchanged book is free and sends nothing (every fast verdict and every strong confirmation is remembered under its own words).
  4. **Which findings the stronger check confirms first:** by rule (C1 first), then those touching the scene you have open (`memory:checkBook` takes its id), then the newest. A confirmed finding is not confirmed again until its words change.
  5. **Spend:** Check the book is spend you asked for: it judges past the day's 30 background pairs and past the Cloud monthly cap, never past your daily cap (it stops there quietly and keeps what it judged). Refusals at the start (no key, the daily cap reached, the Contradiction check off) come back as data with a next step, not as an error, so the panel can show them in place.
  6. **C7 is judged only by Check the book;** background judging never sends a C7 pair (its rows are kept only for the check).
  7. **The rebuild's sweep:** a rebuild that read any scene ends with one asked sweep of at most 60 pairs, tried once (a failure does not retry it on every open), priced in the rebuild's estimate as two calls per 15 scenes, at most 12 (only with the Contradiction check on).
  8. **Notices:** "Rebuild finished: N new lines on N sheets, N names, N possible contradictions" shows only for rebuilds that finish from now on; *Done* dismisses it (its *Review* opens the grouped summary in slice 4). "N possible contradictions wait for Check the book · about X" shows when the day's background limit or the Cloud cap left pairs; *Not now* hides it for the day.
  9. **This one's right** is offered (and accepted) only when both sides are passages of the book. On a note conflict the book is canon, so the answers are *Update my note* or *Not a problem*; a year side is never offered.
  10. **Update my note** is offered only when the book's side is narration in the present (never speech, rumour, a memory, a thought, a dream or a hypothetical) and code can find the note's value in its sentence; the proposal is that sentence with the book's value (for kin, the book's kin word). Saving it makes the line your own words (its AI marks go). A Research page is saved through the same path as an editor save; the open editor reloading it comes with slice 4's panel.
  11. **The instant card hands over to Ms Scribe** while an open possible contradiction names the record and slot (C1, C6, and C4 for an age), only with the Contradiction check on (off, findings are hidden, so the card stands).
  12. **Continuity in v2** stays its own route: until slice 4's chat turn, the chat answers it locally with the open scene's open possible contradictions (no request) and points to Check the book. The v1 route is unchanged; the old `routeForMemory` helper is removed (it no longer changed anything).
  13. **Not a problem's label** reads "Mara Vell: eyes grey (1.1) and brown (1.2): not a problem", with both quotes; a loose end's reads "Loose end: Orrin (last in 3.2): not a problem".
- Alternatives: store the found findings' views in the report (stale after an answer); retry the rebuild sweep on every open; price "about" as the confirmations alone; offer *This one's right* on a note conflict (it would mark the book not right); have the instant card show both values itself.
- To change it: `src/main/memory/findings/checkBook.ts` (1–4), `src/main/memory/findings/findingsService.ts` (5–7), `src/main/memory/reading/rebuild.ts` and `readerService.ts` (7, 8), `src/main/memory/findings/findingActions.ts` and `findingViews.ts` (9, 10, 13), `src/shared/memory/noteValues.ts` `patchNoteParagraph` (10), `src/main/scribe/instant.ts` (11), `src/shared/assistantRoute.ts`, `src/renderer/features/ai/assistantStore.ts` and `src/renderer/features/memory/needsALook/findingView.ts` (12).
- Not run: no new prompt version (`judge.v1` only exports how it sizes the confirm render; the token report is unchanged). Live fidelity for `judge.v1`, including its two stronger-check cases, still needs your key (`MYTHSCRIBE_EVAL_LIVE=1 OPENROUTER_API_KEY=sk-or-… npm run eval:ai`); the checks in a development build come with slice 4's panel.

## 2026-10-13 · F-16.27 · P4 slice 2 review: calls made while fixing it
- Question: The review of slice 2 found verdicts kept for words the judge never saw (a time skip added between two scenes, a story year typed, a context sentence or a note sentence changed), a verdict written onto a pair that changed while its call was out, a half-answered pair that could spend the day's background judging in one burst, a recompute four times over its 50 ms budget, judging shown as "Reading 1 of 1 scenes", counts sent while the Contradiction check is off, and a question that told the judge no change was shown. Fixing them needed these calls.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **What the verdicts were given for is kept on the finding** (migration 0039, an added column): a cheap stamp of everything the judge's words rest on (the two passages' scenes, their cards, the story years you typed, the scenes between them, the note paragraph, the names) and the remembered-verdict keys of the words. A change anywhere in those has the pair read again; if its words moved, its verdicts go and the pair is judged again (free when the same words were judged before). An edit elsewhere in the scene keeps them.
  2. **A verdict lands only on the pair it was given for:** if the pair changed while its call was out, the verdict is not written (it is remembered under its own words) and the swapped call is not sent for it.
  3. **A run that settles nothing pushes nothing:** only a pair that became open, unclear or fine moves the views; a pair left with one order answered waits for the next change, a reopen or a settings change, and never queues another run by itself. The recompute hands pairs to the judge only when it moved something.
  4. **A background run cut short** (today's cap, a provider failure) still counts the pairs it sent toward the 30 a day and shows what it settled; a rate limit or network failure in the background goes quietly (never a "could not be read" row); Check the book shows it.
  5. **Judging is not reading:** the header's indicator counts scenes only.
  6. **The counts follow the switch:** with the Contradiction check off, `memory:findingsChanged` says none, as `memory:findings` does; a settings change sends them again.
  7. **C2's body question no longer says "with no change shown"** ("Mara's hair differs between these two appearances. Can both be true?"), unlike the plan's wording (P4 §2.2): code only infers that from a change line the reader did not find, and the rules already tell the judge to allow a change the story shows or allows. The stronger check's example reason is "grey eyes in one passage, brown in the other". judge.v1 was edited in place (never released; the remembered verdicts are keyed by the words, so old ones are never reused).
  8. **Costs:** the judge.v1 row's out figure is a ten-pair batch's cap (280), not the design's 300, which cannot occur; the in figure stays the design's 1,500 (slice 2's item 11). The judge's note says a background call of 1–4 pairs is about 1,200 in and 120 out.
  9. **Data sharing** names what a pair carries beside the name: a secret's or plot thread's label and the title of the note or Research page.
  10. **A reply that leaves a sent pair unanswered is not kept** in the request cache, so the next run asks again.
- Alternatives: keep the stamp in the `extra` column (no migration); re-render every finding on every recompute (correct, but past the budget on a long book); hash only the claims' sentences (misses time skips, years and notes); retry a half-answered order once a day.
- To change it: `src/main/memory/findings/judge.ts` (`pairInputs`, `readPair`, `judgeFindings`), `src/main/memory/findings/findingStore.ts` (`upsertFindings`, `setVerdicts`), `src/main/memory/findings/findingsService.ts` (3, 4), `src/main/jobs/indexQueue.ts` `uncounted` and `src/main/memory/reading/readerService.ts` (5), `src/main/memory/findings/findingsJob.ts` `findingCounts` (6), `src/main/ai/prompts/judge.v1.ts` (7), `src/shared/aiCostNotes.ts` (8), `src/shared/aiSettings.ts` (9).
- Not run: live fidelity for `judge.v1` (8 cases, `MYTHSCRIBE_EVAL_LIVE=1 OPENROUTER_API_KEY=… npm run eval:ai`) still needs your key.

## 2026-10-13 · F-16.27 · P4 slice 2 (judge.v1, both orders, the verdict cache, background judging): calls made while building it
- Question: Building slice 2 (feature `judge`, the Contradiction check switch, `judge.v1`, the two-call batches, the verdict cache keys, the background judge on the reader's queue, `memory:findings`; no UI) needed calls the plan left to the build.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **What a pair shows the judge:** each passage line names its place, whether it is earlier, later, in the same scene (earlier or later paragraph), the same paragraph, a note or the book side of a note pair, its voice and mode ("on the page" for a presence), "flashback" and the story year you typed or the scene states (never a carried one), then what code read it as saying ("says grey") and its sentence with one either side. The question names the record and the slot, never which passage is which, so the swapped call reads true.
  2. **Fitting a pair in 260 tokens:** the passages' context is cut around the quote from 450 characters down to 160 until the pair fits; the cut is measured without the scene numbers, so moving a scene never changes the words the judge reads (or the remembered verdict).
  3. **The remembered verdict's key** leaves out the pair's number and the scenes' numbers and folds every name and nickname to its record, so a scene move and a Rename in book cost nothing. A secret's or thread's label is not a name: renaming one has its pairs judged again.
  4. **One "fine" settles a pair:** a run always asks both orders, but a pair left with one "fine" and the other order unanswered (a reply cut off) is closed as fine and not asked again (the plan's "any ok clears it"); a pair left with one "contradiction" waits, and the next run asks only the missing order. The judge's reason shown is the A,B call's, else the B,A call's.
  5. **What counts against the limits:** pairs settled from remembered verdicts are free and count toward neither the 10 a run nor the 30 a day; a pair sent counts once whichever orders it needed, answered or not. A call's cost is shared equally among the pairs it carried.
  6. **Quiet holds:** with the Contradiction check off, Use AI off, no provider, a rebuild running, today's 30 pairs used, the daily cap reached or (on Cloud) the monthly cap reached, a judge job goes quietly and the reading queue it shares is never paused; the next change that moves a finding (or turning the switch on, a reopen, a rebuild finishing) queues it again. The daily cap reached in the middle of a run keeps what was judged.
  7. **After a rebuild,** background judging resumes on its own (at most 10 pairs, then 30 a day) until slice 3 adds the rebuild's own sweep of 60.
  8. **Check the book's job** exists from this slice: it judges every unjudged pair as spend you asked for (past the Cloud cap and the daily pair limit, never past the daily cap); C7, the stronger check and the report come with slice 3 and its channel.
  9. **`memory:findings`** answers nothing while the Contradiction check is off; with Use AI off it still lists what was judged ("judging paused", P4-D21).
  10. **The stronger check's render** uses the same version with its own reply line (a verdict and up to 20 words with every verdict), so the batch rules stay one cached prefix.
  11. **Costs:** the registry rows keep the design's sizes (1,500 / 300 a call, 600 / 90 for the stronger check) so Check the book and the heavy month stay priced as designed; the eval fixtures measure 420–1,360 tokens a call on the test book (2,683 for ten pairs at the cap) and about 640 for the stronger check.
- Alternatives: show only the quotes, no values; key the cache with scene numbers; ask the second order even after a "fine"; count free pairs toward the limits; pause the reading queue when judging is off; leave the checkBook job kind for slice 3.
- To change it: `src/main/ai/prompts/judge.v1.ts` (1, 2, 10), `src/main/memory/findings/judge.ts` (1, 3, 4, 5), `src/main/memory/findings/findingsService.ts` (5, 6, 8), `src/main/memory/reading/readerService.ts` `closeRebuild` (7), `src/main/memory/findings/findingViews.ts` (9), `src/shared/aiCostNotes.ts` (11).
- Not run: live fidelity for `judge.v1` (8 cases: `MYTHSCRIBE_EVAL_LIVE=1 OPENROUTER_API_KEY=… npm run eval:ai`) needs your key; the live contradictions run (false alarms, judge agreement, underline precision) is slice 6's, also yours to run.

## 2026-10-13 · F-16.27, F-16.29 · P4 slice 1 review: calls made while fixing it
- Question: The review of slice 1 found false candidates, missed ones, findings that kept verdicts for words the judge never saw, and a slow note check. Fixing them needed these calls.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **One note conflict per value, not per scene:** when your note says "Eyes: blue" and the book says grey in twelve scenes, that is one finding (grey against blue, at the first scene the narration says grey), not twelve; a different value (brown) is its own finding. *Not a problem* on it holds until an earlier scene says grey too.
  2. **Unnamed relatives:** two family lines about relatives who are not on your list are compared only when they name the same relation ("son of the late steward" and "son of a clergyman": Dunmore's lie stays a candidate), never "daughter of a miller" against "mother of two boys". A relation to someone on your list is compared by its kin word ("the Captain's sister" and "his younger sister" agree).
  3. **A value between two others** ("grey-blue" between "grey" and "blue") no longer hides the conflict between them: values are grouped by the first one stated, and each group is paired with the first narrated value.
  4. **Deaths:** "not dead", "nearly killed", "presumed dead", "buried alive", "might be dead" and "rumoured dead" are no death; "had died" is. A value that is neither ("presumed dead") neither raises nor cancels a death; "alive" in a flashback does not cancel a death either, and does not count as alive for loose ends.
  5. **Ages in months, weeks or days** ("six months old", "eighteen months") are never compared with years.
  6. **Flashbacks:** a "learned it" line in a flashback scene, and a thread advanced in a flashback scene, are hidden like the other flashback cases (*Check the book* lists them with *Not a flashback?*).
  7. **A thread's loose-end question** says how far "last" holds when the later scenes are not read yet: "… was last advanced in 7.3 (as far as the book is read, up to 18.2). Where does it go next?".
  8. **A re-opened thread** counts when it is opened again in the same scene before the advance.
- Alternatives: one finding per scene for notes (the plan's first reading); never compare unnamed relatives; keep the first verdicts for a re-read claim.
- To change it: `src/shared/memory/candidates.ts` (1–4, 6, 8), `src/shared/memory/years.ts` `ageYears` (5), `src/shared/memory/looseEnds.ts` (7), `src/main/memory/findings/findingStore.ts` `judgedPairMoved` (the slice 1 entry's item 9, below).

## 2026-10-13 · F-16.27, F-16.29 · P4 slice 1 (candidates, loose ends, the local recompute): calls made while building it
- Question: Building slice 1 (the `finding` and `verdict_cache` tables, rules C1–C7 in code, loose ends, the recompute after every memory change; no AI, no UI) needed calls the plan left to the build.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **A year is not a side.** C4's year (typed or quoted, and where) rides along with the pair (`extra`), so a pair is always two passages, or a passage and a note line.
  2. **C7** (history and details, *Check the book* only) compares values within one slot only, needs at least one side told by the narration as happening now, and skips any pair C1 already makes.
  3. **A dead character "here"** pairs each later appearance with the latest death before it (not every death).
  4. **A falling age** compares with the last age stated outside a flashback; a flashback scene's lower age is made and hidden.
  5. **A thread advancing after it was resolved** is not flagged when the thread was opened again in between.
  6. **Calendars:** two years are compared only when their era marks match (a mark such as "AR" or "BC" beside the digits; none on both counts as the same).
  7. **Loose ends:** a thread you made but never touched is not a loose end (nothing to pick up); the character's question names them ("Where is Celia Ashby after that?").
  8. **Timing:** the `findings` recompute rides the name-scan queue (1.5 s after the last change), and runs after the passages job when a record page or a Research page is saved, because your notes are read from the search index.
  9. **Held findings:** a finding whose sentence you just edited stays (not shown, not closed) while that sentence waits to be read again; once it is read, the finding closes if its rule no longer finds it. A claim read again keeps its finding (the same place and value), but its verdicts are kept only while the judge would read the same thing: a new quote, other sentences, another voice or mode (a narrated line rewritten as speech), the two passages swapped in order, a changed story year or a flashback that came or went clears them, and the remembered verdicts (free) or the judge decide again. (Changed in the slice 1 review: at first a re-read claim kept its verdicts.)
  10. **A thread's status** is decided by its last counted step: a thread resolved and then advanced again is open (this changes the knows eval's expectation for Lottie's match, which h15 now advances).
  11. **The test book:** h15 "The portrait" (1815), h16 "The gate, years before" (a flashback, 1811), and a planned card with no words, "The Captain's reckoning", before them. The hair that changes with no word of it (C2) is planted inside h15 (dark under the lamp, fair at dinner), not across scenes, so no earlier "as of" answer moves; the dated note puts Anne in Vellmouth (the plan said London) while h15 has her at Greyhallow. Dunmore's lie in h02 is now a candidate the judge must rule fine (plan §1.6), not "no candidate". P4's records and scenes stay out of the older eval fixtures, so the token report did not move; one P3 search test builds the book without them.
- Alternatives: a year as a third kind of side; C7 across slots or on speech alone; every death paired with every later appearance; flag an advance even after a re-opening; read notes from the pages directly on every recompute; plant the hair change across two scenes.
- To change it: `src/shared/memory/candidates.ts` (1–6), `src/shared/memory/looseEnds.ts` (7), `src/main/ipc/handlers.ts` `queueFindings` (8), `src/main/memory/findings/recompute.ts` (9), `src/main/ai/eval/knows.eval.ts` `labelStatus` (10), `src/main/memory/testBook/` (11).
- Not run: no live run exists for slice 1 (no AI); the live contradictions run belongs to slice 6 and needs your key.

## 2026-10-13 · F-16.27–16.30 · P4 plan: the calls the design left open
- Question: Planning P4, contradictions and Needs a look (`docs/research/story-memory-2026-10-11/plans/P4.md`, six slices, nothing built yet), needed calls the design did not make. Details are in the plan's §8 (P4-D1 to P4-D26).
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **What can raise a contradiction:** two values the book gives for the same fixed thing need at least one side told by the narration as happening now; a death needs narration; against your notes, the book side may be speech, a rumour or a memory (the note is the canon side), and the AI judge sees which it is. For those, *Update my note* is not offered (also not for thoughts, dreams and what-ifs).
  2. **Grouping values:** "grey" and "grey-blue" count as one; with three different eye colours you get two pairs, each against the earliest one the narration states.
  3. **A dead character "here":** only when the scene shows them on the page with a quote, never because you typed them as POV or "also here"; a later sentence bringing them back cancels it. Hair and scars changing count only between two appearances in a row with no sentence saying they changed.
  4. **Groups** (left to P4 by P2b): a character belongs to a group when the book relates them to it, names it in their role, allegiance, rank, occupation or home, or one of the two sheets names the other in a Pinned line. It is used only to skip "told before learning" when their group already knew.
  5. **Ages against the story year** use only a year you typed for that scene or one the scene itself states, never a year carried forward; one year either way is allowed.
  6. **A thread "advancing after it was resolved"** means an *advances* step only; re-opening it is not flagged.
  7. **Your notes are checked** only where code can read the value (a "Label: value" line or a plain sentence such as "Orrin's eyes are green"), at most two note sentences per fact; changing state (where someone is, injuries) is checked only against your dated lines for that same scene.
  8. **The judge:** each pair is judged twice in two separate calls, with the passages swapped, so the second answer is not an echo of the first. Both must say "contradiction"; any "fine" clears it; otherwise it is "unclear", shown muted, never counted in the badge, never underlined. Every answer is remembered, so an unchanged pair (even after a scene move or a Rename in book) never costs again.
  9. **Background budget:** at most 10 pairs after a reading (two calls), at most 30 a day; a rebuild ends with one sweep of up to 60 (in its price); the rest wait for *Check the book*, with a priced notice.
  10. **Re-checking:** a pair whose sentence you just edited waits (not shown, not closed) until it is read again; a pair that needs a scene not to be a flashback is hidden and listed by *Check the book* with *Not a flashback?*.
  11. **Underlines:** at most 3 marks a scene, each finding marking its quote in each scene; none in a paragraph you changed and that is not read yet, none in the paragraph you are typing in; your notes are never underlined (the book's quote shows the note on hover). Precision counts *Not a problem* against and *This one's right*, *Update my note* or a fix in the text for; the "panel only?" question comes once, and Settings can switch back.
  12. ***This one's right*** marks the other passage not right (as *Not right › Wrong value* does) and never edits your prose; on a note conflict the book wins (your answer 1), so it offers *Update my note* instead, which proposes your note with the book's value for you to edit and save (no AI call).
  13. **Loose ends:** the window is 8 scenes or a quarter of the book, whichever is more; threads count only up to where the book has been read; characters need 3 or more scenes and must not be dead; planned cards before the furthest drafted scene with no link; each has *Not a problem* and *Ask Ms Scribe*.
  14. **Needs a look's order:** My to-dos on top, then Conflicts, Questions, Loose ends, Notices (the rebuild summary among them). The badge counts conflicts only.
  15. **Switches:** a **Contradiction check** switch of its own; off hides findings and underlines; with Use AI off, findings already judged stay (marked "judging paused").
  16. **Check the book** shows "about" and "at most" prices (the stronger check runs only on what the fast one flags) and "free" when nothing changed; its report stays until the next check. "Check this scene for consistency" in the chat answers from the findings at no cost, with *Check the book*.
  17. **The live test** (needs your key, after the build): `MYTHSCRIBE_EVAL_LIVE=1 OPENROUTER_API_KEY=sk-or-… npm run eval:ai -- findings` (stops at 50¢; about 1–2¢), with bars: no trap flagged and at most one unplanted finding, at least 70 % of the planted ones found, the two orders agreeing on 90 % of pairs, underline precision of 70 % or more, and the stronger check confirming 80 % of planted findings and no trap.
- Alternatives: flag only narration against narration (no note conflicts from speech); judge both orders in one call; show "unclear" pairs in the badge; underline notes too; let the judge read note sentences code cannot parse; no daily background limit; *This one's right* on a note conflict marking the book's line not right.
- To change it: `plans/P4.md` §8, before the slice that builds it.
