## 2026-10-13 · F-16.24, F-16.25, F-16.26 · P3 slice 6 review: calls made fixing the scoring, the labels and the miss log
- Question: The review of slice 6 found the search-miss log counting a ranking drop as an FTS miss (the metric that decides F-16.35), replay assertions that checked the oracle's own words, sheet lines scored on place but never on their words, absence answers scored on their scope alone (a "yes, he knows" passed), no question about the test book's traps (an Idea scene, a cut scene, an Ideas page read as canon), a contested value answerable from one side, an ambiguous "first to be told", no selection or other F-5.26 pass in the routing bar, a who-knows card's scoped line and age cards unchecked, system turns skipped by the "no absence sent" check, and the "nothing written" check counting rows. These are the calls made fixing them (no prompt, migration or channel change; nothing in v1 moves; item 7 of the slice 6 entry below is changed).
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **A search miss is a widened citation at a paragraph the turn's words do not match** (`passageMatches`, one FTS query per widened citation), not "outside the hits": a paragraph FTS placed but the ranking left out was found. The reviewers suggested seeding the hits with every placed paragraph instead; that would have changed the paragraphs cite.v1's scope check lists (each scene's best, earliest first) and is cut at the navigation's 50 hits in a long book, so the miss check asks the index directly. The per-scene ranking still makes "When does Lise first learn…" widen once (a strong-tier second call); keeping two paragraphs a scene for first/last questions would avoid it at about 20 more menu lines a question. **Open for you:** keep one paragraph a scene, or two?
  2. **Seven more questions (38):** three traps (kind `trap`: "Whom did Clara Pell marry?" only the cut scene answers; "Is the Captain related to Mr. Varrow?" only the Idea scene; "Has the Captain been married before?" only the Captain's Ideas page), each right when it ends not found (or a scoped "I found no scene…") or names the source for what it is, and wrong whenever a sentence states the trap's words without naming it so; four routes (F-5.26's "Polish this." on a selection, Pacing & structure, Dialogue, Editor's notes), the selection passed as the chat's focus. The inactive draft (h10) cannot be a trap: it is never indexed or read. "Who is the first to be told…" became "When is Lottie's engagement first mentioned?" (book order; who was told first in the story is Bel, before 9.1).
  3. **New measures and bars** (P3-D20 extended): sheet-line words (exactly one line holding the label's words; bar 90 %, beside place), an absence answer whose main claim says the person knows (bar 0), an Idea, Idea scene, cut scene or Not canon citation kept on a sentence that does not name it so or hedge, on any answer (bar 0), trap honesty (100 %). A contested lookup is correct only with both passages cited (`expected.contested`, moved out of `also`); a who-knows card must carry its labelled `checkedUpTo` (5.1, deliberately: the late edit leaves 6.1 unsettled); an age card scores wrong (the test book's ages are all stated).
  4. **The oracle checker plays cite.v1's label rules:** no to a sentence citing what is never the book without naming it, no to an absence answer's "knows" claim. A plant can now replace the checker too (a fooled `cite.v1` that supports everything), so the adversarial replay shows both the app refusing a planted claim and, past a fooled checker, the scoring counting it wrong.
  5. **No app-side check that a sheet line's words come from the message:** a request such as "add what we said about her mother to her sheet" draws its words from the conversation, so a word test would refuse it. Decision 3's confirmation and Undo stay the guard; the eval measures the words. **Open for you** if you want a check in the app.
  6. **"Nothing written" compares the claim, card and reading rows whole** (in the replays, `runScribe.test.ts` and `navigate.test.ts`), and "no absence sent" checks system turns too, past the prompts' fixed rules (which say "never say someone does not know").
- Alternatives: seed the hits with every placed paragraph; two paragraphs a scene now; trap words as notFound questions only; a pin-text check in `pinOf`; leave the who-knows edit until after those questions.
- To change it: `src/main/memory/passageIndexer.ts` (`passageMatches`), `src/main/scribe/runScribe.ts` (the miss loop), `src/main/scribe/step0.ts` (`orderedSceneSearch`, for two a scene), `src/main/memory/testBook/labels.ts` / `labels.schema.ts`, `src/main/ai/eval/scribe.eval.ts` (`SCRIBE_PASS_BARS`, `scoreAsk`), `src/main/ai/eval/scribeOracle.ts`.

## 2026-10-13 · F-16.24, F-16.25, F-16.26 · P3 slice 6 (offline replays, the live Ms Scribe run, the final verification): calls made while building it
- Question: P3's last slice labels the test book with the questions Ms Scribe is asked, replays them offline against an oracle (in `npm run test`), builds the live run for you, and verifies the phase. No prompt, migration, channel or app behaviour changed; nothing in v1 moves. These are the calls made:
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **31 labelled questions, not about 24** (`TEST_BOOK_LABELS.questions`): every kind the plan names, with two or more of most (7 instant, 3 lookups, 4 open, 3 ordinal, 2 absence, 2 idea, 2 not found, 4 routes incl. F-5.26's vague "Make this scene better.", 4 sheet lines incl. Plan). Each question's words are unique (the oracle and the scoring find a question by them), so "Mr. Varrow's eyes?" (an instant card as of 10.1) and "What colour are Mr. Varrow's eyes?" (contested as of 14.1) are worded apart.
  2. **The pages are the Bell's Pinned note ("kept by the Widow Marsh") and the Captain's and Lottie's Ideas**, not a Pinned "Born:" line: a birth year on a sheet would change the ages P2b's labels score. They are written only in the scribe replays and run (`addTestBookPages`), so the knows and planted runs read the book as before.
  3. **One labelled late edit** (Clara's age, 1.1 → "eight-and-twenty" after the book was read), made before any question with the anchors check, so a stale fact is answered from the paragraph as it reads now. Labels check their words against the edited text.
  4. **`--read live` is an environment variable**, `MYTHSCRIBE_EVAL_READ=live`: vitest refuses an unknown flag, so `npm run eval:ai -- scribe --read live` cannot work. The live reading stops at the same $1 as the questions; a question after a stop is listed as skipped.
  5. **What the scores count:** answer correctness covers the lookups, open, ordinal and idea questions (the label's scene, or page, and words cited); an absence answer is right when it says what was searched and states no absence as fact (an honest Not found with its scope counts too); citation validity is measured on every answer reply the model wrote, before cite.v1 or a retry act on it (a sent id and words that item holds, the code check's `quoteHeld`; a map never counts); the open-question cost averages the navigated answers (open, ordinal, absence, idea, not found). Instant rate, cite.v1's agreement with the oracle checker, widenings and misses, cut-offs and reasoning tokens are reported with no bar. The bars are P3-D20's.
  6. **The replays call the chat's own logic** (`runNavigate`, then `runScribe` with the navigation, in Ask or Plan as labelled, no history), the way `ai:navigate` and `ai:agent` call it, not through IPC; the read-first reads through `runReading` and the anchors job's `runAnchors`.
  7. **Changed by the slice 6 review (entry above):** "When does Lise first learn of Lottie's engagement?" searches every earlier scene, and the ordered search keeps one paragraph per scene, so 10.1's best is ¶2 ("engaged"), not ¶5 ("She had overheard every word"), though FTS placed ¶5 too. The answer asked for the scene (Self-Route) and the turn logged a search miss. That was a ranking drop (F-16.25's per-scene ranking), not something FTS missed, so it said nothing about F-16.35 (embeddings); a paragraph the turn's words match is no longer logged as a miss.
  8. **The fake OpenRouter streams** (`fakeChatServer`, SSE for `stream: true`), so Ms Scribe's streamed answer goes through the same SDK path offline as it will live.
  9. **No box is ticked** (the run's instructions); F-16.24–16.26, the M8 row and CLAUDE.md name the checks you still owe in a development build and the live runs owed before the switch-over (the live Ms Scribe run and live fidelity for `agent.v9`, `cite.v1`, `navigator.v1`).
- Alternatives: 24 questions; a "Born:" Pinned line; a `--read` flag through a wrapper script; correctness over every answered kind; validity counted after cite.v1.
- To change it: `src/main/memory/testBook/labels.ts`, `labels.schema.ts`, `project.ts` (1–3), `src/main/ai/eval/scribe.eval.ts` (4–6, the bars in `SCRIBE_PASS_BARS`), `src/main/ai/eval/scribeOracle.ts`, `fakeChatServer.ts` (8), `src/main/scribe/step0.ts` / `tools.ts` (7, if one paragraph a scene should become more).

## 2026-10-13 · F-16.24, F-16.25 · P3 slice 5 review: calls made fixing the sources, the cost parts and the instant cards
- Question: The review of slice 5 found a cited scene note toasting "That passage is no longer in the scene", a Research source waiting 3 s on an editor that never comes, a record source doing nothing in focus mode, a Research source repeating its title, a read-first's cost labelled "navigator", and instant cards that left out a suspicion the sheet lists, drew a re-checked readers-since as checked, showed an AI-read birth year with no ⓘ, and stored "Who knows the sold map" with nothing under it as history. These are the calls made fixing them (nothing changes in v1; no prompt, no migration). Items 2–5 of the slice 5 entry below are changed where they differ.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **A cited scene note opens the scene with its Notes panel** (the docked one, or the floating one in focus mode, as a search hit in notes does): main marks the item's target `notes: true` (`CitationTarget`, read leniently), and the chat never searches the scene's text for a quote that is in its notes.
  2. **A Research source opens its page with the Research tab**, as search and This scene's *Go there* do (one helper now, `openResearchPage`); the page editor takes no passage jump yet, so it lands on the page, not the paragraph.
  3. **A record source (Pinned, Ideas, who-knows, an age) leaves focus mode first**, since a sheet has no focus view, then opens the sheet at its top. The plan asked for "the record's Pinned or Ideas": the sheet shows both, but there is **no jump to the cited paragraph yet** (the page editors take no jump). Say if you want one; it needs the page editor to accept a passage jump, which search's Research hits would use too.
  4. **A source drops its title when its reference names it**, a Research page's ("Research “Harbour notes” ¶1") as well as a record's.
  5. **The read-first is its own cost part**, "read first 0.1¢", listed before the navigator's (`COST_PARTS` gains `read`, read leniently; `ai:navigate` reports `readUsd` apart): a lookup handed to Ms Scribe after a read-first no longer shows a "navigator" part when no navigator request ran.
  6. **Who knows a secret lists a partial line too** (suspects, believes otherwise), muted, as the secret's sheet and This scene list it, so "No other scene shows anyone learning it (checked up to …)" covers only what the card leaves out (lines that do not count, and later ones) and never contradicts the sheet.
  7. **A who-knows card whose readers-since rests on a reveal being re-checked hands over to Ms Scribe** (as a stale holder already did): an instant card answers from live evidence only.
  8. **A computed age shows the birth year it counts from as an AI line** ("Born: 1795", noted "1.1", with ⓘ and *Not right*) when the AI read it, as the sheet's Age does, and its mark then reads "Computed from your book (read by AI · quote checked) · no AI call now"; "Computed from your book · no AI call now" stays for your Pinned birth year with a typed year.
  9. **A who-knows card's mark follows its holders:** your *Knew before the book* marks alone read "From what you marked · no AI call now"; your marks with AI-read lines (or readers-since) read "Read by AI from your book (quote checked) and what you marked · no AI call now".
  10. **A who-knows card with no holder is stored as "the sold map: readers since 1.1"** (or the label alone), never a "Who knows" heading with nothing under it, which would read as "nobody" in the history the next request carries.
- Alternatives: a `notes` kind in `CitationTarget`; passing no quote for a non-prose label; teaching the page editors a passage jump now; leaving the read-first out of the parts; a "for certain" wording in place of drawing the partial lines; drawing a re-checked readers-since dimmed instead of handing over.
- To change it: `src/main/scribe/evidence.ts`, `src/shared/scribe.ts` (1), `src/renderer/features/ai/ScribeAnswer.tsx`, `src/renderer/features/editor/openNotes.ts`, `src/renderer/features/research/openResearch.ts`, `src/renderer/features/search/searchJump.ts` (1–4), `src/main/scribe/navigate.ts`, `src/shared/ipc/contract.ts`, `src/shared/chat.ts`, `src/renderer/features/ai/assistantStore.ts` (`costPartsOf`), `usageFormat.ts` (5), `src/main/scribe/instant.ts` (6, 7), `src/renderer/features/ai/InstantCard.tsx`, `instantCardText.ts` (8–10).

## 2026-10-13 · F-16.24, F-16.25, F-16.26 · P3 slice 5 (the chat's UI): calls made while building it
- Question: Story memory v2's chat now draws what slices 1–4 return: an instant card, Ms Scribe's cited answer with its marks and sources, the navigator's and the check's steps and cost, the Ms Scribe switch and the search misses in Settings, and v2 suggestions. v1 turns keep `QueryAnswer` and their look. Smaller calls:
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **An instant card turn's text holds positive lines only.** The turn's text rides along with later requests as history, so slice 4's "(checked up to 1.2)" (and, with no holder, "No scene shows anyone learning this…") would have reached a prompt (P2b rule 9, P3 rule 5). Now the text is "Who knows the sold map: Orrin: overheard from Tomas (1.1) · readers since 1.1" and the scoped muted line ("No other scene shows anyone learning it (checked up to 14.2)", composed in `knowsView.ts`) is drawn by the card only. This replaces entry 11 of the slice 4 review.
  2. **A computed age and the author's own mark carry their own marks:** "Computed from your book · no AI call now" and "From what you marked · no AI call now"; every AI-read card keeps "Read by AI from your book · quote checked · no AI call now".
  3. **A who-knows card with no holder** shows the sheets' own scoped line ("No scene shows anyone learning this (checked up to 1.2)"); with holders, "No other scene shows anyone learning it (…)". Holders reuse the This scene panel's line (ⓘ, *Not right*; the author's mark as plain words).
  4. **The cost parts**: main reports the quote check's share (`ScribeTurn.checkUsd`), the renderer stores the parts on the turn (`ChatMessage.costParts`, read leniently): navigator, answer, check, each only when it cost anything, a title only for two or more. They are worded with the app's cents format, so "navigator 0.2¢ · answer 1.3¢ · check 0.02¢" rather than the plan's two decimals.
  5. **Sources count paragraphs from 1** for the author ("1.1 ¶1", as ⓘ does) though the prompt numbers them from 0; a source whose reference names its record ("Mara Vell's Ideas ¶1") drops its repeated title. A filed line names its document ("filed from Harbour notes"; `ScribeCitation.doc`). A source with nothing to open (years, ages, the outline, a record gone) is not a button. A Research source opens its page the way a scene does. *(Changed by the slice 5 review, items 1–4: a Research source opens its page with no passage jump, a scene note opens the Notes panel, a record's sheet opens at its top.)*
  6. **A Not found answer shows no separate scope line:** the Not found line already says where it looked and what it searched.
  7. **The folded steps read "Looked up 2 things · checked the quotes"** (or "Checked the quotes" alone), so the check is never counted as a lookup.
  8. **Search misses live under Settings › AI › Story memory › Details**, the count and the five newest (question, scene and paragraph, quote, the words searched); local, never sent.
  9. **v2 suggestions:** four lookups from the character records ("Mara's eyes?", "When does Mara first appear?", "What does Mara know so far?", "What were my ideas for Mara?"); in v2 a question suggestion is gated by Ms Scribe's switch (no `query` toggle there), the routed scene actions by the navigator (not `route`), and "Check this scene for continuity slips" waits for P4.
  10. **A label that says "Ms Scribe" follows the assistant's name** (`nameAssistant` now also names "Ms Scribe’s search" and "Ms Scribe’s quote check", in one pass so a name with "Assistant" in it is never named twice). The Ms Scribe switch and the two data-sharing rows were built in slice 4; this slice adds their tests at the Settings tab.
- Alternatives: keeping "(checked up to …)" in the turn's text and filtering history in main; one cost number with no parts; 0-based paragraph numbers in the chat; Search misses as its own Settings section.
- To change it: `src/renderer/features/ai/instantCardText.ts` and `InstantCard.tsx` (1–3), `src/main/scribe/runScribe.ts` (`checkUsd`), `src/shared/chat.ts` (`CostPart`), `src/renderer/features/ai/assistantStore.ts` (`costPartsOf`), `RequestCost.tsx`, `usageFormat.ts` (4), `ScribeAnswer.tsx`, `scribeAnswerView.ts` (5, 6), `AgentTurn.tsx` (7), `src/renderer/features/memory/SearchMisses.tsx`, `MemorySettings.tsx` (8), `src/shared/assistantSuggestions.ts` (9), `src/shared/assistantName.ts` (10).

## 2026-10-13 · F-16.25, F-16.24, F-5.26 · P3 slice 4 review: calls made fixing the navigator, the read-first and the instant cards
- Question: The review of slice 4 found the fast navigator's turn kind switching Ms Scribe's fact checks off and making an explicit sheet request read-only, a "first" answer losing the earliest passage the search found, a read-first that ignored Stop, skipped the anchors job's pushes and answered past a change it could not read, slots with no value skipping the navigator, tools adding menu items the navigator never saw, short names matching letters inside longer ones, and the instant card's text unmarked as AI-read. These are the calls made fixing them (nothing changes in v1). Still no live run here: the 23 `navigator.v1` cases wait for your `MYTHSCRIBE_EVAL_LIVE=1 OPENROUTER_API_KEY=… npm run eval:ai` run.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed; entry 3 and 4 of the slice 4 entry below are replaced):
  1. **The words decide whether a turn changes anything** (`settleTurn`): a Write this is a draft, an edit or a sheet line asked for in words is an edit (a draft when the navigator says draft); a navigator's edit or draft the words do not ask for is an answer; a Plan turn is never a change turn. The navigator's other kinds (idea, to-do, what-if) still set reasoning and output size. Its `t` never turns the fact checks off: the main-claim, absence and scope rules come from the question's words alone, as before slice 4. A navigator `t` of lookup is an answer; the lookup kind (smaller evidence and output) is Step 0's own handover, and navigator.v1 no longer lists it.
  2. **An explicit sheet request is never read-only:** a navigator `query` whose turn is an edit or a draft runs as `chat`, so Ask and Auto keep write access (decision 3); Plan still writes nothing.
  3. **Only a stale or contested value goes straight to Ms Scribe** (P3-D5). A slot the memory has no value for, or a knows question that is not a yes, goes to the navigator (it searches by meaning), with that slot's evidence on its menu and the read-first's cost carried.
  4. **After a read-first the card shows only if nothing that bears on it is still unread**; a read that left the change unread (a reply the reader could not use) hands over to Ms Scribe as a lookup (E0 has the change). Never a second read.
  5. **The read-first's anchors check is the anchors job's own** (`checkAnchorsNow` in the handlers: its invariant counts, its `memory:changed`, the card's details), so a value the edit made stale dims at once even when the read cannot run.
  6. **Stop stops the read-first:** the turn's id is registered before Step 0's instant path, and a Stop cancels the read running under it and starts nothing more.
  7. **A first/last/ever/never/how-many/knows answer keeps the ordered hits the navigator passed over:** up to 8 scene hits before its first pick (after its last, for a last question; all when it picked none), earliest first (latest first), ahead of its picks so the fit never drops the earliest one.
  8. **Each tool adds at most its cap to the menu** (8; 16 for `state`), skipping what is already there, so every E id is one the navigator was shown; its own paragraphs lead the search-miss baseline, Step 0's after them.
  9. **The navigator's names are whole words:** a tool argument matches a record by its name or nickname, or by whole words of one ("Reyes" is Joanna Reyes; "Ann" is nobody). agent.v8's v2 tools keep the loose match until F-16.31.
  10. **navigator.v1 keeps route.v2's instruction rule word for word** ("the specific request restated … or "" when there is none", as "i"), so a vague rewrite still settles to the quick picks. The eval now scores the turn kind, the chapter, "i" and the decision as `settleRoute` settles it, with four new cases (a vague and a specific rewrite with nothing selected, an explicit sheet request, "save this idea for X"). navigator.v1 was never released, so it changed in place (695 rules tokens, +7 a request).
  11. **The instant card's text carries its label** ("Read by AI from your book · quote checked · no AI call now") and, for who knows with holders, the scope "(checked up to 1.2)"; slice 5 draws the card from the turn's `card`, never this text. *(Replaced by slice 5, entry 1: the turn's text holds positive lines only, since it rides along as history.)*
  12. **`ai:query` is v1-only** too, as `ai:route` is.
- Alternatives: the navigator's `t` deciding the checks; Plan turns keeping the navigator's edit kind; appending the passed-over hits after the picks (the fit drops them first); one navigator search per tool call with no cap; matching any part of a name.
- To change it: `src/main/scribe/runScribe.ts` (`settleTurn`; 1), `src/main/scribe/navigate.ts` (`navigationTurn`, `answerAction`, `instantTurn`, `withOrderedHits`; 1–4, 6, 7), `src/main/ipc/handlers.ts` (`checkAnchorsNow`; 5), `src/main/scribe/tools.ts` (`addUpTo`, `recordByNameV2`; 8, 9), `src/main/ai/prompts/navigator.v1.ts` and `route.v2.ts` (`routeV2InstructionRule`; 10), `src/renderer/features/ai/instantCardText.ts` (11), `src/main/ipc/channelAccess.ts` (12).

## 2026-10-13 · F-16.25, F-16.24, F-5.26 · P3 slice 4 (navigator.v1 replaces route.v2 in v2; instant cards reach the chat): calls made while building it
- Question: In story memory v2 every chat message now goes through `ai:navigate`: a typed action needs no request, a question the memory answers is an instant card, and otherwise `navigator.v1` (fast, at most three steps) picks route.v2's action (F-5.26's edit passes kept) and finds the evidence before `agent.v9` answers. v1 still asks `route.v2`, unchanged. No live run was possible here (no key): the 19 `navigator.v1` cases wait for your `MYTHSCRIBE_EVAL_LIVE=1 OPENROUTER_API_KEY=… npm run eval:ai` run.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **`ai:route` is v1-only** (`CHANNEL_MEMORY`): a v2 project can never send `route.v2`'s prompt, even by a stale renderer.
  2. **No navigation when the navigator cannot help:** with the navigator switched off (Settings › AI, the Ms Scribe group), or a first step that is not one JSON object, the message is answered from Step 0's evidence and the turn's kind from its words, as v1's router off falls back to chat. A later step that fails answers from what was found; a first step that fails, or a stop, ends the turn as before.
  3. **The turn's kind** is the navigator's `t` when it names a known one, else from the words (an edit for a change or a sheet line, an idea turn for an idea question, else an answer). *(Replaced by the slice 4 review: the words decide a change.)*
  4. **A stale or contested value goes to Ms Scribe as a lookup turn** (action `query`) with that slot's claims first, then Step 0's menu, and no navigator request; so does a read-first the reader cannot make now (Use AI off, the reading switch, a pause, a refusal): E0 has the change.
  5. **Before the read-first, the scene's anchors are checked** (the anchors job's own check, run at once): a fact whose sentence the author just changed goes stale before the read, so the read re-checks it (P2a-D23 deletes what it does not revive). Without it the old value went stale only after the read and contested the new one, so "Mara's eyes?" after an edit was handed to Ms Scribe instead of answered (found by the e2e). The same race exists for any read that starts right after a save (Read now clicked at once); only the read-first runs the check here. P4 or a P2a follow-up could run it at the start of every read.
  6. **When the navigator names no evidence** (no `use`, or ids not on the menu), the answer gets the whole menu in rank order; a third step with calls but no `use` runs its calls and does the same.
  7. **What the tools add:** `outline` and `todos` add the outline and to-do lines to the menu (P3-D6); a `read` of a scene sends its paragraphs from the first asked, up to 1,500 tokens, as one menu item; at most two reads per message; `knows` lists positive lines only (rule 5).
  8. **`ai:navigate` takes the caret window** (`focus`: the text before the caret and the selection), as `ai:agent` does, instead of only the selection's opening, so Step 0 and the instant rules see the caret paragraph.
  9. **The instant card's plain text** (until slice 5 draws it) starts with the record's name: "Mara Vell · Eyes: grey (1.1)"; a knows card "Yes: Orrin overheard it from Tomas (1.1)"; who knows "Who knows the sold map: …".
  10. **The Ms Scribe group** in Settings › AI (v2 only) is labelled with the assistant's name (F-7.13) and switches `agent`, `navigator` and `cite` together; in v2 the router and Story Intelligence rows are hidden (their data-sharing rows say the navigator and Ms Scribe answer instead).
- Alternatives: keeping `ai:route` open in v2 and relying on the renderer; a navigator-off message that still sends one router call; a lookup turn through the navigator; running the anchors check at the start of every reading; an empty evidence list when nothing is named; the selection preview only.
- To change it: `src/main/scribe/navigate.ts` (`runNavigate`, `instantTurn`, `navigationTurn`; 2–6), `src/main/scribe/tools.ts` (7), `src/shared/ipc/contract.ts` (`ai:navigate`; 8), `src/main/ipc/channelAccess.ts` (1), `src/renderer/features/ai/instantCardText.ts` (9), `src/shared/aiSettings.ts` (`FEATURE_GROUPS`, `MEMORY_ONLY_FEATURES`; 10).

## 2026-10-13 · F-16.25 · P3 slice 3 review: which answers are asked again, and what the quote check reads
- Question: The review of slice 3 changed when Ms Scribe's answer is asked again and turned into "Not found" (replacing entries 1, 3 and 4 of the slice 3 entry below). Confirm or change these. No live run was possible here: the four new `cite.v1` cases wait for your `MYTHSCRIBE_EVAL_LIVE=1 OPENROUTER_API_KEY=… npm run eval:ai` run.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **Only questions about facts in the book are asked again.** An opinion or critique ("Is Mara likeable here?"), a what-if ("What if Mara never found the map?") or a brainstorm ("How could Mara get off the ship?") is answered as written, its sentences that name someone marked "not checked"; it is never turned into "Not found". Until the navigator (slice 4) names the kind of turn, this is read from the question's words (could, would, should, might, what if, think, likeable, better, brainstorm, ideas for…; a knows or learns question always counts as a fact question).
  2. **A possibility or an opinion is never the claim asked about:** a first sentence with could, might, would, should, perhaps, if…, "comes across as", "I think" is marked "not checked" if uncited, and when the quote check says its quotes do not say it, it is marked, not asked about again.
  3. **In Plan, asking for a change or a sheet line** ("Add to Mara's sheet…", "Tighten Mara's dialogue") is checked as a change: Ms Scribe's reply describing it is shown with "Plan mode writes nothing…", never "Not found".
  4. **"Yes." or "No." on its own is no claim:** the sentence after it is the one checked and, when needed, asked about again (both quoted back).
  5. **"Tomas does not know about the map" is never let stand as a fact** (your rule that absence is never a fact): as the answer, or a bare "No." to a "does … know" question, it is asked again once and then reads "Not found"; later in an answer it is marked unsupported. "I found no scene where Tomas learns it (searched every scene up to 1.3)" stands.
  6. **"Says what was searched"** now means a search word followed by what it covered ("searching every scene up to 14.2", "(searched 1.1–14.2)", "the whole book"); "Mara looked at the map in 2.1" no longer counts.
  7. **An answer that found nothing is checked even when it cites nothing** (replaces entry 4): when the search found passages, one quote check (about 0.02¢) asks whether any of them shows what the answer says was not found; if one does, the answer is asked again, then reads "Not found".
  8. **A retry that fails** (a rate limit, the daily cap) ends "Not found in the manuscript (I looked in …)" and is noted in the developer tools, instead of losing the whole turn.
  9. **The quote check reads labels as Ms Scribe does:** "later in the book" happens after now (the check is told which scene is now), and your notes, source documents and filed lines say who or what things are but never that an event happened. `cite.v1` was changed before it shipped (it is not on main), not given a new version number.
- Alternatives: Ms Scribe marking her own answer as opinion or idea in its reply (a new `agent.v9` field and live run); retrying every uncited first sentence (slice 3 as built); leaving "does not know" marked only; no check for uncited absence answers; a new `cite.v2`.
- To change it: `src/shared/scribeAnswer.ts` (`asksForFact`, `isHedged`, `bareAnswer`, `mainClaimAt`, `statesAbsenceAsFact`, `statesScope`; 1, 2, 4–6), `src/main/scribe/runScribe.ts` (`checkAnswer`, the retry; 3, 5, 7, 8), `src/main/ai/prompts/cite.v1.ts` (9), `src/main/ai/prompts/agent.v9.ts` (`UNSUPPORTED_REASONS.absence`).

## 2026-10-13 · F-16.25 · P3 slice 3 (cite.v1, the scope rule, the one retry, Not found): calls made while building it
- Question: Ms Scribe now checks her own quotes (story memory v2 only; v1's chat is unchanged). These calls go beyond the plan's P3-D13, P3-D14 and P3-D21. No live run was possible here (no key): the six new `cite.v1` cases and `agent.v9`'s `retry-unsupported` case wait for your `MYTHSCRIBE_EVAL_LIVE=1 OPENROUTER_API_KEY=… npm run eval:ai` run.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **Which first sentences are asked about again:** only a claim about the book, one that names a record or a scene (P3-D13's rule), with no kept citation. A first sentence that names nothing ("She left early.") stays marked "not checked" and is never turned into "Not found": a brainstorm answer would otherwise always end Not found.
  2. **"I found no scene where …" stands without a citation** (P3 rule 5's own wording): it is not asked about again, but the answer must say what was searched, or it is asked again once.
  3. **Saying what was searched** is required of first, last, ever, never and how-many answers that make a claim, and of answers that found nothing. A plain "does Orrin know…" yes, or "What did Orrin hear?" (a learns word), need not say it; their search still covers every earlier scene.
  4. **No check when nothing was cited**, even for an answer that found nothing: its scope is code's alone, unchecked against the passages the search found. The live run will show whether such answers are wrong often enough to check them anyway (one call, about 0.02¢).
  5. **The scope item lists up to five passages before the cited paragraph** in reading order, the earliest first (for a last or latest answer, the ones after it, the latest first), each as the scene reads now around the search words. With nothing to list, no scope item is sent.
  6. **A quote's sentence either side** is read across a scene's paragraphs (a one-sentence paragraph of dialogue gets its neighbours); for a fact, a page or who knows what, within its own passage only.
  7. **"Quote and sentence checked"** is shown only when every sentence that used the quote was found "yes"; "partly", no verdict, or a failed check leave "quote checked".
  8. **A failed check never costs the answer:** a provider error or the cap leaves the citations quote-checked; the Quote check switch (Settings › AI, its own row until slice 4's Ms Scribe switch) turns the check off the same way, while code's checks and the one retry stay.
  9. **When the retry cannot run** (the turn is over its cost cap) or comes back cut off or asking for a chapter, the answer is "Not found in the manuscript (I looked in …)", never the unsupported claim.
  10. **"I looked in …" names the scenes the answers cited first,** then the scenes of the evidence sent, then the search.
  11. **The retry turn** quotes the first sentence back (300 characters at most) with the reason and asks for an answer from the evidence or `found: false`; the prompt before it is unchanged, so the cached prefix holds. The fit keeps room for it, so the maxed `agent.v9` case moved from 23,947 to 23,796 tokens.
  12. **A narrower search widened:** when the navigator (slice 4) searched less than every scene up to now for a first/last/ever/never or knows question, Step 0's every-earlier-scene search (already run) is added, its scenes after the navigator's picks, and the chat shows "Searching every scene up to … for …".
- Alternatives: retrying any uncited first sentence (the plan's literal reading); checking absence answers with no citation; asking every ordinal and knows answer for its scope; the scope item's passages nearest the cited scene first; sentences ±1 within one paragraph only; "cite" when any sentence said yes; the unsupported claim shown, marked, when no retry is possible.
- To change it: `src/main/scribe/runScribe.ts` (`checkAnswer`, the retry; 1–3, 8–10), `src/main/scribe/checks.ts` (`enforceScope`, `scopeHits`, `citedSentences`, `applyCite`, `citationLevels`; 4–7, 12), `src/shared/scribeAnswer.ts` (`isAbsence`, `statesScope`, `quoteContext`), `src/main/ai/prompts/agent.v9.ts` (`agentV9UnsupportedTurn`; 11), `src/main/ai/prompts/cite.v1.ts` (the check's caps).

## 2026-10-13 · F-16.25, F-16.26 · P3 slice 2 review: what changed in Ms Scribe's answer call and sheet lines
- Question: The review of slice 2 changed some of the calls below (entries 6 and 8 among them). These are the new ones; confirm or change them.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **A sheet line only when you ask for one:** a line Ms Scribe proposes is offered only when your message asks to put something on a sheet (add, put, pin, save, note, keep, jot, file or log, with a sheet, an idea or a record named). Otherwise nothing is offered and she says "I put nothing on a sheet; ask me to put it on one if you want it there." When your message names records, the line goes on one of them only ("You did not name Tomas, so I put nothing on Tomas's sheet."). A reply of "Yes" to her own offer is not such a request: ask again in words.
  2. **A name must be a record's whole name or nickname:** "Rin" is not Orrin and "Ann" is not Joanna; a name matched in its exact case (Will) must be written so; a name two records share goes to the one whose own name it is, else nowhere ("I could not tell whose sheet that goes on").
  3. **What a quote must be:** 3 to 25 words copied from the book's own words in the passage: a scene paragraph (never its "¶4" number), a fact's paragraph as it reads now (never the "Mara · eyes: grey" line the app wrote above it, so a stale value cannot pass as quoted), who knows what's quotes (never its lines). A shorter quote, or one running across two paragraphs, is not kept and its sentence is marked "not checked".
  4. **"Not checked" on edit turns** (replaces entry 6 there): a sentence that names a record or a scene is marked unless it describes her own change ("I added a beat…"); the first-sentence rule stays for answers only, and advice or a question back to you is never the main claim.
  5. **Plan mode says it wrote nothing:** when a Plan reply still carries a sheet line or a change, the answer adds "Plan mode writes nothing; switch to Ask or Auto to put it on the sheet."
  6. **"Not found" answers questions only:** an edit or "Write this" turn that sets found to false keeps its own words (or "Here is the change.").
  7. **Typing on a sheet while a line lands:** what you type on that Pinned or Ideas page during the write is saved first and the line written again after it (three tries; then the line is given up, never your words). Undo finds its line by its words and the turn's mark, so two lines of one turn undo in either order.
  8. **A long scene asked for whole:** a chapter that is one scene over 8,000 tokens is read in part, around the paragraph the search found (else from its start); a need that names only the open scene, or nothing that fits, ends "Not found…" without a second call.
  9. **A paragraph over 12,000 tokens alone** (imported text with no breaks) goes as its opening and its end with "(¶n shortened: its middle not shown)".
  10. **"Does Tomas know about Orrin's brother?"** (a knower and what is known, no secret named) searches every earlier scene for the words, as a secret question does, so "I found no scene where…" has a scope code searched; "What do we know about…" does not.
  11. **The cost registry's chat row** prices story memory v1's agent.v8 (every project until the switch-over); v2's one call is priced in the story-memory tasks.
- Alternatives: pins on any write turn (the slice as built); a partial-name match; quotes checked against the whole item as sent; no marks on edit turns; a silent Plan; the Not found line on every turn; reloading the sheet over typing; skipping an over-long scene; the registry pricing agent.v9's fixtures.
- To change it: `src/main/scribe/runScribe.ts` (1–6, 8), `src/main/scribe/evidence.ts` and `src/shared/scribeAnswer.ts` (3), `src/renderer/features/ai/agentApply.ts` and `src/main/scribe/sheetLines.ts` (7), `src/main/scribe/selfRoute.ts` (8), `src/main/ai/prompts/agent.v9.ts` (9), `src/main/scribe/step0.ts` (10), `src/main/ai/eval/costRegistry.ts` (11).

## 2026-10-13 · F-16.25, F-16.26 · P3 slice 2 (agent.v9, the one answer call; code quote checks; Self-Route and search misses; sheet writes): calls made while building it
- Question: Wiring Ms Scribe's answer call into the chat (story memory v2 only; v1's chat is unchanged) needed these calls beyond the plan's P3-D1 to P3-D23. No live run was possible here (no key): the 14 new `agent.v9` eval cases wait for your `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` run.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **Self-Route is in this slice** (the plan allowed moving it to slice 3): "I need chapter 9" reads that chapter (shown as "Reading chapter 9…") and asks once more; a second ask, or a chapter or scene that does not exist, ends "Not found in the manuscript (I looked in …)". The cost cap is checked before the second call.
  2. **The cost of a widened question is about 3.8¢, not 3¢:** the call that asks to widen is counted now (`agent.v9:need`, P3-D22), which the design's Part 5 left out; the other sizes stay the design's. The registry's "about 3 calls per use" for the chat is unchanged.
  3. **Scene ids go to the AI only when it may edit** (Ask and Auto): the open scene reads "id n5 in n4" and each scene-opening passage its own id, so "move this scene after 1.3" resolves; a Plan question never sees ids.
  4. **What counts as a search miss** before the navigator exists (slice 4): a kept citation into the widened chapter at a paragraph Step 0's own word search did not return. A question with no words to search for logs no miss.
  5. **When Ms Scribe answers "found": false**, the chat shows the app's own Not found line with what it looked in, not her wording (her wording could claim an absence the scope does not support).
  6. **"Not checked" marks are for answers only:** on an edit or "Write this" turn the reply describes the change, so nothing in it is marked; on an answer, the first sentence without a kept citation is marked too (P3-D14; slice 3 retries it).
  7. **Which turns think:** a Plan question is always an answer; in Ask or Auto a "Write this" choice is a draft, a message that opens with an edit verb or asks for a sheet line is an edit (reasoning low, the voice profile sent), and everything else an answer (reasoning off).
  8. **Sheet lines she cannot place** never become a question you have to answer: an unknown name ("make the record first"), a secret or thread ("has no sheet") or a line the page already holds ("already says that") is said in the answer and nothing is offered. "Is this an idea or canon?" waits in the chat even in Auto; Apply all skips it.
  9. **A Pinned line that contradicts the sheet** (Eyes: green under Eyes: grey) still goes on, and the chat says "It contradicts “Eyes: grey” on the sheet; both lines are there now."
  10. **The line's mark** reads "Written from the chat, 13 Oct" beside it on the sheet, and its chat turn is the request's id.
  11. **The chat history** goes as earlier user turns, each starting "[earlier turn, not a source]", so the cached opening never changes between questions.
  12. **A long open scene** names the left-out paragraphs by their numbers as sent (¶0 is the first): "(¶0–14 and ¶61–79 not shown)".
- Alternatives: Self-Route in slice 3 with a Not found reply meanwhile; the design's 3¢ widened figure; ids always sent; the model's own not-found wording; marks on edit turns too; a pin of an unknown name asking to make the record.
- To change it: `src/main/scribe/runScribe.ts` (1, 4–8), `src/main/scribe/selfRoute.ts` (1), `src/shared/aiCostNotes.ts` and `src/main/ai/eval/report.test.ts` (2), `src/main/scribe/evidence.ts` and `src/main/ai/prompts/agent.v9.ts` (3, 11, 12), `src/renderer/features/ai/agentApply.ts` (9), `src/renderer/features/editor/filedFrom.ts` (10).

## 2026-10-13 · F-16.24–16.26 · P3 slice 1 (Step 0, instant rules, evidence, sheet lines, search misses, reasoning per request): calls made while building it
- Question: Building the local half of Ms Scribe (no prompt, no UI, nothing wired into the chat yet) needed these calls beyond the plan's P3-D1 to P3-D23. Nothing changes in v1; no request changed (token report unchanged).
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **"Does Tomas know?" when you marked him *Knew before the book*:** an instant yes, with no quote (the card says it came from you, `source: 'author'`); slice 5 must not label it "Read by AI from your book · quote checked".
  2. **"Who knows X?" when nobody is shown knowing it and nothing is checked yet** goes to Ms Scribe rather than a card that only says "not checked yet". Readers-since shows on the card only once readers know it as of the open scene, so a later reveal is never shown early.
  3. **A stale line of any kind counts against a card** (P3-D3 says "any claim of the record and slot"), even a dialogue line; a value later in the book never does.
  4. **Read first** looks at the open scene even if it was never read, and at the two latest earlier scenes the reader left unsettled; an earlier scene never read at all is not read first (the question goes on to the card or to Ms Scribe). For "does A know X" and "who knows X" the cue is any word of the secret's label that is not a name.
  5. **Questions with "learn", "find out" or "heard"**, and "know" questions that name a secret, take the every-earlier-scene search too (P3-D8 names knows questions); "What do we know about …" is an ordinary search (changed in review). That search reads the manuscript's own scenes, with the best six of Pinned, Ideas, notes and Research beside them (ahead of them for an idea question).
  6. **Labels:** your Pinned lines and a Research page you wrote read "your notes"; an uploaded Research page "source document"; scene notes "planned"; a scene left out of compile "cut from the book"; the outline (chapters and scenes with references, at most 80 lines) and years and ages "computed from your book", both citable. One rule labels everything (`labelOf`, which the evidence now calls); a Not canon mark wins over notes, Pinned, Research and plans.
  7. **Sheet lines** go only on a person, place, thing, group or term (never a secret's or thread's sheet: VALIDATION); "already says that" compares against the whole page (Pinned or Ideas, Not canon lines included); a line is at most 600 characters (the filed-line limit); the mark keeps the day (2026-10-13).
  8. **A question with a knows word** ("know", "heard") is a knows question or nothing: it never gets a slot card. A secret is named by its label or by at least half its label's words, one of them not a name. The record's own name never counts as a slot cue ("Hope's age" when a detail is called hope).
  9. **"Save it" after "brainstorm"** counts as a request to put something on a sheet (it goes to Ideas in slice 2), as "pin" and "add … to Mara's sheet" do.
  10. **E0 only for a scene you have open** (a chapter opens its last scene); with nothing open, or a Research page or a sheet open, there is no E0 and now is the latest written scene.
  11. **Reasoning per request on a direct OpenAI key:** low asks for `reasoning_effort: low` where the model takes one (new `lowEffort`); a request's own reasoning is part of its cache key (a request without it keeps the key it had).
  12. **Where the per-version input cap lives:** `PROMPT_INPUT_BUDGETS` and `promptInputBudget` are in `src/main/ai/prompts/catalogue.ts` (the plan said `src/shared/ai.ts`, but prompt versions are main's), read by the request path and the token report; empty until `agent.v9`.
  13. **Speed:** to keep Step 0 under 50 ms with three records named, the sheets it digests share one read of who knows what, threads and years (`sheetFrame`), a sheet skips the mention lookup when the book has no thread steps, the passage search takes the caller's nodes, and a first / ever question places every match before making snippets for the 20 it keeps (from their text, not FTS's own snippets, which re-read every match). About 29 ms on the 90-scene book (the timeViews perf test's book, now `perfFixture.ts`); after the review's ranked search (19), about 25 ms for a plain question and 40 ms for a first / ever one, where every paragraph of that book matches (the search reads the matches' places from FTS5's own content table, never their text).
  14. **History:** a "turn" is one message, so the newest two messages go whole (up to 400 tokens each).
  15. **A search miss goes with its scene** (deleting the scene deletes its misses).
  Review fixes (2026-10-13, same slice):
  16. **Instant cards answer only questions about now.** No card for first, last, ever, never or how many (they search every earlier scene), a scene or chapter ("in 1.1", "in chapter 2"), another time (before, after, then, at the start, "when the bell rang"; "When was Mara born?" still gets one), a negation ("Who doesn't know…"), or an edit or sheet request (an edit verb anywhere: fix, rewrite, polish, cut, remove, set …, so F-5.26's passes keep "Polish the paragraph about Mara's eyes"). The detail must belong to the person named: "Mara's eyes", "How old is Mara now", "the age of Orrin", but not "Mara's mother's eyes", "Mara's sword" or "Orrin's daughter"; "Does Orrin's sister know…" gets none; a secret named by only half its label's words gets none ("Does Orrin know where the map is?").
  17. **Read first, where someone is:** any unread finished sentence naming them (also for what they wear, carry and how they are hurt), since prose moves people without saying "where" ("Mara rode north to Vell"); for other details a sentence with the detail's own words in prose ("died", "dyed", "promoted"); the same words find a Pinned line such as "Home: Vell" for a lookup. The paragraph at the caret that has no closing punctuation yet is never a reason to read.
  18. **"How old is Mara?"** answers from the age as of now: a birth year and the scene's story year give a card of its own, "computed from your book" (new card kind `age`); a stated age gets a card only while no time has passed since it was stated, otherwise Ms Scribe answers with the timeline; "not born yet" and a birth year with no story year go to Ms Scribe. A digest shows the age once, never "ten (since 1.1)" beside it.
  19. **The every-earlier-scene search** looks for the question's own words, not the names, the cue words or words that only ask where something is (appear, mentioned, seen, meet, said); it ranks each paragraph by how many of them it holds, then by the people it names (by every name they have), keeps each scene's best paragraph, and keeps the best 20 scenes, the earliest first (the latest first for a "last" question), shown in reading order. With no such word it ranks by the names. When the cap cuts matching scenes, the scope claims only the stretch whose every match is in the menu, never "every scene up to now"; with nothing to search for there is no scope at all. A cut match still counts as found (never a search miss later).
  20. **Edit and sheet requests are read from how the message opens:** "Fix …", "Please polish …", "Can you rewrite …", "Great, now cut …"; "Who cut the rope?" is a question. A sheet line needs a sheet word (sheet, Pinned, Ideas, an idea) or a person: "to/on Mara", "Mara's", or "for Mara" after save, pin, note or keep; a chapter or scene is never a sheet ("Add a scene to Chapter 3"), and "the pin Mara lost" is no request.
  21. **Half-knowing in a digest** reads "Suspects the sold map (1.2)" or "Believes otherwise about …", never "Knows".
  22. **A claim's line in the evidence** says who says it and how: "Orrin says: Mara · eyes: green", "In a dream: Mara · alive: dead", "Remembered: …"; plain narration has no tag.
  23. **A sheet line that contradicts a Pinned line** (same label, another value: "Eyes: green" under "Eyes: grey") is still added, since you asked, and the result names the line it contradicts so the confirmation can say so; Ideas lines may disagree with each other.
  24. **Evidence over budget:** an item too long for the budget is left out and the ones after it still go in.
  25. **E0 carries its label**, so an Idea scene or a cut scene open is never sent as what happened.
  26. **The Step 0 speed test takes the fastest of five warm calls** (as the time views' test does), not the median: with the whole test suite running beside it the median measured the other test workers (it failed even for a plain question whose cost did not change). The budget stays 50 ms.
- Alternatives: no card for an author's mark (always Ms Scribe); a "not checked yet" card; only narration-now stale lines counting; reading never-read earlier scenes first; a sheet line on a secret's sheet; Ideas lines allowed to repeat; cache keys without the reasoning.
- To change it: `src/main/scribe/instant.ts` (1–4, 17, 18), `src/main/scribe/step0.ts` (5, 10, 13, 19, 21, 25), `src/main/memory/passageIndexer.ts` `searchMemoryPassagesInOrder` (19), `step0.perf.test.ts` (26), `src/shared/scribe.ts` `labelOf` (6), `src/main/scribe/evidence.ts` (22, 24), `src/main/scribe/sheetLines.ts` (7, 23), `src/shared/memory/askParse.ts` (8, 9, 16, 17's words, 19's words, 20), `src/main/ai/request.ts` and `providers/openai.ts` (11), `catalogue.ts` (12), `src/shared/scribeAnswer.ts` `scribeHistory` (14), migration 0037's foreign key (15).

## 2026-10-13 · F-16.24–16.26 · P3 plan: the calls the design left open
- Question: Planning P3, Ms Scribe on the new memory (`docs/research/story-memory-2026-10-11/plans/P3.md`, six slices, nothing built yet), needed calls the design did not make. Details are in the plan's §8 (P3-D1 to P3-D23).
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **Instant cards answer only short, plain questions** (at most 12 words) about one person, place or thing and one detail from a fixed list ("Mara's eyes?", "How old is Mara?", "Where is Tomas?"), "Does Orrin know about the sold map?" (only when the answer is yes) and "Who knows about the sold map?". A question asking why, what if, or to change or save something always goes to Ms Scribe, and so do traits, history and family ties.
  2. **A card steps aside** when the value is stale (you edited its sentence and it has not been re-read) or the book gives two values that disagree; a value stated later in the book does not count against it. Contradictions found by P4 will be added to this test.
  3. **If you just changed a sentence about it,** the app reads that change first (about 0.1¢) before showing the card, but only in the open scene and at most two others waiting to be read. If reading is off or paused, the question goes to Ms Scribe, who reads the whole open scene anyway.
  4. **A lookup that needs Ms Scribe skips the search step** and goes straight to her with that detail's lines from the book (about 0.8¢).
  5. **The new search step also routes the chat,** with the same choices and words as today: "make this scene better" still asks what to focus on with the five quick picks, and a specific request still runs its edit pass. With it switched off, Ms Scribe answers from a local word search, as the chat answered without the router before.
  6. **Two extra things the search can look at:** the outline (for "move 7.4 after 7.6") and your to-dos, which the design's list left out.
  7. **"First", "last", "ever", "never" and "who knows" answers** search every scene up to where you are (or the whole book if you ask), and say what they searched. The "checked up to" line from P2b still never goes to the AI.
  8. **The open scene goes in whole** up to about 9,000 words; a longer scene sends the 9,000 or so around your caret and says which paragraphs were left out.
  9. **Thinking:** off for questions, lookups, ideas and to-dos; low for edits, drafting and what-ifs.
  10. **The chat history Ms Scribe sees:** the last two turns whole, older ones cut to their first line, about 1,500 tokens at most, and never used as a source.
  11. **What must carry a source:** a sentence naming a person, place, thing or scene. Without one it is marked "not checked". The answer's first sentence is the claim that must hold; if it does not, she tries once more, then says "Not found in the manuscript (I looked in …)".
  12. **Asking for more:** once per question, up to about 8,000 tokens of one chapter or scene. A passage found that way that the search missed is logged as a search miss (the newest 500 are kept); Settings shows the count. That count decides whether meaning search with embeddings (F-16.35) is ever built.
  13. **Sheet lines from the chat:** only for a record that exists (the chat never creates records), and never a line the sheet already has; either case is said in the answer. In Auto the line lands with a confirmation and *Undo*; in Ask, *Apply* is the one click; "Is this an idea or canon?" waits for your pick in both; Plan never writes. *Undo* works while the line still reads as written.
  14. **Settings** gets one **Ms Scribe** switch for her three parts (the answer, the search and the quote check) in story memory v2; the old router and Story Intelligence switches are hidden there.
  15. **Cost:** a question that asks for more costs about 3.8¢ rather than the design's 3¢ (the design did not count the call that asks); the average open question stays about 2¢.
  16. **The live test** (needs your key, after the build): 24 questions over the test book, with bars of at least 90 % right routing, 80 % right answers, 90 % valid citations, every "first/never" answer stating a full scope, every unanswerable question ending "not found", no "doesn't know" stated as fact, ideas always labelled, and an open question at most 2.5¢. `MYTHSCRIBE_EVAL_LIVE=1 OPENROUTER_API_KEY=sk-or-… npm run eval:ai -- scribe` (stops at $1; about 40¢).
- Alternatives: instant cards for any question that names a slot; a card even when values disagree, showing both; always run the search step for lookups; keep the router and add the search after it (two fast calls per message); let the chat create a record when asked to pin to one that does not exist; no cap on the open scene.
- To change it: `plans/P3.md` §8, before the slice that builds it.

## 2026-10-13 · F-16.22, F-16.23 · P2b slice 5 review: calls made fixing threads, the timeline and ages
- Question: The review of slice 5 found the Timeline and every character sheet re-reading the whole book's text in Electron main after each pause in typing (100–230 ms on a 90-scene book), the elapsed hint shown in the reader's words as if quoted, a known birth year reported as "no birth year", a thread its reveal stands for called "not yet in the book", the Timeline and the Threads not following a reorder or a Pinned "Born:" line, the *Ages of* choice lost on every row click, and AI-read years with no ⓘ. These are the calls made fixing them (nothing changes in v1; no prompt, no migration).
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **Cost.** A scene's text is read only when its card has a field the view needs (a year, a flashback or a hint; for Here a POV or someone on the page); only the paragraph a quote sits in is hashed; a scene's paragraphs and its card's standing are kept until its content or card changes. Measured on a 90-scene, ~117k-word book: the years, the Timeline and a character's sheet went from about 220 ms each to about 10 ms (`timeViews.perf.test.ts` fails above 50 ms). The anchors job now says "the card moved" only when what of the card stands on the text changed, so a pause in typing that leaves it as it stood reads nothing again. Age stays inside the sheet's one read (no separate channel): at 10 ms it costs less than a second round trip.
  2. **An elapsed hint is shown in the book's words**: the reader's value where its quote holds it, in any case ("Three winters later"), else the whole quote ("Three winters later, Mara found the heir"); never the reader's own words. It ends the year carry when either its value or its quote can cross a year, so a vague value ("some time") over "Three winters later" cannot keep a false carry.
  3. **A birth year with no year for the scene** reads "born 1792 · this scene’s year not known since “A year later” (1.3)" (or "· no story year known as of this scene" before the first typed year), with the born line's ⓘ; the Timeline shows nothing more there (its row already says the year is not known). With neither a birth year nor a stated age the line is scoped: "No birth year or stated age read (checked up to 1.2)".
  4. **A stated age lists the later years too**: "19, stated in 1.2 · since then: “Three winters later” (1.3), 1820 (1.5)", each typed or quoted year that differs from the one before it, never added up. The test book's label for Clara as of h09 now lists h08 (its typed 1812) beside h09.
  5. **A thread before a step that counts** reads "revealed in 1.1 · no opening step read yet" (or "· none of its steps counts yet" beside muted steps, "· its first step comes later"), and only a thread with no reveal and no step reads "not opened (checked up to 1.2)" with "No scene shows this thread yet (checked up to 1.2)" under it. An empty list reads "No plot thread found (checked up to 1.2)". "not yet in the book" is gone.
  6. **The Timeline's rows have ⓘ** when their year rests on the card: the year the text states, the flashback, the hint, each with its quote, *Go there*, how it was checked and *Not right* (the card field's, P2a slice 4).
  7. **The Book reads again when the book's order moves**: main sends `memory:changed` (naming the scenes, no record) after a scene is made, renamed, copied, moved or deleted, an Idea flips or a compile exclusion changes, and after a save that changes a record's Pinned "Born:" lines (naming the record). A name-index change reads the sheets with Threads again.
  8. **The *Ages of* choice lives in the memory store**, so clicking a row (which opens its scene and remounts the Book tab) keeps it; it resets when the project closes.
  9. **A first-person step is the POV's**: a sheet's Threads also lists a step whose quote says "I" (or "we") in the narration, or a thought with no speaker, of a scene whose POV is the record (the card's verified POV standing on the text, else the typed one), and the record's own first-person dialogue or thought.
  10. **Changed by the author 2026-10-14: add the switch; the author's setting wins over the AI's guess.** (Was: not built, a Flashback scene detail you can type; only a verified card marked a flashback, so with Use AI off a year typed on a flashback scene carried into the scenes after it.) Built 2026-10-14: see "Flashback switch (2026-10-14)" above.
- Alternatives: a separate `memory:age` channel; a stored per-field "stands" flag written by the anchors job; reloading on the renderer's tree store instead of main's events; showing the reader's value beside the quote; adding the Flashback switch now.
- To change it: `src/main/memory/reading/timeViews.ts`, `readingFrame.ts` (`rowTexts`, `heldCard`), `src/shared/memory/reproof.ts` (`cardStanding`), `anchors.ts` (1, 9); `src/shared/memory/years.ts` (`hintWords`, `changesSince`, `ageAsOf`) (2–4); `memory/timeline/timeView.ts`, `TimelineView.tsx`, `records/AgeSection.tsx` (3, 4, 6, 8); `memory/threads/threadsView.ts`, `ThreadsList.tsx` (5); the `tree:*`, `sceneMeta:set`, `compileState:set` and record page handlers in `src/main/ipc/handlers.ts`, `memoryStore.ts` (7, 8); a migration, `sceneDetails.ts`, `SceneDetailsFields.tsx` and `storyYears` for (10).

## 2026-10-13 · F-16.22, F-16.23 · P2b slice 5 (threads, the timeline and ages; the final verification): calls made while building it
- Question: Showing the threads, the story years and ages (the Book tab's Threads and Timeline, a character's Age, a named record's Threads, a thread's sheet, the sidebar's Threads) needed these calls. Nothing changes in v1, no prompt changed, no migration.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **Not ticked yet.** This run's instructions said not to tick `FEATURES.md` boxes, so F-16.22 and F-16.23 stay open, as P2a's did after its final verification (the ticks followed its last review). Both entries name the checks owed in a development build and the live runs owed before the switch-over. (Ticked after the slice 5 review.)
  2. **A thread's status** reads "open since 2.1 · last 7.3" ("open since 2.1" while nothing came after the opening), "resolved in 20.1", or "not yet in the book" (also when, as of the picked scene, its opening is still to come; changed in the review, item 5 there). A thread with no step as of the scene says, muted and scoped, "No scene shows this thread yet (checked up to 14.2)", or "Its first step comes later in the book." when one does.
  3. **Threads are listed in story order**: where each opened, else where it was revealed; yours with neither come last, by name. An AI-made thread no readable scene of the active draft holds any more is in no view (as a secret no scene reveals is in no grid row); the sidebar's Threads list still names it with "not in the book now (another draft, or an Idea or left-out scene)", so you can open or delete it.
  4. **A step's line is "advances 7.3: Johanna writes to Ashby"** with ⓘ and *Not right* (only *Wrong value*: a step is about its thread, so *Not about* is not offered). A remembered, rumoured, supposed or dreamt step is muted with why ("rumoured") and moves no status.
  5. **The Timeline** lists every readable scene with its year in words ("1811", "1811 · from 1.1", "1812 · stated here", "flashback · 1798", "year not known since “A year later” (18.1)"); a scene's own year-ending hint shows only inside its year, any other hint the card quotes ("“A week later” (1.2)") on its own line; the typed time; and the ages of the characters Here (the POV, those on the page, *+ also here*; groups and places have no age). *Ages of [character ▾]* shows that one character in every row. A row opens its scene.
  6. **Quoted words use curly quotes** (“Three winters later”) where the plan wrote straight ones, as every other quoted line in the panel does.
  7. **A character's Age, as of [scene ▾]**: "about 19 · year from 1.1" with the line it counts from ("Born: 1792", 1.1, with ⓘ and *Not right*; or "Born: 1792 (your Pinned line)"), or "19, stated in 1.2 · since then: “Three winters later” (1.3)" with the stated line ("Age: 19", 1.2, with ⓘ). A stated age shows the reader's own words ("two-and-twenty"). With neither, the section says "No birth year or stated age as of this scene." instead of vanishing (the plan said "nothing"), so its scene picker stays reachable (scoped, and a birth year alone shown, since the review, items 3 and 4 there).
  8. **A sheet's Threads, as of [scene ▾]** is on every named record (characters, places, things, groups, terms), not only characters: each thread with a step whose paragraph names it for certain, with the thread's own status and only those steps. With none, the scoped "No scene shows a thread step naming Mara (checked up to 1.2)". A first-person step by the POV does not name her and is not listed (listed since the review, item 9 there).
  9. **A thread's sheet** has **The thread** in place of From the book: its status, ⓘ (its reveals with *Split*, *Not a thread* when the AI made it; "You made this thread." for yours), and its steps.
  10. **One reading frame**: `readingOrder`, `heldCard` and `hereChips` moved from `views.ts` to `readingFrame.ts` so the new views and the old ones never import each other (no behaviour change).
- Alternatives: hiding Age when no age is known (the picker goes with it); Threads on character sheets only; listing threads open first, then not yet, then resolved; showing the hint twice on its own row.
- To change it: `src/renderer/features/memory/threads/threadsView.ts` (2, 4 wording), `ThreadsList.tsx` (4), `src/main/memory/reading/timeViews.ts` `storedThreads` (3) and `sheetTime` (8), `memory/timeline/timeView.ts` and `TimelineView.tsx` (5, 6), `records/AgeSection.tsx` (7), `records/ThreadsSection.tsx` (8), `records/TopicSection.tsx` (9), `readingFrame.ts` (10); the ticks in `FEATURES.md` (1).

## 2026-10-13 · F-16.22 · P2b slice 4 review: calls made fixing who knows what in the panel, the grid and the sheets
- Question: The review of slice 4 found This scene's Who knows what going stale after another scene was read, places and threads *+ also here* treated as learners, the sheets stating an absence without "checked up to", ⓘ saying an AI judged a merge you made, *Undo* of *Not a secret* failing or losing a topic, and a thought turning into its knower's own once its thinker was deleted. These are the calls made fixing them (nothing changes in v1).
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **This scene is read again after any change to story memory**, not only one that names it: another scene's reading can add a secret no one Here has a line about, or move "checked up to", "+ N more" and the tension lines. A sheet is read again when the change names it, or, for a Knows or *The secret*, when any scene moved.
  2. **Only characters and groups Here can learn a secret** in the panel: a place or thing *+ also here* gets no "No scene shows … learning this" line and is never the one a tension line says has none; a place typed as the POV gives no "Readers know …" line. *+ also here* no longer offers secrets or threads.
  3. **The sheets' empty states are scoped too**: "No scene shows Mara learning a secret (checked up to 1.2)" on a character's Knows; "No scene shows anyone learning this (checked up to 1.2)" and "No scene reveals it yet (not checked yet)" on a secret's sheet.
  4. **ⓘ on a merged reveal says "also read as “the map sale” in 1.3: merged into this secret"** (it said "judged the same by a second AI read"): your own *Same as…* of two secrets leaves the same kind of merged reveal, and nothing stored says who merged it. *Split* of a reveal you merged still makes an AI-made secret (offering *Not a secret*). Telling the two apart needs a new column; say if you want it.
  5. **A secret's sheet shows "Category: Secrets" as text**: the select showed "Characters" because Secrets is in no picker.
  6. **The sidebar's Secrets list notes an AI-made secret no readable scene of the active draft reveals** ("not in the book now (another draft, or an Idea or left-out scene)") instead of hiding it, so you can still open or delete it; the reader and the grid already leave it out.
  7. **Undo of *Not a secret* is all or nothing**: the Dismissed row goes only if everything comes back. A scene deleted meanwhile no longer blocks it (the rows that named the scene keep no scene, as deleting a scene does anyway). When one sentence revealed two secrets and both were said not to be secrets, their one Dismissed row reads "Not a secret: the sold map; Not a secret: the stranger", and *Undo* on it brings both back.
  8. **A thought whose thinker is deleted goes with the thinker** ("Orrin knew, Lise thought" is Lise's belief; with Lise gone it would have read as Orrin's own and counted), and *Undo* (of *Not a secret* or *Not right*) never brings such a thought back without its thinker.
- Alternatives: reloading only scenes after the one read; hiding AI secrets not in the book; a `merged_by` column so ⓘ can say "you said these are the same"; keying each topic's *Not a secret* row apart from the shared sentence (two rows, one per topic, but only one of them would keep the sentence from coming back).
- To change it: `src/renderer/features/memory/memoryStore.ts` (1), `src/main/memory/reading/views.ts` `sceneMemory` and `editor/SceneDetailsFields.tsx` (2), `memory/knows/knowsView.ts`, `records/KnowsSection.tsx`, `TopicSection.tsx` (3, 4), `records/RecordSheet.tsx` (5), `records/RecordTab.tsx` `SecretList` (6), `src/main/memory/reading/topicActions.ts`, `dismissedStore.ts` `relabelDismissed`, the `dismissed:undo` handler (7), `recordEvidence.ts` `deleteThoughtsBy`/`knowsThinker` (8).

## 2026-10-13 · F-16.22 · P2b slice 4 (who knows what in the panel, the Book grid and the sheets): calls made while building it
- Question: Showing who knows what (This scene, the Book tab's grid, the sheets, the sidebar's Secrets, Rebuild memory's summary) and the answers on secrets (*Knew before the book*, *Split*, *Not a secret*) needed these calls. Nothing changes in v1.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **Absence is one muted, scoped line, made in one place**: "No scene shows Mara learning this (checked up to 1.1)", or "(not checked yet)" before the book's first scene is read. Main's views carry only proved lines, your marks and "checked up to"; the words are made only in the panel's code. Tests check that the Story notes block, every v2 prose request, the chat agent's lookup, cards and passages, and the reader's and checker's requests never say "No scene shows", "checked up to" or that someone does not know.
  2. **This scene's Who knows what** (after True now): each secret the scene touches, with ⓘ and "readers since 1.1"; its holders who are Here ("Orrin: overheard from Tomas (1.1)", "Tomas: knew before (1.1)", your own "knew before the book (you said)"; suspects and believes otherwise muted); then, muted, the scoped line for each one Here with no line that counts (someone who only suspects gets both); up to 3 tension lines; "+ N more in Book" when there is more. A holder who is not Here is not listed in this scene (the grid has everyone). The section shows nothing when the scene touches no secret and has no tension, even if the book has secrets.
  3. **"+ N more in Book"** switches the panel to the Book tab and scrolls to the grid. The Book tab's *As of [scene ▾]* starts on the panel's scene and starts there again when you open another scene; the grid reads as of it.
  4. **The grid's cell actions open just below the grid** (not a floating menu): a line's ⓘ and *Not right*; your mark's *Remove*; an empty cell's scoped line with *Knew before the book*, offered only for a character or a group (the POV column could be another kind of record). A row's label opens the secret's sheet.
  5. **Not a secret / Not a thread only on what the AI made**: a secret you made is deleted from its sheet instead. It takes the topic, its reveals and the lines it stood behind in every draft, and your knew-before marks on it, and writes one Dismissed row per reveal ("Not a secret: the sold map", with the reveal's sentence), so those sentences never make it again while a new reveal elsewhere may (a topic with no reveal left gets one row keyed by itself). *Undo* on any of its rows brings everything back as it was (your Pinned and Ideas, marks, to-dos pointing at it, the Dismissed rows that went with it) and takes its other rows off the list; a label another record took meanwhile comes back as "the sold map (secret)".
  6. **Split** makes the merged reveal a secret (or thread) of its own, labelled as the reader read it (" (secret)" and then its scene added if another record holds that label), with the lines that came with it; its Dismissed row keeps the two from merging again.
  7. **A secret's or thread's sheet** keeps one label: no Names section, no Rename in book, no nickname Split, no Named in; its category cannot be changed; *Same as…* offers only its own kind (a named record never offers a secret or thread). A secret's sheet has **The secret** in place of From the book (readers since, ⓘ with *Split* and *Not a secret*, who knows it as of [scene ▾] with your marks' *Remove*, how many learn it later, *+ Someone knew before the book* for a character or group). A thread's sheet shows no memory section until slice 5's *The thread*.
  8. **A character's or group's Knows, as of [scene ▾]**: one line per secret ("the sold map: overheard from Tomas", 1.1), your marks with *Remove*, the lines that never count under "Not counted as knowing" (someone else's words, a rumour, a dream), "2 more later in the book"; with none, the scoped "No scene shows Mara learning a secret (checked up to 1.2)" (changed after review) *Not about* on a knows line offers only characters and groups.
  9. **The sidebar's Secrets section** comes after the categories (a secret is never shown as a stray category), listed in the picker once a secret exists and under "Show unused sections" before that (where *New secret* is); each row says "readers since 5.2 · 3 know" (as of the end of the book) and carries the AI mark when the reader made it.
  10. **Rebuild memory's "New secrets and threads (n)"** lists the topics the rebuild made (AI-made, made since it started, revealed in one of its readings), with one *Not right* that sends each to Dismissed as *Not a secret* would; a new thread's steps are not listed again as a sheet's new lines.
  11. **A tension line carries its secret's label and "readers since"** (a change to slice 1's view), so a line about a secret the scene does not touch can be worded.
- Alternatives: a hover menu per cell; offering *Knew before the book* for any record; letting *Not a secret* delete your own secrets; one Dismissed row per topic instead of per reveal; keeping From the book on thread sheets until slice 5; listing holders who are not Here in This scene.
- To change it: `src/renderer/features/memory/knows/knowsView.ts` (1, wording), `WhoKnowsWhat.tsx` (2), `ThisScenePanel.tsx` `BookTab` and `records/asOf.ts` (3), `KnowsGrid.tsx` `CellDetail` (4), `src/main/memory/reading/topicActions.ts` and `readerRows.ts` `TopicSnapshot` (5, 6), `records/RecordSheet.tsx`, `TopicSection.tsx` (7), `KnowsSection.tsx` (8), `shell/sectionList.ts` `secretsSection` and `records/RecordTab.tsx` `SecretList` (9), `rebuild.ts` `rebuildTopics` (10), `src/shared/memory/whoKnows.ts` `sceneSecrets` (11).

## 2026-10-13 · F-16.22 · P2b slice 3 review: calls made while tightening the live knows run
- Question: The review of slice 3 found that the live knows run could pass with a wrong "suspects" or "believes otherwise" line on the wrong person, or with an invented "told by"; that one provider error (a 429, or the credit running out) lost the whole report; that a model with no price and no reported cost made the 50¢ stop blind; and that an `OPENAI_BASE_URL` in your shell could quietly move the OpenAI route. Fixing them needed these calls (nothing changes in the app, nothing in v1).
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **Attribution precision counts every line the grid shows as a cell**: counted lines and partial ones (suspects, believes otherwise), each judged against a label in its scene (learner, secret, how class), and a line that names a source ("told by Mrs. Pocock") is right only when its label names that source. The report lists counted lines no label has, partial lines no label has, and lines with a source no label names, each under its own heading. The negatives (a dream, town talk, Mr. Harrow) are checked over the same cells, so "Mr. Harrow believes otherwise about the debts" fails the run as surely as a counted line would. This tightens P2b-D23's attribution bar (still 90 %).
  2. **A provider error stops that pass, not the report**: the scene it happened on and the code (rate limit, no credit, provider down) are recorded, the pass is marked failed ("stopped by a provider error after N scenes"), and the report is written with what was read and what it cost; the second pass still starts (a 402 or a bad key fails at once and costs nothing). The planted-error run does the same, through its spot checks too. No automatic retry.
  3. **A cost the 50¢ stop cannot see stops the run**: an answer with no reported cost on a model the price tables do not list (a `MYTHSCRIBE_EVAL_*_MODEL` override, or another server) counts 0 USD, so the run stops there and says "cost unknown for <model>"; a server on this machine (`OPENAI_BASE_URL` on localhost) charges nothing and is never stopped for it.
  4. **The OpenAI route goes to OpenAI**: with only `OPENAI_API_KEY` set, the run's requests go to `api.openai.com` even if your shell exports `OPENAI_BASE_URL` (the OpenAI library would otherwise read it on its own); the app's own path is unchanged (the e2e test relies on it).
  5. **The adversarial replay now does what the plan asked**: the dream in h06 and the town's talk in h07 are also offered as Lise being told of the engagement (already listed by then); the checker answers "only in a dream" and "only as rumour", and both lines are kept with that mode and voice, never counted, shown apart from her cell (which stays her overhearing in h05). The new-secret plants stay too.
