  6. **The live fidelity run sends every case once**: each case's cache key now covers its temperature and output cap, so no two cases can share an answer, and the offline test checks one request per case at the reported cost.
- Alternatives: a separate partial-precision bar; ignoring sources in attribution; retrying a 429 once after a pause; a conservative per-token estimate instead of stopping on an unknown cost; clearing `OPENAI_BASE_URL` only in the tests; recording the plan departure instead of adding the plants.
- To change it: `src/main/ai/eval/knows.eval.ts` `scoreKnows` / `knowsReport` (1, 5), `runLiveKnows` and `memoryOracle.ts` `readBook` / `readingStop` (2), `memory.eval.ts` `runLivePlanted` (2, 3), `src/main/ai/eval/liveProvider.ts` `liveRequestDeps` / `costUnknown` / `liveProviderError` / `isLoopbackUrl` (2, 3) and `OPENAI_API_URL` with `providers/openai.ts`'s `openAiURL` (4), `knows.eval.ts` `plantedRead` / `PLANT_TRUTH` (5), `prompts.eval.ts` `runLivePrompts` (6).

## 2026-10-13 · F-16.22 · P2b slice 3 (the knows replays, the live knows run, one key): calls made while building it
- Question: Replaying the test book's who-knows-what labels through the reading pipeline, building the live knows run for you, and making every live check start from one key needed these calls. Nothing changes in v1, and nothing shows in the panel yet.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **One key, `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`, for every live check**: the live fidelity run, the live planted-error run, the live knows run and the price probe read it first (OpenRouter, V4 Flash and V4 Pro unless `MYTHSCRIBE_EVAL_FAST_MODEL` / `MYTHSCRIBE_EVAL_STRONG_MODEL` say otherwise); without it they fall back to `OPENAI_API_KEY` (with `OPENAI_BASE_URL`, where an OpenRouter address still counts as OpenRouter); with neither they stop with one message naming both. The commands are in `docs/OPERATOR-SETUP.md` ("One key for every live run").
  2. **The live fidelity run now goes through the app's request path**, so each prompt leaves with the tier and reasoning the app gives it (story memory's reader and checker with reasoning off) and its cost is what OpenRouter reported; before, it went straight to the provider on the catalogue's tier with no reasoning setting.
  3. **Each live check is tested end to end without spending**: a fake OpenRouter on the machine answers every request (the knows and planted runs with the oracle's answers), so the tests prove each run starts from `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` alone, sends what the app sends, and writes a report with no key and no prompt text. The live knows run must pass every bar against that fake, and stops once the reported cost passes 50¢ over both passes (a pass the cap cut short fails).
  4. **A told, overheard or read line in its source's own words now finds its learner** (pipeline change): "Mr. Dunmore and I are engaged," Lottie tells Bel: the "I" is Lottie, the source, so Bel is found among the people named just before, as for "she" (Lottie herself is left out). The replay found that such lines were refused (Bel in h05, Johanna reading Lottie's letter in h08); the live reader would quote exactly these sentences. A line whose speaker is not its source still refuses a first person that is not the learner's.
  5. **The test book's knows labels say how each line is told** (narration, or dialogue with its speaker: the learner's own words for tells and refers, the source's for told and read), and every learning or holding label counts as the rules read it; the oracle's scene card lists the scene's learners as on the page.
  6. **How the oracle reads a secret afresh**: a secret or thread not yet listed is proposed new with the sentence that shows it in that scene; a listed one is named by its id, except at its defining sentence, where it is proposed again under its other name, and the checker's "same as …?" merges it. So the book-order replay makes each of the four topics once and merges two later reveals, the reverse-order replay makes them once and merges three.
  7. **The adversarial plants and their honest answers**: Bel knowing the engagement in h08 (Lottie's letter says so, but Bel is not in the scene: refused, not here); a rumour in h07 and a dream in h06 offered as new secrets (not made) and, since the review, as Lise being told of the listed engagement (kept with their voice and mode, never counted, shown apart); the town's talk of the Captain's cards as the Captain's own knowledge (kept as a rumour, never counted, shown apart); "the officer's wife had told everybody" as the regiment (refused: the regiment is not named); three more new secrets on the debts' page (the fourth over the cap); "Lottie's secret betrothal" (merged into the engagement); Lottie's marriage after you dismissed it as a secret (dropped silently); a step on the engagement's id (refused); and "Mr. Harrow does not know" (refused: not a way of knowing).
  8. **The live knows report**, per pass: knows recall, partial lines found, attribution precision (since the review: counted and partial lines, with their sources), how and from accuracy, thread steps and each thread's end, the duplicate rate with the extra topics listed for you to read, counted negatives, anything from never-read scenes, yields by reason, cut-off readings and cost; P2b-D23's bars, plus a pass the 50¢ cap stopped counts as failed.
- Alternatives: a separate key per run; keeping the fidelity run off the request path; checking only the key choice (no fake server); fixing the label quotes to name each learner instead of the ladder; taking the learners from a hand-written list; making each topic only from its defining scene (it would leave earlier lines without a secret); no bar for a stopped pass.
- To change it: `src/main/ai/eval/liveProvider.ts` (1), `src/main/ai/eval/prompts.eval.ts` `runLivePrompts` (2), `src/main/ai/eval/fakeChatServer.ts`, `memoryOracle.ts` `oracleChatServer` and the "end to end against a fake OpenRouter" tests (3), `src/shared/memory/ladder.ts` `resolveSubject` (`sourceId`) and `pipeline.ts`'s `resolve` (4), `src/main/memory/testBook/labels.ts` / `labels.schema.ts` and `memoryOracle.ts` `oracleCard` (5), `memoryOracle.ts` `oracleAnswer` (6), `knows.eval.ts` `plantedRead` / `PLANT_TRUTH` (7), `knows.eval.ts` `knowsReport` / `KNOWS_PASS_BARS` (8).

## 2026-10-13 · F-16.22, F-16.23 · P2b slice 2 review: calls made while fixing the reading of secrets and threads
- Question: The review of P2b slice 2 found ways the reading could fail, store a rumour as a secret, send text from Idea scenes, or lose secrets in long or cut-off replies. Fixing them needed these calls (nothing changes in v1, nothing shows in the panel yet).
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **The list of secrets and threads the reader sees has a ceiling** (about 140 at short labels, 1,000 tokens; P2b-D10 amended). Past its 400 tokens the labels shorten and the overflow comes out of the facts-near-the-window block, then out of the page itself (the scene is read in slightly smaller pages), so a reading never fails for being too long. Past the ceiling the AI-made secrets and threads with the least behind them are left out of the list (yours last), counted in the reading; one the reader proposes again is caught by the "same as …?" check.
  2. **Idea scenes never feed the secrets.** A secret or thread the AI made that only an Idea scene, a scene left out of compile or text outside the manuscript backs is not listed to the reader and is no "same as …?" candidate, and its sentence is never sent; a sentence you are re-checking is not sent either (the secret goes by its label).
  3. **A secret the text only rumours, supposes or dreams is not made** ("Some said the Captain was ruined at cards"): the checker now says how each revealing sentence is told (about 16 more output tokens a reveal), and only narration, a thought or someone's own words, now or remembered, make a secret. A thread is made however it is set up (a vow or a plan is a supposition). A re-checked reveal that now only rumours its secret goes.
  4. **A group knows where its sentence names it by its head noun**: "By Sunday the whole regiment knew" is the regiment's (design §2.5's "the whole harbour knew"); the checker still confirms it. This settles item 12 of the slice 2 entry.
  5. **Rebuild memory checks the scene's secrets and threads again**: the reader sees every revealing sentence in the scene as "re-checking"; one it does not state again (or the checker does not pass) goes, and a secret or thread left with nothing behind it is removed. This changes item 8 of the slice 2 entry (it kept them).
  6. **A learner the scene card names still gets their own on-page check**, so a card whose POV check fails does not lose the POV's own knows lines. This changes item 3 of the slice 2 entry for named learners (one check per learner and page).
  7. **A secret revealed on one page of a long scene and proposed again on a later page** under another label is compared with the earlier one in the same check batch ("same as …?") and merged when the checker says so.
  8. **A missing answer is never a judgment**: a "same as …?" item the checker skipped, an on-page check it skipped, or a secret lost when the reader's reply was cut off leaves the secret, its lines and their paragraphs to the next reading, rather than making a second secret or refusing the line; a cut-off reply that lost its new secrets is read again from the last one it gave (the whole page when it gave none). None of these count against the model's health, and neither does a line the checker passed that the here-rule refused in code.
  9. **A new secret the reader gives a listed id is still new** (its lines go with it, never onto the listed secret).
  10. **Not right › Not about on a knows line** (no button yet; slice 4): only a character or a group can be picked; the moved line keeps a source only for told, overheard or read, from the people named there, never the new learner; a character not here is checked on the page in the same request, and not shown there, the line is dismissed as for an unsupported one.
- Alternatives: a fixed page size with the roster cut first; listing every topic whatever its scenes; storing a reveal's voice and mode and dimming rumoured secrets (needs a migration); keeping the word-for-word group rule; keeping reveals on a rebuild; skipping the card-named learners' checks; merging duplicates only across readings; a missing verdict read as "not the same"; refusing a labelled item with a listed id; leaving *Not about* to the panel slice.
- To change it: `src/shared/memory/topics.ts` `TOPIC_ROSTER_MAX_TOKENS`, `src/main/memory/reading/rosterCache.ts` `buildTopics`, `src/main/ai/prompts/reader.v2.ts` `readerV2PageTokens` (1); `src/main/memory/reading/recordEvidence.ts` `topicsInDraft`, `topicContext.ts` `revealPicker`, `open` (2); `src/main/ai/prompts/verify.v2.ts`, `pipeline.ts` `revealCounts` (3); `src/shared/memory/ladder.ts` `groupHeadIn` (4); `pipeline.ts` `rechecks` and the transaction's reveal clean-up (5), the here checks (6), `freshFacets` and `stands` (7), `retryAfter`, `topicLine`, the topic verdict loop and `notHere` (8), the topics loop and `topicRef` (9); `src/main/memory/reading/notRight.ts` `notAbout` (10).

## 2026-10-12 · F-16.22, F-16.23 · P2b slice 2 (reader.v2, verify.v2 and the reading of secrets and threads; nothing to see yet): calls made while building it
- Question: Teaching the reader and its checker who knows what and plot threads needed calls the plan did not spell out. Nothing changes in v1, and nothing shows in the panel yet (slices 4 and 5).
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **The checker confirms a thread step's voice and mode too**, not only the step, so a supposed or rumoured "he will come back" is stored as such and never opens a thread; its verdict is budgeted like a fact's (28 tokens), not the plan's 12.
  2. **A knows line never waits.** A shared surname that fits two people, or a "she" the checker cannot pin to one person, drops the line (P2a's facts wait in both cases); the next reading tries again.
  3. **A "she" or "he" who learns something gets no on-page check of their own**: they count only when they are already here (the typed POV, *+ also here*, the card) or on the card this reading writes. A named learner who is not gets one on-page check per page; if it passes they are here for the reading and join the card's on-page list.
  4. **A new name cannot learn a secret or move a thread** until it is a record (its knows lines and steps are dropped with the reason "name"); its other facts still wait on your answer.
  5. **The "same as …?" check also compares a new secret or thread with the ones you made yourself**, even when no scene reveals yours yet, so the AI does not make a second "Ashby's return" beside yours.
  6. **A reveal you said is not a secret drops silently**, with everything that came with it, and is not counted against the model.
  7. **The health gate counts each new secret or thread as one item** (its sentence found or not, its reveal checked or not); same-as and on-page checks are not counted, so a model is never paused for saying two secrets differ. Topics made and merged are counted beside the rejections (`topicsCreated`, `topicsMerged`).
  8. **A re-checking reveal goes once its paragraph is read and nothing restates it** (as a re-checking fact does); a secret or thread left with nothing behind it is then removed. *Rebuild memory* keeps a reveal whose sentences still stand (there is no "unchanged" answer for reveals).
  9. **The reader's tail lists who is known to know a listed secret** (each person's earliest line, at most four, "r7 overheard 9.3 (from r2)") or "revealed 1.1" when nobody is shown knowing it yet, never who does not; open threads show "open since 2.1, last 7.3".
  10. **A source ("from") is kept only when the checker names one of the people offered** (the reader's own pick, the speaker, everyone named a sentence either side), and only for told, overheard and read.
  11. **The cost registry gains three more shipped rows than the plan named** (`reader.v2:card`, `verify.v2:pro`, `verify.v2:spot`, sized like their v1 rows) so every task, the rebuild price and the backlog price read v2 throughout. Index ≈ 12.8¢ and a session ≈ 1.44¢ on an own key, both within 30 % of the design.
  12. **Test book note for slice 3:** h13's group learner ("By Sunday the whole regiment knew") does not name "the regiment" word for word, so the rule "a group must be named in its sentence" refuses it. Slice 3's replay will either give the record the nickname "regiment" or the rule will need to accept the group's head noun; your call if you prefer the looser rule now.
- Alternatives: steps judged on their step alone at 12 tokens; knows lines waiting like facts; an on-page check for every possible "she"; new names learning secrets while they wait; comparing new topics only with ones the book reveals; counting dismissed reveals as refusals; leaving topic checks out of the health gate; keeping re-checking reveals until a full read; listing the plan's three cost rows only.
- To change it: `src/main/ai/prompts/verify.v2.ts` `verifyV2ItemOutput` (1), `src/main/memory/reading/pipeline.ts` (`ladder`, `topicLine`, the here checks, the verdict loop, the transaction's reveal clean-up; 2–4, 6–8, 10), `src/main/memory/reading/topicContext.ts` `open`, `lines` (5, 9), `src/shared/aiCostNotes.ts` (11), `src/main/memory/testBook/labels.ts` or `ladder` in `pipeline.ts` (12).

## 2026-10-12 · F-16.22, F-16.23 · P2b slice 1 (who-knows-what data, threads, years and ages; no AI, nothing to see yet): calls made while building it
- Question: Building the data layer and the pure rules of P2b needed calls the plan did not spell out. Nothing changes in v1.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **A secret stays on the grid while its reveal is re-checked.** "Readers have known since" counts a reveal or a learning line whose sentence you just edited until the next reading confirms or drops it, so an edit never makes a secret vanish for a moment. It is shown dimmed: "readers since" and a tension line carry a re-checking mark when they rest only on such lines (with Use AI off nothing re-reads them, so they stay dimmed, as P2a's re-checking facts do).
  2. **A secret you made yourself that no scene reveals yet is still a row** of the grid ("not revealed yet"), so your *Knew before the book* marks have a place; it never makes a tension line, since readers do not know it.
  3. **"Touched in this scene"** is any line about the secret there, a suspicion or a mistaken belief included, by anyone (someone who already knew it telling it again or referring to it too), and any reveal of it there, a merged one included.
  4. **A thread opens only at its first counted step** (an opening, advance or resolve in the story's now, not a rumour). Its reveal is shown as its quote, never as a status change: a reveal carries no voice or mode, so a supposed sentence ("If Mr. Ashby came back tomorrow") must not open it. (Changed in review: it first opened at its reveal.)
  5. **Someone's own words** show what they hold ("I knew it all along", telling someone) and what they suspect or wrongly believe; "I saw him sell it" in their own words does not count as learning it unless the narration says so.
  6. **Your own secrets and threads:** no name check; an exact duplicate of another secret's (or thread's) label is warned about, and making it anyway adds " (secret)" / " (thread)"; a label another record holds (a character "Ashby", a thread "Ashby") gets that added silently, and so does a label whose words a name made later takes (a new record, a nickname, Rename in book): a label never blocks a name. A secret or thread has one label and no nicknames, its category cannot change, it merges (*Same as…*) only with its own kind (the kept one keeps its label; the other's reveal stays as a merged reveal under its own label, with *Split*), and it splits by reveal, never by names.
  7. **An AI-made secret or thread with nothing left behind it is removed** after a reading, a record deleted, a scene deleted or a draft deleted (one it only came from). One you wrote on (Pinned, Ideas) or marked *Knew before the book* is kept. One only an inactive draft backs is not sent to the reader as "already found".
  8. **Ages "since then"** list the year-crossing hints of the scenes after the one that states the age (that scene's own hint is not "since"), and leave flashbacks out. Any season counts as crossing a year ("a summer later"), as the plan says, and so do six months or more in digits or words ("fifteen months", "twenty-four months") and a month span with no count or a vague one ("Months later", "some months": not knowing the year is safer than a wrong one); "two months", "a few months" and "that month" do not. An age stated in a flashback is never the age after it (story time); in a flashback only its own stated age shows.
  9. **Rename in book** renames a person inside a secret's reveal and the label the reader gave it ("waiting for Hesk" becomes "waiting for Haskel"), the label of a secret or thread the AI made included; one you made keeps its label (renamed only on its sheet). Undo puts them back.
  10. **The panel and sheet fields** for secrets, knows, threads and ages are added with the slices that fill them (4 and 5), so `memory:scene` and `memory:sheet` never carry empty placeholders in between.
  11. **The test book** gains two scenes of about 370 words each (h13, h14) and a group record "the regiment"; the new records are left out of the first reader's eval roster, so its token rows stay as they were. The new rule checks (a knows line about something that is not a secret, a topic in the name index, an AI topic nothing backs) heal on save like the others but are not counted in diagnostics (no Worker change).
  12. **A thought counts only for its thinker**: "Orrin knew, Mara thought" is Mara's belief, not Orrin knowing (a refinement of P2b-D7).
  13. **A scene with no words yet counts as checked** for "checked up to" (nothing is there to read, and no reading ever marks it), so one planned scene does not stop the line for the whole book.
  14. **A Pinned birth year** is read only from a line written "Born: 1792" or "Born (story year): 1792", never from a sentence that merely opens with "Born".
  15. **A secret's or thread's label is never a name question**, even when its words stand capitalised in the prose ("the Crimson Pact"), and the editor's near-name underline never takes its words.
- Alternatives: drop a re-checked secret from the grid until it is read again; hide your unrevealed secrets; touched = counted lines only; threads open at their reveal; count "I saw it" in dialogue; refuse a clashing label instead of renaming it; prune only after readings; list a stated scene's own hint; leave the reveal's label alone on rename; add the view fields now with empty values; count any thought; leave empty scenes unchecked; read any "Born …:" line.
- To change it: `src/shared/memory/whoKnows.ts` (1–3, 13), `threadsAsOf.ts` (4), `knows.ts` `knowsLine` (5, 12), `src/main/memory/recordStore.ts` `createTopicRecord`, `insertTopicRecord`, `yieldTopicLabels`, `updateRecord`, `sameAs` (6), `pruneTopics`, `tree:delete` and `drafts:delete` in `src/main/ipc/handlers.ts`, `rosterCache.readerRoster` (7), `src/shared/memory/years.ts` `hintsSince`, `elapsedBreaks`, `recordAge`, `BORN_LINE` (8, 14), `src/main/memory/reading/renameEvidence.ts`, `recordStore.renameInTopicLabels` (9), `src/shared/memory/views.ts` (10), `src/main/memory/testBook/` and `src/main/ai/eval/memoryFixtures.ts` (11), `nameQuestions.nameAsker`, `src/renderer/features/editor/nameCheck.ts` (15).

## 2026-10-12 · F-16.22–16.23 · P2b plan: the calls the design left open
- Question: Planning P2b, who knows what, threads and time (`docs/research/story-memory-2026-10-11/plans/P2b.md`, five slices, nothing built yet), needed calls the design did not make. Details are in the plan's §7 (P2b-D1 to P2b-D23).
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **Secrets get their own Secrets list** in the sidebar (shown once there is one), beside your Threads. Secrets and threads are not names: they never show as mentions, never block a new character's name, and Rename in book does not apply to them.
  2. **A secret can exist before anyone on the page learns it.** When the narration reveals it, the reveal itself (checked by a second AI read) is the proof, so "readers have known since" can start there.
  3. **Duplicates are compared only when they look related** (they share a word or someone who knows them), at most 3 comparisons per new secret or thread. A merge is kept with its own quote, so *Split* puts back exactly what came with it; after a Split the two are never merged again.
  4. **What counts as knowing:** the narration, a thought, or the person's own words (or the words they heard). Rumours, dreams and "what if"s never count. *Suspects* and *believes otherwise* are shown as such, but they are not knowing.
  5. **"Checked up to 14.2"** means every scene up to 14.2 has been read whole by the new reader, with nothing left unread. Scenes read before P2b show "not checked yet" until they are read again or you rebuild memory. This line is only ever shown to you: it never goes into a prompt or the chat.
  6. **At most 3 new secrets and 2 new threads per page of reading,** to keep the grid from filling with noise. The live test will show whether that is too tight.
  7. **Story years:** a time jump ends a typed year's carry-forward only when it could cross a year ("three winters later", "a year later", "years later"). "The next morning" or "a week later" do not, and are not listed under ages.
  8. **Birth years** come from the book ("Mara was born in 1792"), or else from a "Born: 1792" line you pinned (your old sheet fields become such lines at the switch-over), so the ages you had keep working. Ages always say "about".
  9. **You can add a secret yourself** (*New secret*) as well as a thread, and mark *Knew before the book* on any cell of the grid.
  10. **This scene** shows every secret the scene touches and at most 3 tension lines (first "readers know, the POV doesn't"), with "+ N more in Book".
  11. **The live test** (needs your key, after the build, CLAUDE.md 1c) reads the test book twice, in order and backwards, and checks: at least 70 % of who-learned-what found, at least 90 % of what it stores right, at most 1.25 records per real secret or thread, and nothing from rumours or dreams. Every live run, the probe included, now takes `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` alone: `MYTHSCRIBE_EVAL_LIVE=1 OPENROUTER_API_KEY=sk-or-… npm run eval:ai -- knows`.
- Alternatives: secrets only under the Book tab; a learner required before a secret exists; compare every new secret with every old one (more cost, more false merges); count rumours as "heard"; let any time jump stop the year; ages from the book only; no cap on new secrets.
- To change it: `plans/P2b.md` §7, before the slice that builds it.

## 2026-10-12 · F-16.16–16.21 · P2a ticked before your checks in the app
- Question: P2a's final verification passed on the reviewed code (full gates; `e2e/memory.spec.ts` drives the reader at the fake provider: typing sends nothing, *Read now*, leaving a scene, a stale line after an edit, the backlog's price, Here, True now, ⓘ, *Not right* and its *Undo*, decision 4's conflict, Settings, *Rebuild memory*, card lines, plan links with *Unlink*, its *Undo* and *Keep*, `synopsis.v2` and the beta reader on cards; the v1 smoke passes unchanged). Tick now, or wait for your checks?
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed): ticked F-16.16 to F-16.21 on 2026-10-12, as P1 was and as P2a-D18 planned, each entry naming the check still owed. v2 is not in installed builds, so the checks run in a development build: `MYTHSCRIBE_STORY_MEMORY=v2 npm run dev` with a new project or a copy (the override makes every project it opens run v2). **Owed before the switch-over:** the live planted-error run (F-16.17's merge condition: `MYTHSCRIBE_EVAL_LIVE=1 OPENAI_API_KEY=sk-or-… OPENAI_BASE_URL=https://openrouter.ai/api/v1 npm run eval:ai -- memory`, stops at 50¢, report in `test-results/eval-memory/`) and live fidelity for `reader.v1`, `verify.v1`, `betaReader.v2`, `betaReaderRegen.v2`, `synopsis.v2`, `notesSuggest.v4` and `planLinks.v2`; no build session had a key. The Cloud cap was not driven end to end (the e2e has no Cloud); its tally and notice are unit-tested. This supersedes item 13 of the slice 5 review entry below.
- Alternatives: leave them unticked until you have checked each one and the live runs pass.
- To change it: untick the entries in `FEATURES.md` §2.16 and the M8 row.

## 2026-10-12 · F-16.20, F-16.21 · P2a slice 5 review: plan links that follow the book, cards only from scenes that happened
- Question: The slice 5 review found plan links that could stick to the first scene read, link on a shared name alone, hand a plan to the runner-up after *Unlink*, and outlive a draft switch; and readers that took an Idea scene's old card, a paraphrased place, or one side of a contradiction as fact. These are the calls made fixing it; items 1, 5 and 9 of the slice 5 entry below are superseded where they differ.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **A name counts once.** A name in the plan that the name scan resolves is one term (its record), not also a word, and a code link needs at least one shared word besides names: "Mara learns about the map" is never linked to a scene just because Mara is in it.
  2. **Code links are matched again after every reading,** from the cards as they are then: a scene that now clearly beats the linked one takes the plan; one that comes too close takes the link off and, when the reading moved one of their card lines, the AI picks. So a *Rebuild memory* that reads the weaker scene first no longer keeps the plan there. An AI pick stays until it no longer stands, or until a reading moves its scene's card line and that scene no longer fits the plan at all. Your own links are never touched.
  3. **After *Unlink*, the unlinked scene still counts in the ranking:** when it is the best, or one of a close two, nothing is linked, so the runner-up never gets the plan in its place. A clearly better scene written later can still be linked.
  4. ***Undo* of an *Unlink* in Dismissed puts the link back** as it was (yours into the scene's metadata, an automatic one into the links), unless the plan has words or another link by then.
  5. **Automatic links are per draft** (migration `0035_plan_link_draft`): only the active draft's count; another draft's wait for it. A link from before 0035 is dropped and matched again on the next reading.
  6. **The plan-link pick is background spend,** even after *Read now*, a rebuild or a priced back-fill (their prices covered readings only), a reply that does not read included; past the Cloud cap it is not asked at all, and code still links what is clear.
  7. **An Idea scene or one left out of compile has no card for any reader:** not in the story map, not in the agent's `cards` by name (by id it says it never happened), not for the beta reader, synopsis, notes or the mood; a plan linked to it reads *Not yet drafted* again.
  8. **The agent's `lookup` says "Last seen" only for a scene where the record is Here** (the POV, on the page by a verified quote, or *+ also here*); a later scene that only names it is "Last named in …" with its line and no place, time or POV.
  9. **A place or time goes to a prompt only as the text words it** (verified, or the value is words of its own quote), the rule decision 4 already uses; a paraphrase is left out.
  10. **The Story notes block** gives the Here records' first Pinned paragraph the room before the book's values (your canon wins a tight budget, such as ghost text's 150 tokens), never sends a value your Pinned line already says, and leaves out a fixed detail the book gives two different values for (grey in 2.1, brown in 11.2) until you settle it.
  11. **Your link to a scene with no words yet** reads *Not yet drafted* in the Outline, with "linked to …" as a note and *Unlink*, and the plan stays on the story map.
  12. **`notesSuggest.v4` has no "follow the text where the card lags" rule** (`synopsis.v2` has one). Kept as is: its system turn is v3's byte for byte for the prompt cache, its rules already say to rely on the scene, and a card is redone whenever its quotes or a quarter of the scene change (P2a-D17). The next notes version adds the clause; the live fidelity run will show whether it is needed sooner. FEATURES F-16.20 now says the card goes without its mood.
  13. **F-16.16 to F-16.21 stay unticked.** P2a-D18 and plan §5.5 say to tick them after the final verification, naming your checks still owed, as P1 was; the run that built slice 5 was told not to tick, and this review run leaves that as it is. Tick them, or have them ticked, once you have checked them in a development build; the owed checks are listed in CLAUDE.md. (Superseded the same day: ticked after the final verification, entry above.)
- Alternatives: link only when no name is involved; match plans once, at the end of a rebuild; ask the AI whenever a dismissed scene is close; keep links across drafts; tally the pick as asked spend inside asked work; show an Idea scene's card with an [idea] mark; send both values of a contradiction ("Eyes: brown · also grey 2.1"); a new `notesSuggest.v5` now.
- To change it: `src/shared/memory/planMatch.ts`, `src/main/memory/reading/planLinks.ts`, `src/main/memory/reading/planLinkStore.ts`, `src/main/memory/reading/readerService.ts` (`linkPlans`), `src/main/memory/reading/cardContext.ts`, `src/main/ai/agentToolsV2.ts` (`lookupV2`), `src/main/memory/reading/views.ts` (`hereValues`), `src/shared/memory/storyNotes.ts`, `src/main/ai/context/storyTime.ts`, `src/renderer/features/outline/OutlineTab.tsx`.

## 2026-10-12 · F-16.20, F-16.21 · P2a slice 5 (readers on cards, plan links applied automatically): calls made while building it
- Question: Slice 5 moves everything that read stored summaries onto the reader's cards and makes plan links derived data (story memory v2 only; v1 is unchanged, by test). These are the calls the plan left open.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **When code links a plan:** the plan's terms are the words of its title and synopsis (plurals folded, common words out) plus the names the name scan is sure of; a drafted scene's are its title, its card's line and gist, and its names. The best scene is linked when it holds at least 35 % of the plan's terms, at least two of them, and 1.5 times the runner-up's share; a plan of fewer than two terms is never matched. Tuned on the fixtures only.
  2. **One plan per drafted scene, automatically:** a scene you linked yourself, or one already linked automatically, is not offered to another plan. Structure-template beats stay yours (nothing links them in v2).
  3. **The AI is asked only after a reading,** only when the best two are too close, only when that reading moved the card line of one of them and the scene it read is one of the two, and at most once per reading (the same question again is answered from the local cache). A new plan or a synopsis edit waits for the next reading of any scene; nothing is sent for it on its own.
  4. **The Plan links switch covers the code links too:** off, no link is made at all, so the switch is your one say over it.
  5. ***Unlink* on your own link also sends the pair to Dismissed,** so code never makes it again (*Undo* there lets it); *Keep* turns an automatic link into yours (`fulfilledBy`). A link whose plan gets words, or whose scene is emptied, goes at the next reading.
  6. **The Story notes block's values** are for the records Here only (on the page, the POV among them): their fixed values and lasting values standing as of this scene ("from the book so far"), and transient values stated in this scene; never what someone says, dreams or hears as rumour. The mood line is the card's mood and theme ("as read from the prose"); the card's mood is not re-checked against edits (it has no quote). The edit passes' continuity piece gets neither (its continuity form), and rewrite and edit passes keep their own mood line, so the block leaves its mood out there.
  7. **The beta reader in v2** reads earlier scenes that have a card, as their gists; an Idea scene or one left out of compile is skipped and not counted; one with no card yet is counted as "not read yet" with *Rebuild memory* as the way out. Every item must quote the scene being read: an item quoting an earlier scene is dropped (counted as uncited); an item may name the earlier scene it relates to ("Relates to …").
  8. **A new read-only channel `memory:cardLines`** gives the Outline and the cork board every scene's card line, reloaded for the scenes a reading changed.
  9. **The chat's tools:** `cards` prints each scene's card as a map ("No card yet; read the scene." when it has none), `lookup` adds where a record was "Last seen" (the last readable scene up to the open one), and `read_summary` (reachable only from older tool lists) answers with the card's gist, marked as a map.
  10. **A scene read before its names are indexed** matches by its words only (the name scan runs on save, so this is rare); the next reading matches it again with its names.
- Alternatives: links only when a scene's card names the plan's title; asking the AI for every close pair; code links free of the switch; *Unlink* of your link without a Dismissed row; values for every record named in the scene; the beta reader quoting gists; a `card:get` per row instead of one list.
- To change it: `src/shared/memory/planMatch.ts` (thresholds), `src/main/memory/reading/planLinks.ts`, `src/main/memory/reading/planLinkStore.ts`, `src/main/memory/storyNotes.ts`, `src/main/memory/reading/views.ts` (`hereValues`), `src/shared/memory/storyNotes.ts`, `src/main/ai/betaReader.ts` (`runBetaReaderV2`), `src/main/memory/reading/cardContext.ts`, `src/main/ai/agentTools.ts`, `src/main/ai/agentToolsV2.ts`, `src/renderer/features/outline/OutlineTab.tsx`.

## 2026-10-12 · F-16.18 · P2a slice 4 review: card values on the text, Not right everywhere, and what Undo can bring back
- Question: The review of slice 4 found the card and Here trusting values the text no longer says (or never said in its own words), card values with no *Not right*, and *Not right* paths with no Undo. These are the calls made fixing them.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **A card value counts only while it stands on the text:** its quote is still in the scene once and the sentences it sits in are the ones the card was read from. An edited one is shown dimmed in the card ("AI read: the quay · re-checking after you pause"), never as Here's POV or on-page character, and raises no typed-detail question; a scene that is an Idea or left out of compile has none standing. *Update my detail* asks again after your saves land, and writes nothing if the text changed meanwhile.
  2. **The card shows what decision 4 counts:** a POV or year the second read did not confirm, or a place or time that is not the quote's own words, never stands for the scene: your typed value shows ("the harbour (typed)") with "AI read: the docks district, not checked" dimmed under it. A place or time in its quote's words says "quote checked in code" in ⓘ.
  3. **Not right on card values:** the POV and on-page chips in Here and each card row have ⓘ with *Not right*; it puts the value in Dismissed for that scene (the POV or character by record, a place, time or year by its words), so Here, the card and the question leave it out and your typed detail stands, for every later reading of that scene too, until *Undo*. *Keep mine* keeps its meaning: it only stops the question; the text's value still shows.
  4. **Wrong value on a line whose sentence changed** still makes a Dismissed row (keyed by the line's stored sentences), so *Undo* always brings it back; if you undo your edit instead, the line does not come back by itself (Undo in Dismissed does).
  5. ***Not about Mara*** shows the checker everyone named in the line's sentence and the eight before it, plus the record you pick (marked "not named here" when nothing names it), and moves the line only when the checker picks yours. No answer, or a sentence changed since it was read, changes nothing and says so (Wrong value is still there). A moved line stays moved: a later reading, a rebuild too, stores it for the record you picked.
  6. **After the trial ends (read-only), *Wrong value*, *Keep mine* (and closing the question) and a card's *Not right* are refused,** as their Undo is (this replaces item 12 of "P2a slice 4"). *Not about* is refused by the AI path as before.
  7. **An edit dims a line at once:** once a save settles, a line whose sentences changed dims in True now, an open sheet and ⓘ, with no reading in between; a scene with a card is looked at again after each settled save.
  8. ***Keep mine* on a POV remembers the record,** so renaming it never asks again. Sums round to the cent first: $0.996 reads "$1", never "100¢".
- Alternatives: *Keep mine* making the typed value win in Here and the card (instead of *Not right* on the value); *Not right* on a card value keyed by its quote (a new quote would bring it back); refusing *Wrong value* on a changed sentence; *Not about* with the picked record alone; letting the read-only app keep writing Dismissed rows.
- To change it: `src/shared/memory/reproof.ts` (`holdingCard`), `src/main/memory/reading/views.ts` (`heldCard`), `src/main/memory/reading/detailConflicts.ts`, `src/main/memory/reading/notRight.ts` (`cardNotRight`, `notAbout`, `writeNotRight`), `src/main/memory/reading/pipeline.ts` (`notAboutCheck`), `src/shared/memory/reconcile.ts` (`movedTo`), `src/main/ipc/handlers.ts` (the anchors job, `dismissed:undo`, `memory:notRight`, `memory:answerNotice`), `src/renderer/features/editor/CardBlock.tsx`, `src/renderer/features/memory/CardValue.tsx`, `src/renderer/features/thisScene/ThisScenePanel.tsx`.

## 2026-10-12 · F-16.18, F-16.16, F-16.19 · P2a slice 4 (the panel, ⓘ and Not right, sheets as of a scene, the card, Settings, Rebuild memory): calls made while building it
- Question: Slice 4 puts the reading on screen (story memory v2 only; v1 shows what it did). These are the calls the plan left open.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **The typed-detail conflict ships now, with F-16.18** (P2a-D13, as planned): "14.2: the text says the quay; you typed the harbour" under Conflicts, with *Go there*, *Update my detail* and *Keep mine*, and the Needs a look badge counts it. *Update my detail* writes the text's value into your detail: the POV becomes the card's record; a place becomes the one place record whose name or nickname the text's words hold, or else the words themselves with no record; a time the words; a year only when the text gives a year. *Keep mine* (and closing it with ×) leaves your detail and is never asked again for that value (a `notProblem` row in Dismissed; a different value in the text is asked again). F-16.29 keeps book against book, notes, Research and loose ends.
  2. ***Not about Mara*** makes one fast check (`verify.v1`) of the line for the record you pick, reading three sentences either side so a "she" can find its name. Supported: the line moves to that record, marked as checked by a second AI read, and Mara gets a Dismissed row for it, so a later reading does not hand it back to her. Not supported: it goes to Dismissed as a wrong value. The check is costed in the ledger but writes no reading row, so it never moves the health gate.
  3. ***Wrong value* and Undo:** the line's stored row is kept beside its Dismissed row, so *Undo* brings the line back as it was; the scene's quotes are then checked again (a sentence changed since leaves it dimmed until the next reading). A line whose scene, draft or record is gone since is not brought back. The rebuild summary's *Not right* for a sheet works the same way.
  4. **The footer's "today" is what story memory reading cost today** (reader and checks), no longer all AI spend as in P1, since the footer is the reading's.
  5. **Here, once the scene has a card:** the POV shows as a chip only when the card's checked POV quotes one; otherwise your POV picker stays. The characters on the page say where on hover. *Mentioned* leaves out whoever is Here. True now shows for anyone Here or Mentioned as soon as something is known about them, even before this scene has its own card.
  6. **True now and sheet lines read** "Last seen at the quay" (a label ending in "at" runs on) or "Eyes: grey", with "since 12.1" / "last stated in 13.4" beside them; a sheet's fixed values that differ list the others under it as "also “grey” 2.1"; other voices read "Tomas says: …", "Rumour: …", "Remembered: …", "If: …", "In a dream: …".
  7. **A sheet reads "as of" the scene open in the editor**, else the end of the book; the picker lists every manuscript scene by title (one left out of compile or marked Idea reads as of the readable scene before it).
  8. **The card in scene details** sits where the summary was, collapsed with its line in the header; open, it shows the line and gist as "the AI's map of this scene, never quoted", where, when, POV and year with ⓘ (the quote, *Go there*, checked or not), your typed value dimmed beside a differing one, a value only you typed marked "(typed)", then mood and theme. Flashback and elapsed time are not shown yet (P2b's timeline).
  9. **Rebuild memory** lives in the Book tab: the price first (nothing is sent), then the run with its progress in the dialog and the activity bar and *Stop*, then the summary grouped by sheet. Closing the dialog leaves the rebuild running; the Book tab and the activity bar's note reopen the summary.
  10. **Settings › AI › Story memory** shows "Read after a pause of [3] minutes", on Cloud the monthly cap (typed dollars, pulled to quarter-dollar steps between $0 and $20) with this month's background and asked spend, "proof agreement: 48 of 50 checked" (fast and strong checks together), the share of the last 100 readings cut off at their limit, and health per model under a fold. The health notice's *Details* opens this tab.
  11. **Small sums read in cents:** "0.09¢", "0.3¢", "12¢", "<0.01¢" for less, "$1" and "$1.25" from a dollar.
  12. **After the trial ends (read-only), *Not right* and *Keep mine* still work** (they write only Dismissed rows, like the notices' other buttons; `memory:notRight` and `memory:answerNotice` are `ai` channels, refused only where a model would run). P1's *Not a mention* is a `write` channel and is refused then; say if both should be refused.
- Alternatives: the typed-detail question with P4 (F-16.29) as first specced; *Not about* as a fresh reading of the scene; *Undo* that waits for the next reading to bring a line back; the footer keeping all AI spend; the POV chip always shown with the picker in scene details only; the card open by default.
- To change it: `src/main/memory/reading/detailConflicts.ts`, `src/shared/memory/typedVsText.ts` (`detailFromText`), `src/main/memory/reading/notRight.ts`, `src/renderer/features/thisScene/ThisScenePanel.tsx`, `src/renderer/features/memory/` (`memoryView.ts`, `ClaimLine.tsx`, `RebuildMemory.tsx`, `MemorySettings.tsx`), `src/renderer/features/records/FromTheBook.tsx`, `src/renderer/features/editor/CardBlock.tsx`.

## 2026-10-12 · F-16.16, F-16.19, F-16.17 · P2a slice 3 review: held-back reading never blocks what you asked for, back-fills per record
- Question: The review of slice 3 found that background reading held back by the Cloud cap, the health gate or a local cut-off stopped the whole reader queue, so a *Rebuild memory* or a confirmed backlog waiting behind it never ran, plus smaller gaps. The fixes change items 1, 6 and 9 of the entry below (edited there to match). None changes v1, and there are still no buttons in v2.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **Held-back background reading is dropped, not waited on.** A reading nobody asked for that reaches its turn after the cap, the health gate or a local cut-off began holding such work back is dropped quietly; the scene stays unread and the queue goes on, so what you asked for behind it runs. When the hold lifts (*Allow another $1*, a higher cap, a new month, *Keep reading anyway*, a switched model, a probe that passes), the backlog and the back-fills are checked again at once: up to 5 scenes left meanwhile are read, more ask with the price.
  2. **The health gate also holds the bulk work you ask for.** *Rebuild memory* answers "this model's readings aren't reliable enough" instead of starting (a rebuild replaces what it does not prove again, so a weak model would remove good facts); *Read them* on a backlog, *Read* on a draft never read or on a back-fill wait, their notice kept beside the health notice. *Read now* of one scene still reads. A rebuild already running when the model falls below the bars stops where it stood, scene by scene; its spot checks run only if it read a scene.
  3. **The Cloud cap comes before the health gate:** while both hold, no probe reads (a probe would spend past the cap).
  4. **A back-fill's 3 pages are per record, all its names together**, both when it asks and when it runs: a record whose name and nickname come to 4 pages asks, and is not read on the side when another record's small back-fill runs. One record's names are read together, a paragraph naming it twice once.
  5. **Filing a Research page back-fills** the records and nicknames it adds, as making them by hand does.
  6. **A scene left again while it is being read** is read once more afterwards.
  7. **A rebuild closes when its last scene job ends, however it ends** (read, failed for good after its retries, or held back), so its summary and spot checks never wait forever. It takes no request id: the indexing indicator's Stop ends it.
  8. **Cloud spend is counted request by request**, so a reading or spot check that fails part-way still adds what it spent to the month's tally.
  9. **Scenes the chat writes without opening them** (a new scene, a split) count as a write outside the editor, so the backlog check reads them. The chat's edits to an existing scene open it, so leaving it reads it; edit passes work on the open scene the same way.
  10. **Until slice 5, the chat in v2 uses a scene summary only while it still matches the text** (the open scene's summary, the `cards` fallback, `read_summary`), since v2 no longer writes summaries. The other summary readers (beta reader, scene steer, story map, synopsis, notes suggestions, plan links) are re-pointed in slice 5 as planned and read the last summaries until then.
- Alternatives: keep the pause and let the queue skip held jobs; hold *Read now* under the health gate too; pause asked work under the health gate instead of refusing it; a separate channel for the chat's writes; leave the chat's summaries as they were until slice 5.
- To change it: `src/main/memory/reading/readerService.ts` (`gate`, `backgroundBlock`, `askedBlock`, `publish`, `closeRebuild`, `runBackfill`, `answerNotice`, `rebuild`, `spender`; 1–4, 6–8), `backfill.ts` (`backfillsByRecord`; 4), `src/main/jobs/indexQueue.ts` (`JobSkippedError`, `indexAll`'s `afterRunning`; 1, 6), `src/main/ipc/handlers.ts` (`research:applyFiling`, `document:save`; 5, 9), `src/renderer/features/ai/agentApply.ts` `writeDocument` (9), `src/main/ai/summarize.ts` `currentSummary` (10).

## 2026-10-12 · F-16.16, F-16.19, F-16.17 · P2a slice 3 (when a scene is read, the backlog, Rebuild memory, the Cloud cap, summaries off): calls made while building it
- Question: Building the reading triggers, the reader's queue, the backlog, the back-fill, the health gate, the spot checks, *Rebuild memory* and the Cloud monthly cap needed calls the plan did not spell out. None changes v1; in v2 there are still no buttons (slice 4 adds the panel, the footer, Settings and the rebuild dialog), but reading now happens on its own when you leave a scene or pause.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **More than 5 changed scenes: one question for all of them.** After a write outside the editor (Replace in project, a revert, a draft switch, a snapshot restore, Rename in book, an import) and on every open, the app counts every scene of the draft with something unread, not only the ones that write touched. Up to 5 are read at once; above that none is read and Needs a look asks "Read 37 changed scenes: about 3¢". *Read them* reads them all as asked (never held back by the Cloud cap, and still asked after a close; while the health gate pauses the model, *Read them* waits, review item 2); *Later* leaves each to be read when it is next left.
  2. **Turning Use AI on reads nothing by itself.** Nor does adding a key or changing the source: scenes are read when you leave them, pause, press *Read now*, or at the next open (up to 5). This keeps the switch itself free of surprise spending.
  3. **With reading off, nothing waits in a queue.** Leaving a scene with Use AI or the reading switch off, or with no key, queues nothing: the scene stays unread and is read when it is next left (or at the next open) once reading is on. A reading already queued when you turn it off stays queued and runs when you turn it back on.
  4. **A draft never read** asks only when more than 5 of its scenes are due (a new project with a few scenes is simply read); the question is priced like *Rebuild memory*, and *Read* starts a rebuild (so its spot checks and summary follow).
  5. **The Cloud cap's notice** shows while background reading has reached the cap, until you answer or close it, once a month. After *Allow another $1 this month*, reaching the new limit does not ask again that month: the footer (slice 4) shows "Cloud $2 of $2" and Settings changes the cap. The tally is raised from `/credits` whenever Settings shows your balance and at most once an hour while reading on Cloud.
  6. **The health gate** judges the fast model in use. *Keep reading anyway* is remembered per model in this project; closing the notice hides it for the session but reading stays paused (one probe in 10 due scenes still reads, through the queue, and none while the Cloud cap holds). *Switch to DeepSeek V4 Flash* is offered on OpenRouter and Cloud only, when the fast model is another, and sets the fast model app-wide for that provider.
  7. **A local model that answers empty at its limit** pauses background reading for the session, with one notice; *Keep reading anyway* lifts it. *Read now* still runs (and answers "paused" if it happens again).
  8. **Spot checks:** a rebuild's 50 fast and 20 strong checks are part of its price and spent as asked; from then on the 1-in-20 checks are background (held by the Cloud cap). A claim the strong model refuses is deleted and noted in the developer tools log, not counted in diagnostics (the server accepts only `memory.invariant.*` names for new counters).
  9. **A back-fill is per record and per name**, sized when the name is added (the paragraphs naming it ±2 in scenes already read); up to 3 pages per record (all its names together) are read without asking, more ask "Read 14 scenes for Hesk: about 0.4¢"; *Not now* declines it for good. Each name back-fills once.
  10. **The rebuild summary's new lines** are the facts the rebuild's readings stored (not those it kept or brought back); *Not right* for a sheet removes them all and keeps them out; *Not names* answers every name the rebuild left waiting as not a name.
  11. **Summaries are off in v2:** *Summarize now* and *Summarize all scenes* answer "Scene summaries are off in this project" until slice 5 retires those channels in v2; the summaries switch is hidden in v2 Settings (it sends nothing there).
  12. **Today's spend in the status** is the day's reader and checker cost from the usage ledger (readings, their checks and the spot checks).
  13. **A queue fix shared with v1:** a job queued in the instant a queue was finishing an empty pass could wait for the next unrelated trigger; it now runs at once. This affects the summary, voice, mention and plan-link queues too, only by removing that delay.
- Owed by the author (unchanged): the live planted-error run before the switch-over, and live fidelity for `reader.v1` and `verify.v1`; slice 3 adds no prompt.
- Alternatives: queue the first 5 of a large backlog and ask about the rest; read the backlog when Use AI comes on; keep queued work while reading is off; a draft-unread question for any unread draft; a cap notice each time the limit is reached; health judged per routed model; persisting the local cut-off pause; a diagnostics counter for strong rejections (needs a Worker change); back-fills sized at run time; summaries left running in v2 until slice 5.
- To change it: `src/main/memory/reading/readerService.ts` (`checkBacklog`, `settle`, `settingsChanged`, `queueBackground`, `computeNotices`, `answerNotice`, `tally`; 1–8, 12), `backlog.ts` (`BACKLOG_UNASKED`, `priceReads`), `backfill.ts` (9), `rebuild.ts` (10), `spotChecks.ts` (8), `src/main/ai/cloudTally.ts` (5), `src/main/ipc/handlers.ts` (`SUMMARIES_OFF_V2`, 11), `src/main/jobs/indexQueue.ts` `kick` (13).

## 2026-10-12 · F-16.16, F-16.17 · P2a slice 2 review: who a "she" may mean, edits during a reading, replies that do not read
- Question: The review of slice 2 found ways a fact could land on the wrong person, stay live after its sentence changed, or be lost without a trace. The fixes change items 1, 3, 4, 5 and 9 of the entry below (edited there to match). None changes v1, and nothing is visible yet.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **A "she" is checked against everyone it could mean:** the checker now sees every character named in the eight sentences before it, not only those in the nearest sentence that names someone. "Clara and Lise sat by the window. Tomas brought the tea. She had green eyes." offers Clara, Lise and Tomas, so an honest checker answers "more than one could" and the fact waits on all three instead of being stored on Clara.
  2. **A person the text names nowhere near is offered only as "not named here":** the checker may pick them only when the sentences point to them by a role ("his wife" makes "She" Mrs. Harrow in the test book). Otherwise it must answer "none". A checker answer of "none" with only one person offered refuses the fact.
  3. **A sentence that names someone the roster does not know, and has no pronoun, is about that new name:** "Jem Falsire had green eyes" is never stored on Rynna Falsire, whatever the reader proposed.
  4. **An edit saved while a reading is out is respected:** before writing, the reading loads the scene again; if a sentence changed (or the scene became an Idea or was left out of compile), every fact it wrote is checked against the text as it is now, so one whose sentence is gone is "re-checking", never live.
  5. **A reply that does not read (a refusal, prose, an empty answer) reads nothing:** its paragraphs stay unread, nothing waiting or re-checking there is deleted, and it is not kept in the local cache, so the next reading asks again. The reading and its cost are still recorded.
  6. **The checker is given room for every verdict:** about 28 tokens per fact and 12 per card or on-page check (it was 15 per item, which cut real replies short), at most 13 facts per batch. A reply still cut off has its unanswered items asked once more; an item never answered is not counted as the checker's refusal, and its paragraph is read again later.
  7. **A long scene's card** takes each field from the first page that gives it with a quote found on that page, and who is on the page from every page. A card that only a retry gave (the first reply was cut off before it) counts as having seen only the retry's paragraphs, so it is asked for again sooner.
  8. **The reader is asked to list its facts in paragraph order;** a cut-off reply that did not keep that order is read again whole, not just after its last quote.
  9. **New names are asked about only when they stand capitalised mid-sentence** (the design's rule): "Hesk was a smuggler" at the start of a sentence is not enough. A titled name ("Captain Vell") the twin gate ties to a record, which no question ever asks about, is dropped. A name two records share ("Harrow") waits on both, for the name scan to settle. Facts waiting on a name settle when a record gets that name any way (made in the Story bible, a nickname added), not only through the question.
  10. **A stored fact is refused only when the checker refused that very fact** (same value, voice and mode), not a different value proposed on the same quote.
  11. **The scene-summary switch stays in Settings for v2 projects** until slice 3 turns `summary.v5` off there (summaries still run in v2 until then, so their switch must stay).
  12. **The live planted-error run gains a bar:** support precision ≥ 90 % (verified facts on labelled sentences, about the labelled person). Facts stored on sentences no label covers are listed in the report as "unreviewed" for the author to read; the 50¢ stop now also holds during the spot checks.
- Owed by the author (unchanged): the live planted-error run before the switch-over, now with the support-precision bar and the unreviewed list to read.
- Alternatives: only the nearest naming sentence's people (the design's literal wording; a wrong woman could be forced); never offering a person the text does not name (loses "his wife"); refusing every fact whose sentence holds any unknown capitalised word (loses "In London she…"); writing what a reading found even when the text changed under it (it goes stale on the next save); fewer facts per checker batch; counting unlisted facts against attribution.
- To change it: `src/shared/memory/ladder.ts` `resolveSubject`, `unknownName` (1–3); `src/main/ai/prompts/verify.v1.ts` `VERIFY_RULES`, `VERIFY_CLAIM_TOKENS`, `verifyBatches` (2, 6); `src/main/memory/reading/pipeline.ts` (4–8, 10); `src/shared/memory/nameQuestions.ts` `nameAsker`, `src/main/memory/reading/waiting.ts` `settleOwnedNames` (9); `src/shared/aiSettings.ts` `MEMORY_ONLY_FEATURES` (11); `src/main/ai/eval/plantedScore.ts` `PLANTED_PASS_BARS` (12; in `memory.eval.ts` until Switch-over slice 5).

## 2026-10-12 · F-16.16, F-16.17 · P2a slice 2 (the reader, its checker, Read now): calls made while building it
- Question: Building `reader.v1`, `verify.v1` and the reading pipeline needed calls the plan did not spell out. None changes v1; in v2 nothing has a button yet (*Read now* is a channel the This scene panel will use), and nothing reads on its own until slice 3.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **One switch, two features:** Settings › AI shows one **Story memory reading** switch in v2 that turns the reader and its checker on or off together (a reading without its checker would keep nothing), and hides v1's tags, continuity, Organise, To do and sheet-sync switches there (they do nothing in v2; the summaries switch stays until slice 3 turns summaries off in v2, review item 11). The data-sharing panel and the usage table keep one row each (Story memory reading, Story memory check). *Read now* with either off, or with Use AI off, answers "turned off" and sends nothing.
  2. **Reasoning off everywhere, and the lowest effort on a direct OpenAI key:** OpenRouter and local servers get no reasoning; a direct OpenAI key on a reasoning model gets its lowest effort (`minimal` on gpt-5, `none` on gpt-5.1 and later, `low` on o-series), because OpenAI cannot turn it off. Only the reader and the checker ask for this.
  3. **A description is pinned like a pronoun:** "the tall woman" goes to the checker with every person named in the eight sentences before it, the same as "she"; nothing is guessed in code (review items 1–3). First person is pinned only to the speaker of a speech or to the scene's typed POV.
  4. **A new name needs a name capitalised mid-sentence in its paragraph** (review item 9) before its facts are kept; then they wait on the name's question (no record is made silently). Answering *Yes, make a record* attaches them: live when their sentences still stand where they were, else "re-checking" until the paragraph is next read. *No, not a name* deletes them. A name with facts waiting is asked about after one appearance instead of the usual several.
  5. **The scene card of a long scene** (several pages) takes its line and gist from the first page that gave one, each other field from the first page that gives it with a quote found on that page, and who is on the page from every page (review item 7); POV, story year and flashback count only when the checker confirms them.
  6. **A reply cut off at its cap** keeps every complete item, and the paragraphs after the last one it quoted are read once more (once, never a third time). On a local model a cut-off reading stops and *Read now* answers "paused".
  7. **The prompt's rules and the roster form one system message** (the stable prefix the providers cache); the roster lists records by short id, in the order they were made, capped at 1,200 tokens (the least named are left out over the cap), and a new nickname is added at the end without rebuilding the cached part.
  8. **The cost table's design sizes stay** for the shipped reader and checker rows: the test book's roster is far smaller than a real one, so its measured sizes would understate the cost.
  9. **The live planted-error run's pass bars:** quotes found ≥ 90 %, checker agreement ≥ 80 %, the right person ≥ 95 %, facts proved in code right ≥ 98 %, checked facts right and about the right person ≥ 90 % (review item 12); the run stops once it has spent 50¢. The spot checks inside the app (1 in 20) wait for slice 3; the live run makes its own (50 code-proved, 20 with the strong model).
  10. **This scene's data (`memory:scene`) now carries the scene's facts** with their state, so the panel can show them when P2a's UI lands.
- Owed by the author: the live planted-error run, P2a's merge condition, before the switch-over: `MYTHSCRIBE_EVAL_LIVE=1 OPENAI_API_KEY=sk-or-… OPENAI_BASE_URL=https://openrouter.ai/api/v1 npm run eval:ai -- memory` (report in `test-results/eval-memory/`), and live fidelity for `reader.v1` and `verify.v1`.
- Alternatives: two switches; reasoning left at the model's default on OpenAI; descriptions left unpinned (their facts dropped); a record made for every new name; the last page's card; no retry of a cut-off reply; the measured sizes in the cost table; other pass bars.
- To change it: `src/shared/aiSettings.ts` `FEATURE_GROUPS`/`MEMORY_ONLY_FEATURES` (1), `src/main/ai/providers/openai.ts` `lowestReasoningEffort` (2), `src/shared/memory/ladder.ts` `resolveSubject` (3), `src/main/memory/reading/pipeline.ts` and `waiting.ts`, `src/shared/memory/nameQuestions.ts` (4), `pipeline.ts` `mergeCards` and the retry (5, 6), `src/main/ai/prompts/reader.v1.ts` and `src/main/memory/reading/rosterCache.ts` (7), `src/shared/aiCostNotes.ts` (8), `src/main/ai/eval/plantedScore.ts` `PLANTED_PASS_BARS` (9; in `memory.eval.ts` until Switch-over slice 5), `src/shared/memory/views.ts` (10).

## 2026-10-12 · F-16.16, F-16.17, F-16.18 · P2a slice 1 review: what proves itself in code, and how facts follow your edits
- Question: The review of slice 1 found ways a fact could be proved for the wrong person or kept after its sentence changed. The fixes change some of slice 1's calls (items 3, 4, 5 and 9 of the entry below). None changes v1, and nothing is visible yet.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **Code proves a fact only from a plain sentence, as the design says:** any of the stop words (not, might, if, than, like, "had fought", said, heard…) or a question mark anywhere in the sentence, even after a comma or a dash, sends the fact to the second AI read instead. So "Mara was dead; no, she was only sleeping" is never proved in code. The cost: in the test book, 5 of the 7 facts code used to prove now go to the checker (about a hundredth of a cent each), for example "Johanna was two-and-twenty that spring, and had learnt to read bad news…". The alternative is to let "had …" in a later clause pass (two of the five would prove in code again).
  2. **Traits are never proved in code** ("Mara was asleep" is not a lasting trait), **nor is family with a second person** ("Mara was his daughter": code cannot tell who "his" is), **nor "Mara died"** (the word "dead" is not in the sentence). A stated age or death must end its phrase: "Mara was ten minutes late" and "dead tired" prove nothing.
  3. **A possessive proves only its own owner's looks:** "Mara's brother had blue eyes" and "Mara's mother's grey eyes" never become Mara's eyes.
  4. **Speech is read across the paragraph:** a sentence in the middle or at the end of a speech counts as speech, in double or single quotation marks, and a speech left open at the end of a paragraph runs on into the next one (one paragraph at most, so a missing closing mark does not silence the rest of a scene).
  5. **A fact stays only while its sentences stand together:** the quote's sentence and its neighbours must still be side by side, in order; a sentence put between them makes the fact "re-checking". When the sentences come back as they were (Ctrl+Z, or the open scene saved after *Rename in book*), the fact comes back for free. When paragraphs are added above it, the fact follows its sentence.
  6. **A scene you mark as an Idea or leave out of compile** has its facts set aside (not counted as an error) and they come back when you change your mind. Its This scene panel shows what is true as of the scene before it.
  7. **Scene numbers** ("12.1") count only the chapters compile prints and the scenes it reads; an Idea scene is named by its title.
  8. ***Not right* sticks to the sentence that stated it:** editing the sentences around it, *Rename in book* or *Same as…* keeps the line dismissed; a new sentence stating the same value is new evidence.
  9. **Place and time from the text win over what you typed only when the AI quoted them word for word** (or the second read confirmed them); a paraphrase never replaces your typed place.
  10. **The card is redone when a quarter of the scene changed since it was made**, counted across several small reads (the card now keeps what it was made from; migration 0034 was regenerated before it shipped, so a project opened with the first slice-1 build needs its pre-migration backup).
- Not fixed, left for the author: after *Rename in book* with a scene open, if you type in that scene and then press *Undo*, the open scene keeps the new name (its undo is refused, as now) but its facts go back to the old one, so they show "re-checking" until the scene is next read (one small read). Fixing it needs the window to tell the app whether its undo went through.
- Alternatives: the clause-scoped stop words of slice 1; traits proved in code; one placeholder key for Not right with its neighbours; leave a renamed scene's numbering as the tree counts it.
- To change it: `src/shared/memory/literal.ts` and `markers.ts` (1–4), `anchor.ts` `anchorInPlace` and `reproof.ts` (5), `src/main/memory/reading/anchors.ts` (6), `views.ts` `readingOrder` (7), `dismissed.ts` `statingKey` (8), `typedVsText.ts` (9), `windows.ts` `cardDue` (10).

## 2026-10-12 · F-16.16, F-16.17, F-16.18 · P2a slice 1 (the evidence data): calls made while building it
- Question: Building the data layer of the evidence reader (no AI yet, nothing you can see yet) needed calls the plan did not spell out. None changes v1.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **Names out of the reading gate:** each name in a sentence counts as "this person" rather than as its letters, so renaming someone never makes a scene read again, while swapping one person for another in a sentence does. What was read is kept with the names in, so a name or nickname you add later changes nothing either; *Rename in book* rewrites what was read along with the prose.
  2. **A fact that went stale is re-checked** the next time the scene is read even when the only change near it was cosmetic (a typo fix, a comma) or a name: otherwise "re-checking after you pause" could wait for ever. When a scene is read anyway, the cosmetic edits in it are read too.
  3. *(Changed by the review above: traits are no longer proved in code.)* **Facts proved in code:** besides eyes, hair, scars, birth, age, death, place, clothing and things carried, two more kinds of sentence prove themselves: "Mara was good-looking and gentlemanlike" (a trait) and "Mara was the daughter of a miller" (family), because the test book expects them proved without the second AI read. Code proves only plain narration of the present; speech, rumour, memories and dreams always go to the checker.
  4. *(Changed by the review above: a stop word anywhere in the sentence counts.)* **Words that stop a proof in code:** not, never, might, if, than, like, "had fought" and the like, as planned, plus words that report rather than narrate (said, heard, rumoured, believed, remembered, dreamt…). They count before the quote and in the rest of its clause; in a later clause only the ones that reach back over a whole sentence count ("…, if the letter was true", "…, or so they said"), so "Johanna was two-and-twenty that spring, and had learnt to read bad news…" still proves her age.
  5. *(Changed by the review above: no longer.)* **"Mara died"** proves she is dead, although "dead" is not a word of the sentence (the slot has two values).
  6. **Card lines are left out of the passage search** unless asked for by name, so Ms Scribe's passage search does not change before P3 gives cards their own place.
  7. **Rename in book also renames** the person in the AI's card line and summary and in a fact's value ("waited for Hesk" becomes "waited for Haskel"), not only in the quotes.
  8. **Diagnostics:** a fact demoted because it broke one of the memory's rules is counted (only the count, never the text) as `memory.invariant.<kind>`, when you have turned diagnostics on. The Cloud server accepts these counts from this build on, so **deploy the Worker from this build before a release that carries them** (an older server refuses a report with a counter it does not know).
  9. *(Changed by the review above: only compiled chapters and readable scenes are counted.)* **Scene numbers** ("14.2") count every chapter in reading order, including empty ones; a scene outside any chapter shows its title.
- Alternatives: one placeholder for every name (swaps would not trigger a read); leave stale facts until the next real edit; only the plan's slots proved in code; a whole-sentence marker check (fewer facts proved for free); leave card lines in the default search; rename only the quotes; no diagnostics count.
- To change it: `src/shared/memory/gate.ts` (1, 2), `literal.ts` and `markers.ts` (3–5), `passages.ts` `DEFAULT_PASSAGE_SOURCES` (6), `src/main/memory/reading/renameEvidence.ts` (7), `src/shared/diagnostics.ts` (8), `sceneRef.ts` (9).

## 2026-10-12 · F-16.16–16.21 · P2a plan: the calls the design left open
- Question: Planning P2a, the evidence reader (`docs/research/story-memory-2026-10-11/plans/P2a.md`, five slices, nothing built yet), needed calls the design did not make. Details are in the plan's §7 (P2a-D1 to P2a-D25).
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **Closing the project never waits for the AI.** A scene you leave unread at close is read the next time you open the project (up to 5 scenes at once; more asks with the price first).
  2. **The pause before a read** (2 to 5 minutes, 3 by default) is a per-project setting beside VibeWrite's pause, counted from the last save of the open scene. The paragraph you are typing in is skipped only on that pause, not when you leave the scene or press Read now.
  3. **"Fixing a misspelt word" doesn't trigger a read** when the old word appears nowhere else in your book and isn't a name or a dictionary word you added (the app has no spelling list of its own to ask).
  4. **No sheet is made silently in P2a.** A new name the reader finds becomes a "new name?" question, and what it read about that name waits until you answer. (Secrets and threads, made silently, come with P2b.)
  5. **Cloud cap:** your monthly cap ($1 by default) limits only reading you didn't ask for. Read now, Rebuild memory, and anything you approved with a price are spent because you asked (the daily cap still applies). Past the cap you get one notice a month: *Allow another $1 this month* or *Change the cap*. The month's figure is checked against your Cloud account so another computer's reading counts too.
  6. **Spot checks:** when the stronger model disagrees with a fact the fast check passed, the fact goes; when the fast check disagrees with a fact code proved, it is only counted in "proof agreement".
  7. **Story notes for prose** gain each present character's current facts, this scene's states and its mood, inside the existing prompt versions rather than 11 new ones.
  8. **Plan links in the new memory cover planned scenes only.** Story-structure beats (from a template) stay yours to place. The AI's links are kept apart from your own, with *Keep* and *Unlink*.
  9. **Typed POV, place or time against the text:** the "Update my detail · Keep mine" question comes with P2a, not P4, since that is where both values first show.
  10. **The beta reader** in the new memory sees earlier scenes only as short maps and quotes the scene you are on, so it needs its own regenerate version too: P2a ships 7 prompt versions, not 6.
  11. **The live planted-error run** (the merge condition) can't run here without a key. It is built for you to run once (about 5¢): `MYTHSCRIBE_EVAL_LIVE=1 OPENAI_API_KEY=sk-or-… OPENAI_BASE_URL=https://openrouter.ai/api/v1 npm run eval:ai -- memory`, before the switch-over. P2a's features are ticked after its final checks with that run named as owed, as P1's were.
- Alternatives: read at close and make closing wait; a word list for the typo rule; let the reader make sheets for new names; count asked reading against the cap; 11 new prose versions; AI links for template beats too; leave the typed-detail question to P4.
- To change it: `plans/P2a.md` §7, before the slice that builds it.

## 2026-10-12 · F-16.4–16.15 · P1 ticked before your checks in the app
- Question: The slices said each P1 feature stays unticked until you check it. P1's final verification passed (full gates; `e2e/memory.spec.ts` covers the v2 path with Use AI off, the Story notes block and filing at the fake provider, and the switch-over on a copy, beside the v1 smoke). Tick now, or wait for your checks?
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed): ticked F-16.4 to F-16.15 on 2026-10-12, as P0 was, with each entry naming the check still owed. v2 is not in installed builds, so the checks run in a development build: `MYTHSCRIBE_STORY_MEMORY=v2 npm run dev` with a new project or a copy (the override makes every project it opens run v2, your real one included), and for F-16.15 Help › Try the story-memory switch-over on a copy… on a copy of your real project with no override. Live fidelity is unrun for the 14 new prompt versions (`MYTHSCRIBE_EVAL_LIVE=1`).
- Alternatives: leave them unticked until you have checked each one.
- To change it: untick the entries in `FEATURES.md` §2.16 and the M8 row.

## 2026-10-12 · F-16.15 · P1 slice 6 review: calls made while fixing it
- Question: The review of slice 6 found AI-written sheet text that would have converted as canon, author text that would have been lost or hidden, and a few dry-run gaps. The fixes needed calls of their own. Nothing here changes your installed app (v1); the switch-over still runs only on a test copy in a development build.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **What counts as the AI's (decision 8), replacing item 5 of the slice 6 entry:** every paragraph any applied library, Organise, chat or sheet-sync run added to a page or a field, while it still reads as written (your edit changes it, and then it is yours). A field the AI appended to keeps your own paragraphs as your Pinned line under the field's label, and only the appended ones go under "Filed by AI earlier: please check". A sheet such a run made stays all the AI's while only AI runs have written in it: its stamp is the one the creation logged, or no later than five minutes after its latest AI run (sheet sync stamps its run before it asks the model). An edit of yours between two AI runs cannot be told apart, so that text is filed as the AI's (kept, Not canon, for you to check), not as yours.
  2. **An AI sheet you added to is kept:** a sheet the background reading made is dropped only when it holds nothing of yours. One with a dated line, a relationship, a picture or a Plan/Idea status of yours becomes a record ("from a sheet the AI made, kept for what you added to it"): your lines in Pinned (or Ideas), the picture kept, its own text under the Not canon heading.
  3. **Plan and Idea sheets:** the AI's text on them goes to Ideas under the same heading, never to Pinned.
  4. **Inline tags' default words (changes item 3 of the slice 6 entry):** a tag that becomes a record now defaults to the record's name ("#tomas" → "Tomas", "#the-burned-mill" → "The Burned Mill"), so the new name scan finds it and the scene does not list it under "also here". Only a label tag, a dropped tag or a deleted one keeps the tag's words with hyphens as spaces.
