This reads the design's "otherwise the tag name with hyphens turned into spaces" as the fallback for tags that make no record. You still edit every row.
  5. **The timeline's events:** with the Timeline section hidden in v2 until P2b, every event (its name, when, year, the scenes linked to it and its note, linked or not) is written into a Research note "Timeline", one line each, counted as canon like any note. The story years still go on the first scene of each run.
  6. **AI plan links:** the links the plan-link job applied itself at chat mode Auto come off the outline when you convert, and its open suggestions go; the links you confirmed or dismissed stay. P2a re-derives AI links with *Unlink*.
  7. **Time text:** a scene's Time takes up to 500 characters, as v1's timeline text did, so a long one converts whole; a stored detail that fails to read now empties only that field, not the scene's whole set.
  8. **The trial copy and Convert:** starting the trial now offers the Drive copy-back of the original first (as every project switch does), and Convert writes pending typing before the conversion. When the copy fails to open again after converting, the done screen says the copy is converted and to open it from File › Open project…; Convert is not offered again.
  9. **Smaller:** the rebuild price leaves out scenes left out of compile (and Idea scenes, inherited from a folder too); nesting is listed for every tag of yours, a record's too; merge rows and shared-name notes carry the category ("Harrow (character) and Harrow (place)"); a v0 file converted in v2 keeps a character and a place of one name as two records, the name shared on both.
- Alternatives: only the last AI run's text (the slice as built); a made sheet's text the AI's only while its stamp never moved; dropping every AI sheet; the tag's words as the default for record tags; listing the timeline events in the dry run without converting them; leaving AI plan links in place and marking them as kept; cutting a long time text to 200 characters.
- To change it: `src/main/memory/switchover/model.ts` (`aiWritten`, `AI_RUN_SLACK_MS`, `sheetLines`, `inlineTagRows`, `timelineLine`), `src/main/memory/switchover/convert.ts`, `src/main/ai/planLinks.ts` (`dropAiPlanLinks`), `src/shared/memory/sceneDetails.ts`, `src/renderer/features/switchover/switchoverStore.ts`, `src/main/project/legacyImport.ts`.

## 2026-10-12 · F-16.15 · P1 slice 6 (the switch-over flow on copies, imports in v2): calls made while building
- Question: Slice 6 needed calls the plan did not spell out. Nothing here changes your installed app: the switch-over runs only on a test copy, only in a development build, and a v1 project imports, compiles and opens exactly as before.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **Where it starts:** Help › Try the story-memory switch-over on a copy… appears after a separator in any development (unpackaged) build while a v1 project is open, whether developer tools are on or not (the menu has no submenus, so "Help › Developer" is the end of Help). It writes your pending typing, copies the project beside itself as `<Name> (story memory test 2026-10-12 1430)` and opens the copy. The copy gets its own project id and the name "<Name> (story memory test …)", so its backups never mix with the original's or count against how many of them are kept.
  2. **Order of the steps:** the dry run and the consent screen come first; the safety step runs when you press Convert, just before the only write: the Drive working copy is copied back, a full backup is made (if it fails, nothing converts), and a dated copy `<Name> (before story memory …)` is saved beside the project. Then main closes the copy and opens it again on the new memory (the window shows the welcome screen for a moment).
  3. **Names:** a record made from a tag with no sheet is named by the tag's words capitalised ("mara-vell" → "Mara Vell"). The consent screen proposes the linked sheet's name for each inline tag, otherwise the tag's words as written ("mara"), as the plan says; you edit each row. Two sheets that shared one tag (a character and a place both called Harrow): the first keeps the tag's links; a name several records hold is marked shared on each and offered in the merge list *(changed 2026-10-14, "Switch-over slice 5 review" item 1: a character and a place are never offered, and a shared nickname is never the reason for an offer)*; after the merges you tick, a name only one record still holds is no longer shared.
  4. **Typed details:** a scene's POV or location matches a record by name, nickname, the words of a tag name (v1's pickers offered tag names) or the tag itself. A POV nothing answers to becomes a Character (as the plan says); a location nothing answers to stays as the place's text only. "Also here" comes from your character and place tags on a scene only when the converted scene does not name the record (checked with the new name scan, after your replacements), at most 30 per scene. A timeline year outside ±100,000 is left out.
  5. **Sheet text the AI wrote (decision 8):** a field whose text is still what the last library, Organise, chat or sheet-sync run wrote; page paragraphs such a run added that are still there; and every line of a sheet such a run made that nobody has edited since. An old sheet-made row without its date stamp counts as yours. The Project notes' AI parts get the same Not canon heading on the Research note.
  6. **Line forms:** dated lines read `Age (from 3.1 'Scene 1'): 36` (chapter.scene by reading order); relationships `Relationship: Mentor of Oren Hale (sworn teacher)`; thread events `Opened (from 2.1 'Scene 1'): The ring is gone`. A field's further paragraphs follow it as lines of their own.
  7. **Dismissals:** dismissed names become `name` dismissals unless the name is a record now (listed); a kept spelling becomes "not the same" only when it is a record's name and the twin gate pairs it with another record (listed otherwise); dismissed plan links become `unlink`. AI-tag removals, hidden facts, To do and continuity dismissals are listed as dropped.
  8. **Rebuild price:** one `reader.v1` request per 2,500 words of prose per scene plus a `verify.v1` per page, and the 20 strong and 50 fast spot checks, priced on your fast and strong models; Idea scenes are not counted; minutes at 8 seconds a scene.
  9. **Imports in v2:** `importStructure.v2` is version 1 without the tag bank (so v2 imports send less); a project made by importing or by converting a v0 file runs whatever memory new projects run (v1 unless the development override says v2). A v0 file converted in v2 makes records (its references, their pages as Pinned, and its name and plot-thread tags) and labels (the other tags, spelled as in v0, on the documents they were on), never tags or sheets. Compile's "Keep # on tags" is hidden in a v2 project and treated as off.
- Alternatives: requiring developer tools on for the menu item; keeping the original's project id on the copy; the safety step before the dry run; reopening the copy without the welcome screen in between; proposing the title-cased name as the default replacement; a Place record for every unknown location; "also here" from every link.
- To change it: `src/main/memory/switchover/` (`model.ts`, `plan.ts`, `convert.ts`, `trial.ts`), `src/shared/memory/switchoverMapping.ts`, `src/shared/memory/inlineTagReplace.ts`, `src/shared/menu.ts` (`SWITCHOVER_MENU_ENTRIES`, `menuFor`), the `switchover:*` handlers in `src/main/ipc/handlers.ts`, `src/renderer/features/switchover/`, `src/main/ai/prompts/importStructure.v2.ts`, `src/main/project/legacyImport.ts`.

## 2026-10-12 · F-16.14, F-16.9 · P1 slice 5 review: calls made while fixing it
- Question: The review of slice 5 found filing could fail as a whole on one clashing nickname, and a few places where the block or filing did not follow the design. The fixes needed calls of their own. Nothing here changes your installed app (v1).
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **Prompt versions edited in place:** `contextImport.v3` (no titles as nicknames), `editPass.v3` (the continuity task), `whatNext.v4` and `notesSuggest.v3` (their words for the block) were changed in place rather than as new versions, since none of them has left this branch or had a live run. The token report diff is in the commit.
  2. **One bad name never sinks Apply:** new records are made with their main name first, then each nickname one by one; a nickname another record holds by then is left out, and a card whose name another record took since the review is skipped and counted in the toast ("1 card left out: its name is another record's now"). The review chat refuses a rename or a nickname another record or card holds, and says why.
  3. **A line that fills or extends one of yours** ("Eyes: unknown" → "Eyes: grey") still replaces it in place and starts accepted (the card shows "replaces …", and Undo filing puts yours back). The review suggested appending instead, as the plan says; this is left for you to decide.
  4. **Undo filing** takes the page it undoes, and refuses another page's.
  5. **Not changed:** the activity indicator and the usage list still name filing "Context library sorting" (Settings now says "Filing uploads into sheets" in a v2 project).
- Alternatives: new prompt versions for each wording change; skipping the whole filing when a name clashes; appending every filed line; a read channel for whether Undo filing still stands (the renderer keeps it, as main does, for the session).
- To change it: `src/shared/filing.ts` (`planFiling`, `isTitleAlias`, `filingAsNew`, `applyFilingOps`), `src/main/research/filing.ts` (`applyFiling`), `src/main/research/filedLines.ts`, `src/shared/memory/storyNotes.ts`, the four prompt files.

## 2026-10-12 · F-16.14, F-16.9, F-16.7 · P1 slice 5 (the Story notes block for prose, filing uploads into Pinned): calls made while building
- Question: Slice 5 of P1 needed calls the plan did not spell out. Nothing here changes your installed app: a v1 project sends every prompt exactly as before (the new prompt versions are used only in a v2 project) and keeps the Library's upload review.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **What the block says:** "Story notes (the manuscript wins: …)", then "Here: Mara Vell (POV), Tomas Harrow", "Named: The Hollow Elm, Wren", one line per record with up to five of its nicknames and its Pinned paragraphs ("Mara Vell (Mara, the Captain): Eyes: grey. Lost her brother…"), then the scene's tone, content and theme labels. Never an Ideas line, a Not canon paragraph, or a line saying something is unknown; custom labels never leave the machine. When it is over budget (400 tokens; ghost text 150), each record here gets its first line first (the POV's, then the others'; one that does not fit is cut short, and then no named record gets a line), then the rest of their lines, then the named ones in what room is left (most named first; the first that does not fit ends them), and long name lists end "… and 3 more". The scene line of a v2 prompt (where, POV, when) comes from the scene's typed details (the place record's name, the POV record's name, the time and year). The card's mood joins with P2a's cards.
  2. **Where it goes:** in the old story bible's place, last in the stable part of the prompt, and the separate scene steer is gone in v2 (the labels are lines of the block). What next and scene-notes suggestions say "story notes" where their v1 rules say "story bible", and their story-time rule says the notes are timeless facts that never make a plot event happen. Edit passes carry the block on the continuity pass only, in its own form: headed "Story notes (the author's notes the passage is checked against)", without the tone and content lines, and not sent at all when no record has a Pinned line; the task asks for each disagreement as a change whose reason names the note and says the note may be what needs updating, so you decide. Developmental, line, copy and custom passes send what they sent.
  3. ***What the AI is told*** (a fold at the bottom of This scene) shows the block at the 400-token budget, read from the project when you open it and again when the scene's names, details, labels or records change. Ghost text sends the same block cut to 150 tokens.
  4. **Filing a Research page:** *File into sheets* on the page's header (not for a Not canon page, an image, or a page already filed, which shows "Filed on 2026-10-12"). It opens a dialog: the estimate ("14 requests to … · about $0.14"), *File with AI*, progress with Stop, then the review deck. Filing runs once per page; afterwards the page's changes are watched (point 7), not filed again. A Not canon paragraph is never sent. Pictures come from the Research image pages, offered to a record when its name or the model says so.
  5. **The deck:** groups New records · Additions · Conflicts · New categories; every card starts accepted, as the upload review does; a proposed category's card shows only when a new record uses it, and skipping it puts those records in World. A line whose words are not in the page is dropped and counted ("2 lines dropped: quote not in the document"), as are lines Pinned already says, a name you dismissed, lines filed under a name two records share (never guessed), and a nickname left out: one another record holds (shared there or not) or another card already gave, one dismissed for that record, or a title ("the Captain", "Captain Vell"; you can still add one on the sheet or ask the review chat). A line that fills an empty or placeholder line of Pinned ("Eyes: unknown"), or keeps every word of it and adds more, replaces that line in place; a line that disagrees ("Eyes: grey" against "Eyes: green") is a conflict, and the deck keeps Pinned's line unless you pick the upload's. A name the twin gate thinks is an existing record ("Thomas" beside Tomas Harrow) gets a card of its own that starts undecided, with *Same as Tomas Harrow* (its lines go to Tomas and "Thomas" becomes a nickname) or *New record “Thomas”*.
  6. **Apply and Undo:** Apply writes everything in one go, each line marked as filed (page, paragraph, quote), shown under the line as "Filed from “Bestiary” ¶4" (the quote on hover) until you edit that line, which makes it yours. The toast has *Undo filing*, and so does the page's header beside "Filed on" for the rest of the session: it takes back each filed line still as Apply wrote it (a replaced line comes back), the new nicknames and pictures, and a new record nothing has changed since and no scene's details name; categories it made stay; anything you changed stays and the toast counts it. The review chat on the deck can merge, change a kind, rename, set nicknames and leave a card out; *split* and moving text into or out of notes answer "not available when filing: text that fits no record stays in the document".
  7. **When the page changes:** a filed line whose quote is no longer in its page is a **conflict** at the top of Needs a look, and it lights the badge: "Mara Vell: “Keeps a fen-wight skull…” · Filed from “Bestiary” ¶3, which now reads “…”" with *Go there* (the page), *Update line* (you rewrite the line beside what the page now says; nothing is written until Save) and *Keep line* (a Dismissed entry; it asks again only if the paragraph it is about changes again, not for an edit elsewhere on the page). Switching the whole page to Not canon flags every line filed from it ("which is now Not canon"). Checked locally, with no AI, when a Research or Pinned page is saved and when a v2 project opens. The spec says the app proposes updates: here the conflict is the proposal and the new words are yours, since an AI rewrite would be another AI call per change.
  8. **Channels:** two the plan did not list, `research:keepFiledLine` and `research:updateFiledLine`, which *Keep line* and *Update line* need.
- Alternatives: the block after the task context (it would break provider caching); the scene steer kept beside the block; the block on every edit-pass type; filing as side work in the assistant column rather than a dialog; filing again after an edit (re-reading only what changed); a conflict defaulting to the upload's line; *Update line* as an AI proposal; Undo filing also removing the categories it made; the "Filed from" mark stored in the page instead of drawn beside it.
- To change it: `src/shared/memory/storyNotes.ts` (`renderStoryNotes`), `src/main/memory/storyNotes.ts`, the v2 prompt versions in `src/main/ai/prompts/` (`*.v5`/`*.v6`/`critique*.v4`/`editPass.v3`/`whatNext.v4`/`notesSuggest.v3`, `contextImport.v3`, `reviewChat.v3`), `src/shared/filing.ts` (`planFiling`, `decidedFiling`, `applyFilingOps`), `src/main/research/filing.ts` and `filedLines.ts`, `src/renderer/features/research/filingStore.ts` and `FilingDialog.tsx`, `src/renderer/features/thisScene/filedConflictStore.ts` and `ThisScenePanel.tsx`, `src/renderer/features/editor/filedFrom.ts`.

## 2026-10-12 · F-16.12, F-16.10, F-16.13, F-16.4, F-16.5 · P1 slice 4 (This scene panel, Needs a look, Dismissed, Rename in book, the agent in v2): calls made while building
- Question: Slice 4 of P1 needed calls the plan did not spell out. Nothing here changes your installed app: it runs story memory v1, whose Tags panel, menus, chat agent, router and data-sharing table are exactly as before.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **Where the panel lives:** in the Tags panel's slot (its place in your layout is kept), called This scene, with its own header button (an eye icon) and *View › This scene* in a v2 project (in place of View › Tags; changed after review). With no project open the menu is the one you have now.
  2. **Named in this scene:** one chip per record the scene names for certain, most named first, with how often. Hovering says why ("named 2 times: ¶1, ¶3"; paragraphs counted from 1). A click opens the why card: one line per place, each with *Go there* and *Not a mention* (a phrase is dismissed per place, since that is what Not a mention remembers), and *Rename in book…* for the record. A name the scan could not settle shows dashed and dimmed ("Rynna Falsire or Tomas Harrow?") with *Go there* only.
  3. **Here:** the POV picker and *+ also here* chips are the same controls as the scene details (one owner of the save), so a change in either shows in both.
  4. **Needs a look:** says "Nothing needs a look." when empty. *+ To-do* opens a field; a new to-do points at the open scene unless you untick "For this scene", and at a record when you pick one ("About Mara Vell"; changed after review). My to-dos lists your open to-dos in your order, with up/down and delete; ticked ones wait behind a collapsed "2 done" line, where you can untick or delete them (changed after review: they no longer stay in the list struck through). Questions: *Add as…* lists every category by its noun (Character, Place, …), *Not a name*; nicknames *Yes* · *No*. Notices: only "Inline tags show as plain text until you switch over." for a scene that still holds inline tags. The badge counts conflicts only, and there are none before slice 5, so it never shows yet.
  5. **Footer:** "Today $0.03" is the app-wide day's AI spend (the figure Settings › AI shows), read when the panel opens, a dash when it cannot be read; **Dismissed (n)** opens the list. *Read now* and the last-read time come with the reader (P2a).
  6. **Dismissed dialog:** newest first; each row shows its kind ("Not a mention", "Split", …), what it was about, the words when they differ, and the date, with *Undo*. Opening it reads the list again from the project.
  7. **Rename in book:** from the sheet (*Rename in book…* under Same as and Split) and from a name's chip in the panel, so the open scene changes in its editor. You pick which name (the record's own or a nickname) and type the new one; after a short pause it shows "2 matches in 2 scenes (+1 in other drafts)", the scenes with up to two samples each, and what stays. Changed after review: the places are the ones the name scan reads as this record by exactly that name, so these stay as they are and the preview counts them ("Left as they are: 1 marked Not a mention · 2 other uses of “Mara” (a longer name, another record's, or a sentence start)"): a longer name that holds it (renaming the nickname "Mara" leaves "Mara Vell"), another record's name ("Ella Harrow" when Tomas's "Harrow" is renamed), a span that may be someone else, a sentence start the scan does not count ("Will you…" beside "will"), and a place you marked Not a mention. Each place keeps its case: "HESK!" becomes "HASKEL!", and "The captain" at a sentence start becomes "The skipper" for the nickname "the captain". A name shared with another record is refused ("the book cannot tell whose it is everywhere"); rename a name only this record has. The preview runs the same checks as the rename (the name it already has, another of its names, another record's unshared name), so Rename stays off with the reason shown. Other drafts change only when you tick "Also rename in other drafts (+3)": no snapshot holds their text, so only Undo rename (until the project closes) puts them back. A snapshot "Before renaming "Hesk" to "Haskel"" is taken whenever the rename changes any text; a rename that changes only the name takes none. Notes, Research and sheets are not changed (P1-D10). The name keeps its settings (its case rule follows the new spelling when it was the default). A Not a mention mark whose phrase held a rewritten word ("Hesk, Mara, and") follows it; when an untouched draft still writes the old phrase, the old mark stays and the new phrase gets its own, and Undo takes the new one away. The open scene is the editor you were last in; other scenes open in a stack are reloaded, so their Ctrl+Z history starts again (as Replace in project does). If the open scene cannot be changed after the rest was renamed, the toast says so and its Undo is still this rename's. One Undo, in the toast (8 seconds) and on the sheet ("Undo rename to “Haskel”") for the session: each scene and draft text is put back only while it still reads as the rename left it (the ones you edited since are named and left), the name only if it was not changed again. A "Haskel: not a name" or "“Haskel” is not Hesk" dismissal the rename cleared does not come back with the undo.
  8. **The chat agent in v2** (`agent.v8` unchanged until P3, no prompt change): the tag and sheet reads (`tags`, `read_sheet`, `list_sheets`) and the edits `tag`, `rename_tag`, `sheet`, `create_sheet`, `merge_sheets`, `rename_sheet`, `recategorise`, `status`, `clear`, `todo`, `summaries`, plus deleting a sheet or a tag and the upload dialog (`open`; Research takes uploads), answer "not available in this project: names and records replaced tags and sheets; use lookup, cards, find_passages or read_scene". `lookup` reads the record: its names, its Pinned lines as [canon] (Not canon ones left out), Ideas as [idea], the scenes it is the POV of with their place and time, and where the book names it. `find_passages` searches the local index: the book, Pinned, notes and Research [canon], scene notes [plan], Ideas, Idea scenes and Not canon text [idea]; Research is labelled by its page title. `todo` lists your open to-dos with their scene and record, and how many are done; the agent never ticks one. `cards` with a name finds the scenes by the name scan. Changed after review: a passage from an Idea scene or a scene left out of the book reads "(an Idea scene: never happened)" or "(left out of the book: never happened)", a scene note "(notes for n3: a plan)", and only the book's own scenes say whether they come before or after now; `lookup` counts only the book's scenes in "Named in" and lists Idea and left-out scenes apart. An Organise request in an answer is dropped and counted, and the answer ends with "(Organise is not part of this project's story memory, so no plan follows. Tidy names on the record sheets with Same as or Split.)" (changed after review: the model is told to promise a plan); a message the router sends to Check consistency is answered by the agent as a cited question instead.
  9. **Data sharing:** rows may carry a v2 text, shown for a v2 project. The agent's names records, Research and to-dos in place of sheets, tag names and the To do list; proofread's says "the names and nicknames of your records" (planned for slice 5, added now because slice 1 already sends those).
- Alternatives: one Not a mention per chip for every place at once; done to-dos struck through in the list, or cleared after a day; to-dos always book-wide; a live spend figure; Rename in book only from the sheet (the open scene would then always be written by main and lose its Ctrl+Z history); renaming every whole-word match of the name (the first build: it rewrote "Mara Vell", other records' names and Not a mention places); renaming a shared name for every owner at once, or for this record only (its unsure places would then read as the other owner's); renaming the longer names that hold a renamed nickname ("Mara Vell" to "Mira Vell") in the same go; other drafts renamed by default, or kept in a snapshot of their own; an undo that also restores cleared dismissals; keeping `open` (the upload dialog) for the agent in v2.
- To change it: `src/renderer/features/thisScene/` (`ThisScenePanel.tsx`, `thisSceneView.ts`, `DismissedDialog.tsx`, the question, to-do and Dismissed stores), `src/renderer/features/records/RenameInBook.tsx` and `renameStore.ts`, `src/renderer/features/editor/renameInEditor.ts`, `src/main/memory/renameStore.ts` (and `checkAliasRename`/`renameAlias`/`restoreAliasText` in `recordStore.ts`), `src/shared/memory/rename.ts` (`planRename`, `renameReplacement`, `notMentionMoves`), `replaceAtPositions` in `src/shared/replace.ts`, `toggleThisScene` in `src/shared/menu.ts`, `src/main/ai/agentToolsV2.ts` (`AGENT_V2_REFUSED_TOOLS`, `AGENT_V2_REFUSED_EDITS`, `passageMark`), `routeForMemory` in `src/shared/assistantRoute.ts`, `sendsV2`/`dataSharingSends` in `src/shared/aiSettings.ts`, `dockPanelLabel` in `src/shared/dock.ts`.

## 2026-10-12 · F-16.6, F-16.7, F-16.11, F-16.4 · P1 slice 3 (sheets, labels, typed details, the v2 shell): calls made while building
- Question: Slice 3 of P1 (the screens for records, labels and typed details, and the v2 editor) needed calls the plan did not spell out. Nothing here changes your installed app: it runs story memory v1, whose section list, editor, search request, scene details and Settings tabs are exactly as before (the section list is pinned by a test against the old code).
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **Making a record:** *New character…* (and Insert › Character/Setting/World-building note) opens a dialog that checks the name as you type. A name that looks like an existing record says so ("Looks like Mara Vell (already called “Mara”)") and offers *Same as Mara Vell*, which adds what you typed to Mara Vell as a nickname (unless it already is one of her names) and opens her sheet, or *Create anyway*. While the warning shows, the plain Create button is off, so the choice is always explicit. A name on Dismissed says so and offers *Create anyway* only. There is no quick-add field at the foot of the list (the check needs the dialog).
  2. **The sheet:** the name at the top is edited in place. Each nickname has a choice of how it is matched: *As a name* (the default: where it is capitalised, and in exact case when the book also writes it in lower case), *Exact case* or *Any case*, a *Shared* tick that names the other records sharing it, and × (it goes to Dismissed, where Undo brings it back). Adding a nickname another record already has (a surname) asks "Share “Harrow”?"; Share marks it shared on that record too and adds it shared here, Cancel keeps what you typed (changed after review). A name change that is refused keeps the typed nickname in its field and puts the Name back as it was. The record's own name has the same choice. *Same as…* lists every other record, says what moves ("2 names and the mentions in 3 scenes"), and keeps the sheet you are on; that record's Pinned and Ideas are added below this one's. *Split…* moves the ticked nicknames to a new record named after the first. *Delete* asks first. *Named in* lists the manuscript's scenes in reading order with how often each names the record (a scene placed outside the manuscript no longer counts there or in the lists); a click opens the scene with the first mention selected, hyphenated names included (changed after review). Records are matched only by the names on the sheet, never by the book's text yet (no reader before P2a).
  3. **Pinned and Ideas** are pages inside the sheet. Pinned has the Not canon mark per paragraph; Ideas has no such button, since nothing on it is ever canon (changed after review). They save a second after you stop typing and when the sheet closes, and they are in the crash-recovery journal like a scene: after a crash the recovery prompt lists them as "Mara Vell (Pinned)" (changed after review).
  4. **Labels:** made in one Labels dialog (*Manage labels…* beside every label picker): name, colour, kind (Tone, Content, Theme, Custom, each with a hint of what it does), order (up and down), delete (asks first; the label comes off everything, and every filter on it, word search's included, is cleared). Until slice 5 the dialog says labels only filter and nothing about them is sent to the AI; the steering of tone, content and theme arrives with the Story notes block, which rewords it (changed after review). A new label is Tone unless you pick another kind. Any scene, chapter, part or Research page can carry labels; front and end matter documents too. Each place has its own filter: the Manuscript tree (whose count includes front and end matter, as the tree does), the Research tree, the Outline and word search; a filter is not remembered when the project closes (the v1 tag filter was).
  5. **Typed details** replace Location, POV and Timeline in a manuscript node's scene details in v2: POV lists Characters and ends with *New character…*, Place lists Places and ends with *New place…* (each opens the record dialog, name check included; the record made, or the one you pick with *Same as*, is chosen and you stay in the scene) (changed after review), plus a free-text line for the place, Time is free text, Story year a whole number, *+ also here* any record. Each saves at once. v1's timeline link is not shown (the Timeline is hidden in v2 until P2b). The Outline's Threads view is hidden in v2 for the same reason.
  6. **The v2 editor:** an existing inline tag shows as its stored name in muted text (no #, no colour, no menu) until the switch-over turns it into text; a tag range shows nothing. Typing # is just a character. The right-click menu over a selection keeps only Rewrite and Ask AI; with AI off it is the browser's own menu. The Tags panel and its header button are hidden, and View › Tags and Tools › Tags are not in a v2 project's menu (changed after review; slice 4 gives the panel's slot to This scene). The References panel keeps your pins but not its "In this scene" list, which read v1's tags (slice 4's This scene panel replaces it). Settings has no Story bible tab in v2. The near-name underline (F-3.14) reads the records' names and nicknames.
  7. **Search:** v2 offers Documents, Notes, Sheets and Source documents; a sheet is found by its name, a nickname ("Nicknames"), Pinned ("Pinned") or Ideas ("your idea"), and opens the sheet. The tag filter becomes a label filter; sheets carry no labels, so a label filter leaves them out.
  8. **Two read channels the plan did not list:** `label:links` (every label on every node, for the chips and filters) and `names:byRecord` (how often each scene names each record, for *Named in* and the lists).
  9. **Dropping a file on the window** in v2 adds it to Research as a page (the Research tab opens on it), as *Add files…* does; nothing is sorted and no AI runs. The Library is hidden in v2, so a drop never goes there (added after review).
- Alternatives: let Enter create anyway while warned; a quick-add field without the check; keep a single tag-style label filter for every view, remembered with the session; leave Pinned and Ideas out of crash recovery; show v1's timeline link and Threads view in v2 until P2b; keep the tag menu items and explain; a Shared tick on the add-nickname field instead of the Share question; drops into a Research folder you pick.
- To change it: `RecordCreateDialog.tsx`, `RecordSheet.tsx`, `recordView.ts` and `recordStore.ts` in `src/renderer/features/records/`; `labelStore.ts`, `LabelsDialog.tsx`, `LabelPicker.tsx`, `LabelFilterBar.tsx` in `src/renderer/features/labels/`; `SceneDetailsFields.tsx` and `MetadataPane.tsx`; `pageStore.ts` (journal); `buildSections` in `sectionList.ts`; `InlineTag.ts`, `TagRange.ts`, `DocumentEditor.tsx`; `menuFor` and `MenuItem.memory` in `src/shared/menu.ts`; `ReferencesBody` in `ReferencePanel.tsx`; `DropOverlay.tsx` and `researchStore.addDropped`; `holdersOf`/`sharingInput` in `recordView.ts`; `openNameMention` in `openPassage.ts`; `namedInCounts` in `src/main/memory/nameIndexer.ts`; `RECOVERY_KINDS` in `src/shared/recovery.ts`; `settingsTabsFor`; `searchTypesFor` and `RECORD_SEARCH_FIELD` in `src/shared/search.ts`, `searchProject` in `src/main/search/searchStore.ts`; `OutlineTab.tsx`.

## 2026-10-11 · F-16.8, F-16.6 · P1 slice 2 (the Research section and Not canon pages): calls made while building
- Question: Slice 2 of P1 (the Research section, uploads as editable pages, the Not canon marks) needed calls the plan did not spell out. Nothing here changes your installed app: it runs story memory v1, has no Research section, and its search offers the same five types.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **The same file twice:** uploading a file that is already in Research (same name, same contents) adds nothing and says "Already in Research as …". A different file with the same name becomes a second page, and each page keeps its own original, so *Open original* always opens the file that page was made from. (The old Library treated a same-named file as a newer version and replaced the original.) Once the page is deleted, the same file can be added again. A page's original belongs to Research: the Library section no longer lists it, a file of the same name added to the Library (its dialog or a drop on the window) becomes a Library file of its own, and the Library's Update is refused on it. A file already in the Library that you then add to Research (same name, same contents) becomes that page's original and leaves the Library list (the Library is hidden in v2 from slice 3).
  2. **How uploads read:** Word keeps its headings, paragraphs, bold and italics; Markdown its headings and emphasis; a text file one paragraph per line (or per block when it is hard-wrapped). Headings deeper than level 3 show as level 3, a line of `* * *` becomes a break, and blank lines are dropped (a Research page has no scenes to split). The paragraphs carry the same "imported" mark as an imported manuscript. A PDF becomes its text, marked "Converted from PDF"; a scanned PDF with no text layer gives an empty page you can type into, with *Open original* beside it. An image is a page with one empty caption line and *Open original*; there is no inline picture yet.
  3. **Saving and counting:** a Research page saves like any document (autosave, crash recovery) but counts no words written, towards no goal and in no statistic. Its word count shows in the Research tree only.
  4. **Snapshots and duplicates:** project snapshots leave Research out, and a snapshot of a single Research page is refused ("Research documents are not part of snapshots"). *Duplicate* on a Research page or folder keeps each page's original and its Not canon switch.
  5. **Search:** a Research hit opens the page (it does not yet jump to the words; P3 can add that). The tag filter in search finds no Research page, because Research carries no tags. In story memory v2 the search offers a sixth chip, "Source documents", on by default.
  6. **Not canon details:** pressing Enter in a Not canon paragraph keeps the new paragraph Not canon; an AI mark (`aiFiled`, `aiWritten`) is never carried into the new, empty line. Making text bold, switching Not canon, or the app's own writes never clear an AI mark; any change to the paragraph's words by you does (typing in it, joining another paragraph onto it, splitting it mid-sentence, Replace in project), and Undo brings the mark back with the words. Enter at the end or the start of a marked line, or Backspace on the empty line below it, leaves its words and so its mark. Headings can be marked Not canon too (an upload keeps its headings), and the search index reads a marked heading as Not canon.
  7. **Where things sit:** a Research folder in the main pane shows "Select a Research document to open it." (no stacked view or cork board: its pages are not scenes). A Research page has no notes pad ("A Research page is its own note: write on the page.") and no tags in the Tags column. In the sidebar, Research comes first of the tools, right after the story bible, until slice 3 hides the v1 sections (P1-D8). New notes and folders go beside the selected Research item, else at the end of Research. The upload dialog is the Library's ("Add worldbuilding documents and images").
  8. **The chat until P3:** in story memory v2 the chat assistant does not see Research at all: no line in its outline, nothing it can read, merge, move or write into, and an open Research page is not what it looks at. Ms Scribe reads Research from P3, with every passage labelled and Not canon text never taken as true.
- Alternatives: treat a same-named file as a new version of the page's original (as the Library does); keep blank lines and turn double blanks into breaks (the manuscript importer's rule); count Research words in the session total; take a snapshot of a single Research page; let the tag filter search Research by its text; let the chat read Research pages before P3 with a "source document" or "your notes" label.
- To change it: `addResearchFiles`, `researchPage` and `blocksToPage` in `src/main/research/researchStore.ts`; `addContextFileOutcomes` (`sameName`) and `researchOriginalIds` in `src/main/library/libraryStore.ts`; `loadAgentProject` in `src/main/ai/agentTools.ts`; `NotesContent` in `src/renderer/features/editor/NotesPanel.tsx`; `takeSnapshot` in `src/main/snapshot/snapshotStore.ts`; `copyResearchMeta`; `searchProject` in `src/main/search/searchStore.ts` and `jumpToResult` in `src/renderer/features/search/searchJump.ts`; `PageParagraph` and `clearAiMarksTransaction` in `src/renderer/features/editor/pageParagraph.ts`; `PaneContent` in `src/renderer/app/App.tsx`; `buildSections` in `src/renderer/features/shell/sectionList.ts`; `researchTarget` in `src/renderer/features/research/researchView.ts`.

## 2026-10-11 · F-16.4, F-16.5, F-16.7, F-16.10, F-16.11 · P1 slice 1 (records, names, the local index): calls made while building
- Question: Slice 1 of P1 (the data layer: tables, the name and passage index, the v1/v2 gate; no screens yet) needed calls the plan did not spell out. Nothing here changes your installed app: it runs story memory v1, and every new channel refuses to answer there.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **Names compare without full stops:** "Mr. Harrow" and "Mr Harrow" are one name (the plan's normal form plus this). Titles still count in the prose: "Mr. Harrow" and "Mrs. Harrow" stay two people.
  2. **The twin check, in detail:** a new name matches an existing one with titles kept first (spelled one way: "Capt" is "Captain"), then with titles taken out; a one-word name is also compared, for spelling slips, with each word of a longer name ("Liesl" against "Liesel Harrow"). The word-subset rule works both ways, so a shared surname makes every new family member look like a twin. For a name you type yourself that is only a warning; for a name the app would suggest it becomes a nickname question instead.
  3. **Sentence starts:** "Then Mr. Pruett came" is read as Mr. Pruett, but "Jem Falsire came in" stays an unknown name (not Falsire). The first word of a sentence is dropped for certain only when it is a common sentence opener (a built-in list of about 100 words such as Then, Old, Dearest). A title is never dropped ("Mrs. Harrow bowed" is not Harrow, as mid-sentence). A word your scene also writes in lower case ("Rose Harrow" beside "the rose") is dropped only to give an uncertain, dimmed match, never a certain one (changed after review, 2026-10-11).
  4. **Other matching details:** "Mr. Varrow of Elsdale" is tried whole and then in its parts (both are named), and so is a name joined by "the"; a name joined by a surname particle ("Anne de Vere", "Jan van Eyck") is kept whole, so it is never the "Vere" or "Eyck" of another record (changed after review). "the Captain" is matched with its "the"; a plural of a person's name ("the Ashbys") is shown as uncertain rather than as that person.
  5. **Removing a nickname** puts it on Dismissed for that record, so it is not asked about again; adding it back takes it off. Renaming a record leaves no Dismissed row for its old name, and a name you make a record or a nickname leaves the "not a name" list (changed after review).
  6. **Name questions:** they are built from whole names as the scan reads them, so a recurring "Hesk Varn" is one question, not "Hesk" and "Varn", and adding it makes the scan find it (changed after review). A name must be capitalised mid-sentence at least twice; one you also write in lower case needs four (the rule the old proposed tags used). A record's name used on its own ("Rynna" for Rynna Falsire, or the surname alone) is asked about as a nickname, because the scan only matches whole names. A title with a name ("Mr. Vell" for Tomas Vell) is never offered as a nickname (design identity rule 2: you add those yourself), but "Mr. Pruett" with no record near it is asked about as a new name. A name you said is not a name stays silent even once a record with a similar name exists.
  7. **Idea and left-out scenes in the word index:** a scene inside a chapter or part marked Idea, or left out of compile, is labelled that way too.
  8. **Changes to the test book's labels (P0 test data):** Mrs. Harrow was listed in chapter 1 only because "Harrow" is inside "Mr. Harrow" (removed); Wexley in the Thornmere ball and Gullhaven in "The Captain" are named in the text and were missing (added); the twin case "Dunmoor" is two letters from "Dunmore", over the one-letter limit for a seven-letter name, so it is now "Dunmoore".
  9. **While a project runs the new memory,** scenes the old summary prompt read are still held back from re-reading (no spend you did not ask for), also from "Summarize all scenes", but the old conversion dialog never appears (it could not run there), and the background consistency check does not run (P4 replaces it). The switch-over (slice 6) and P2a's reader settle what happens to those old summaries.
  10. **Name length and count:** a nickname may be as long as a record name (200 characters), since *Same as…* turns the merged record's name into a nickname. A record keeps at most 30 other names; a nickname, a merge or a split that would go past that is refused with "Remove some first" (added after review).
  11. **Passage labels:** Ideas, Idea scenes and scenes left out of compile are stored as not canon in the local word index, like a paragraph you marked Not canon, and keep their own label (added after review).
- Alternatives: keep full stops significant; compare one-word names only with whole names; fold titles when matching mentions (rejected by the design); ask about every capitalised word twice, ordinary words included; drop a sentence's first word for certain whenever the scene writes it in lower case (rejected after review: "Rose Harrow" became Harrow); offer "Mr. Vell" as a nickname question.
- To change it: `normName`, `SENTENCE_OPENERS` and `NAME_PART_JOINERS` in `src/shared/memory/names.ts`; `twinGate` in `src/shared/memory/twinGate.ts`; `scanNames` in `src/shared/memory/nameScan.ts`; `setAliases`, `requireAliasRoom` and `forgetNotAName` in `src/main/memory/recordStore.ts`; `countNameSpans` and `nameQuestions` in `src/shared/memory/nameQuestions.ts`; `RECORD_ALIAS_MAX` in `src/shared/memory/records.ts`; `passageCanon` in `src/shared/memory/passages.ts`; `sceneStatuses` in `src/main/memory/passageIndexer.ts`; `src/main/memory/testBook/labels.ts`.

## 2026-10-11 · F-16 (story memory redesign) · Approved: the eight decisions, and what it supersedes
- Question: The from-zero story memory redesign (`docs/research/story-memory-2026-10-11/design.md`, the plan doc linked from that folder's `README.md`) ended with eight open decisions, each with a recommended default.
- **Decided by the author 2026-10-11: approved, build it.** The author took the recommended default on 1, 2 and 4–8, and changed 3:
  1. Build before launch (about 10 weeks). The launch line moves below the new milestone M8 Story memory (`FEATURES.md` §6).
  2. Plan links are applied automatically, marked AI-made, with *Unlink* (F-16.21).
  3. **Changed by the author.** The recommended default was "goes into Ideas unless you say pin it". Instead, when the author explicitly asks Ms Scribe to put something on a sheet ("add this to Mara's sheet"), it goes there (Pinned) with a quick confirmation and an Undo. When Ms Scribe cannot tell from context whether it is canon or an idea, it asks "Is this an idea or canon?" as a two-choice quick question (F-16.26).
  4. When the text disagrees with a typed POV, place or time, the text's value shows with the typed one dimmed, and the author is asked once (F-16.18, F-16.29).
  5. One typed story year per time jump carries forward for ages (F-16.23).
  6. Uploads are filed on the strong model with reasoning off, about 15¢ per 20,000 words (F-16.9).
  7. The Cloud monthly background cap defaults to $1 (F-16.19).
  8. Sheet text the AI wrote earlier is kept in a "Filed by AI earlier: please check" block marked Not canon (F-16.15).
- **Decided by Claude, unconfirmed (decision 3 against the chat modes):** in Auto, an explicit sheet write lands at once with the confirmation and Undo; in Ask, Ask's one click is the quick confirmation; Plan never writes. An explicit "save this idea" or "brainstorm" goes to Ideas without the question.
- **Superseded by the redesign (open entries closed without action):**
  - M7: `2026-10-08 · M7 (F-8.7, F-9.12–F-9.15, F-5.24) · Knowledge model: the plan's decisions`. Everything there except F-8.7 is replaced by F-16 and never ticked; F-8.7's backup stays and starts the switch-over.
  - F-9.17–9.19: the five 2026-10-10 entries on the List/Cards choice, sheet sync and Story bible settings. The redesign has one sheet view with no sync, so there is nothing for you to test there.
  - F-5.25 (two 2026-10-10 entries) and F-4.14 (three 2026-10-08 entries): replaced by Dismissed, *Rebuild memory* (F-16.10, F-16.19) and records with aliases (F-16.4).
  
  Each of these entries carries a one-line Superseded note below. During the build (Part 4 rules), edit requests about tags, entities, the knowledge model, Organise and To do are parked and logged here as superseded. The installed app keeps today's memory (`storyMemory: v1`) until you start the switch-over (F-16.31).
- Status: P0 (F-16.1–16.3) built, reviewed and ticked 2026-10-11; plan `docs/research/story-memory-2026-10-11/plans/P0.md`. Your one step in P0: run `npm run ai:probe -- -u` once with your OpenRouter key (a few cents); see the P0 entry below. P1 slice 1 (records, names and the local index; data layer) built 2026-10-11, slice 2 (Research) built and reviewed 2026-10-11, slice 3 (sheets, labels, typed details, the v2 shell) built 2026-10-12, slice 4 (the This scene panel, Needs a look, Dismissed, Rename in book, the agent in v2) built 2026-10-12, see their entries above; slices 5–6 are next.
- To change it: decision 3 lives in F-16.26 (`agent.v9`, P3); the others are in the F-16 lines named above and `design.md` › "Decisions (2026-10-11)".

## 2026-10-11 · F-16.4–16.15 · P1 plan: the calls the design left open
- Question: Planning P1 (`docs/research/story-memory-2026-10-11/plans/P1.md`, six slices, nothing built yet) needed calls the design did not make. Details are in the plan's §7.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **Data copies:** the copy from tags, sheets and uploads into the new tables happens at the switch-over you start, not in the migrations. The installed app keeps running the old memory after the migrations, so an early copy would go stale. The migrations only add empty tables.
  2. **The switch-over in P1 runs only on a duplicate**, from Help › Developer in a development build. Your installed app cannot start it until F-16.31.
  3. **Your custom tags become custom labels,** which stay local and no longer steer prose (they steered as "Themes" before). The dry run tells you, and you can change a label's kind to Theme.
  4. **A character or place tag you linked to a scene that never names them** becomes "+ also here" for that scene.
  5. **"Names that are also dictionary words"** are read from your manuscript (the name also appears in lower case, like "will"), since no word list ships. A record you create yourself is warned about by the twin check ("Looks like Rynna Falsire"), never silently blocked.
  6. **Rename in book** rewrites the manuscript in every draft, the name itself and the Dismissed entries. It leaves notes, Research and sheets to Replace in project.
  7. **Range tags** are left in your documents, hidden and inert, until the old tables go (P9). The only change to your prose is the inline-tag rewrite you approve on the consent screen.
  8. **Sections:** in the new memory, Timeline, Threads, To do, Changes, Index and Library are hidden from P1 on. Their content returns inside the This scene | Book panel in P2b–P4, and timeline years become typed story years at the switch-over.
  9. **Scene suggestions** get `notesSuggest.v3` in P1, so P2a's version is `notesSuggest.v4`.
- Alternatives: copy in the migrations and refresh at the switch-over; custom tags as Theme labels; keep the old sections visible until P2b.
- To change it: `plans/P1.md` §7 (P1-D1 to P1-D13), before the slice that builds it.

## 2026-10-11 · F-16.1–16.3 · P0 ground truth: the details the plan left open
- Question: Building P0 (`docs/research/story-memory-2026-10-11/plans/P0.md`) needed a few calls the plan did not make.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. **Your step:** `OPENROUTER_API_KEY=sk-or-… npm run ai:probe -- -u` once (about 5¢; it stops at 25¢, counting the table estimate for any call that reports no cost). One live run writes the dated report and, only where OpenRouter's prices differ from the table, `src/shared/modelPrices.ts` (check with `git diff`); prices it confirms keep `source: list`. Then `npm run eval:ai -- -u` and commit the report, its JSON, the price module and the registry. Without `-u` the run only compares (passes when the prices match, fails showing the differences); `AI_PROBE_REPLAY=<the saved .json> npm run ai:probe -- -u` then writes them without spending again. F-16.1 is ticked for the tooling (unit-tested, gates pass); the measurement is yours. **The Worker's first deploy waits for it** (`docs/OPERATOR-SETUP.md` 1.8): until then cached prompt tokens bill at the full input rate on Cloud. Changed after review 2026-10-11: the probe judges a cache discount against OpenRouter's listed price and the first uncached run's bill, never the table it is checking, so a stale table can no longer make a full-price run look discounted.
  2. The probe's unit fixtures are shaped after OpenRouter's documented answers, not recorded: this build machine cannot reach openrouter.ai. Your first run is the first real recording; the report says what it saw.
  3. A stored toggle whose id is retired is dropped on read and the rest of the row kept, so the stored Use AI level stays; a toggle whose value is not true/false reads as **off** (a broken toggle never turns a feature on). Toggles for ids this build does not know stay in the row on save, so a newer build reads its own off toggle back. Writes from Settings are strict: a malformed toggle is refused. Before, any odd toggle reset the whole row to Off. (Changed after review 2026-10-11 from "dropped, so it reads as on".) The same leniency now covers the diagnostics counts in app-state.json (a retired `ai.request.*` counter is dropped instead of resetting the whole file) and stored chat turns (a turn that no longer parses is dropped, never the whole history).
  4. The Usage history's new fields (`reasoningTokens`, `costReported`) are optional, so rows and fakes from before parse; a row with no field reads as "Estimated from the price table".
  5. The planned task costs in the registry add the design's 5 % for retries. After review they cover every request the design names: the re-pointed `synopsis.v2`, `notesSuggest.v3`, `reviewChat.v3`, P2b's `reader.v2`/`verify.v2` (priced like the versions they replace until measured), the 50 fast spot checks per rebuild, card refreshes, a widened open question (2.8¢; one in five, so the average is about 1.8¢) and the what-if (P5, 10.4¢). The heavy month reads $1.51 own key against the design's $1.60–2.30 (the design rounds open questions up to 2¢ each); tests guard index 11.8¢, open question 1.5¢ and its average, session 1.3¢, Check the book 3.5¢, the what-if and the month.
  6. The test book is about 7,800 words (5,550 adapted, 2,250 original), a little under the plan's 8,500. Its labels are a first pass for P2a to score against; correct any label you read differently. After review, its last real places (London, St. James's, Brighton) are renamed and the novel's first sentence is paraphrased, so no line gives the source away.
  7. If you attach your own DeepSeek key inside OpenRouter (BYOK), OpenRouter's `usage.cost` is only its fee; the ledger and the daily cap then count the fee plus `cost_details.upstream_inference_cost`, or the price-table estimate when the upstream cost is missing (added after review; this follows OpenRouter's documented fields, not a live BYOK run).
- To change it: `src/main/ai/probe/`, `src/shared/aiSettings.ts` (`StoredAiSettings`, `unknownFeatureToggles`), `src/shared/diagnostics.ts` (`DiagnosticCounterTally`), `src/shared/chat.ts` (`parseStoredConversations`), `src/shared/ai.ts` (`AiUsageRecent`), `PLANNED_TASK_COSTS` in `src/shared/aiCostNotes.ts`, `src/main/memory/testBook/`.

## 2026-10-11 · F-9.8 · Upload review: what counts as a conflict, and a name the story bible already has
- Question: You reported conflicts that were not conflicts, and Apply failing with 'A world-building item named "Fathoms" already exists'. Where is the line, and what happens to a sheet Apply cannot write?
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed): (1) A conflict is decided locally, word by word, not by the AI: case, accents, punctuation, apostrophes, spacing, and a plural's final s are ignored. Same words, or the sheet already saying everything the upload says: nothing to review. An empty sheet field, a placeholder (Unknown, TBD, TBC, n/a, todo), or an upload that keeps every word of the sheet's value and adds more ("Captain of the guard" → "Captain of the city guard since 1201"): a fill, listed under Updates, and the upload's longer value replaces the sheet's. Each side saying something the other does not ("34" against "35", "Stubborn" against "Gentle"), or a negation gained or lost ("married" against "not married"): a conflict, the sheet's value kept unless you pick the upload's. Two files saying the same thing, or one saying more of it, give one value instead of "A / A+". (2) The Fathoms error: the model filed one name under two categories (a place in one chunk, a world item in the next); the first claimed your World sheet and the second became a new "Fathoms". Now a group named after a sheet another group already fills joins it (its fields not of that category go to its details), and before Apply any new sheet whose name its category already has fills that sheet instead; two new sheets of one name in one category become one. (3) Apply writes each sheet on its own: if a create still collides, it fills the existing sheet (empty fields only; its own values stay, since you never saw that conflict); a sheet that cannot be written (deleted since the review, or a page over its length cap) is left out with a warning naming it and why, and everything else applies.
- Alternatives: let the AI judge conflicts (a second request per sheet, or a new prompt version telling the model which values contradict); treat any different text in a long field (Appearance, Background) as an addition and append it instead of asking; keep one failing sheet rolling back the whole Apply.
- To change it: `relateValues`, `PLACEHOLDER_VALUES`, `NEGATION_WORDS`, `settleNameClashes` in `src/shared/contextLibrary.ts`; `applyContextReview`, `asUpdateOf` in `src/main/library/apply.ts`.

## 2026-10-11 · F-5.26 · "Make this scene better": the details you did not specify
- Question: You decided that improvement requests run the edit passes, a vague one asks first with five quick picks, a specific one runs its pass, a selection is edited alone, and Plan never edits. Several details were open.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. A new router version (`route.v2`) with an `editPass` action carrying the pass (or "ask"), the target (selection / scene / chapter), and the chapter's name. Every routed message pays about 200 more input tokens (a cached prefix; about $0.000003 more per message on the fast default).
  2. Proofreading left the router's list: "proofread this" now runs the proofread *pass* (tracked changes) instead of the quick proofread above the chat. Typing exactly "Proofread" still runs the quick one.
  3. A rewrite asked with no selection ("rewrite this scene darker") is now a custom edit pass on the scene carrying your words (it used to fall through to chat); with no words, it asks.
  4. A specific request starts at once without showing its estimate first (you asked for no question); its cost is in the pass's report and the ledger. On MythScribe Cloud a quote over the confirm threshold still stops and asks, as the Edits workspace does. The quick picks show each pass's estimate before you click.
  5. Plan: an edit request is answered by the read-only chat (as every other edit request is in Plan), so no picks appear there; a pick on an older turn is refused with the Plan message.
  6. Editor's notes is not offered for a chapter (it reads one scene). Dialogue is a custom pass with the built-in dialogue preset's instruction (your own words appended when you gave any).
  7. A selection under 20 characters or over 12,000 (one request) runs on the whole scene. The selected passage is stored on the pass (new column, migration 0027) so Resume reruns the same text.
  8. A chapter is found by its title ("The Crossing", "chapter 3") or by its place in the book ("chapter 3", "chapter three"); a name that matches nothing says so and starts nothing.
  9. Not done: teaching the chat agent itself (agent.v8) to hand whole-scene improvement to this route. The router now catches these requests before the agent sees them; changing the agent costs a new prompt version and an eval round. Say if you still see scattered edits for such requests.
- Alternatives: keep the quick proofread for "proofread this"; show the estimate and a Start button even for specific requests; let Plan show the picks disabled as suggestions; offer Editor's notes per scene of a chapter.
- To change it: `src/main/ai/prompts/route.v2.ts`, `settleRoute` in `src/shared/assistantRoute.ts`, `src/shared/editRequest.ts` (choices, labels, `findChapter`), `runEditRequest` / `pickEdit` in `src/renderer/features/ai/assistantStore.ts`, `src/renderer/features/ai/editRequest.ts`, `EditRequestCard.tsx`.

## 2026-10-10 · F-5.6 · Scene mood and theme: where they come from and what uses them
- Question: You asked each scene to deduce a general mood and theme so edit suggestions stay in line with them. How are they read, stored, shown, and used?
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed; the idea is yours): (1) The per-scene reading deduces them in the same request (`summary.v5`, about 20 more output tokens, same cap); each is a short phrase of at most 60 characters. (2) No re-read: a scene read by `summary.v4` keeps its reading and gets a mood and theme only when it is next read because its text (or a name in it) changed; no conversion dialog, no cost on open. (3) Stored on the scene card's AI part (no migration) and shown on the scene card as Mood and Theme with the AI mark; not in the Outline row's one-line card. (4) Used by Rewrite in my voice (`rewrite.v4`, `rewriteRegen.v4`) and by the developmental, line, and custom edit passes (`editPass.v2`), as a short block after the scene steer; copy edit, proofread, and continuity passes never get it (they fix what is wrong whatever the mood); ghost text and What next do not either. (5) The chat agent sees them when it reads a scene card (the card line now ends with `Mood: …; Theme: …`); feeding them into the agent's own edits waits for the next agent prompt version.
- Alternatives: re-read the whole book once for them (one summary request per scene, about $0.0008 each on the fast tier); also send them to ghost text (every suggestion pays about 25 more input tokens); let you edit or override them on the scene card.
- To change it: `src/main/ai/prompts/summary.v5.ts`, `SUMMARY_CURRENT_MIN_VERSION` in `src/main/ai/summarize.ts`, `EDIT_PASS_MOOD_TYPES` in `src/main/ai/prompts/editPass.v2.ts`, `renderSceneMood` in `src/shared/sceneCard.ts`.

## 2026-10-10 · F-7.12 · Activity bar and drop notifications: details you did not specify
- Question: You described the bar, the drop, focus-mode behaviour, and Open. Several details were open.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed):
  1. The crawl: an exponential creep toward 90 % with a time constant per job (Organise 25 s, upload 20 s per step, edit pass 15 s per scene, book check 30 s, consistency check 15 s, re-read 8 s per scene); with real steps it crawls through the current step only, so a one-scene pass still moves.
  2. "Check consistency on demand" today checks one scene (`Check this scene`); that check is tracked.
  3. The conversion re-read is tracked from Update now until the index queue settles (empty, paused, or only failures left); its progress is the whole queue's, so a summary queued meanwhile counts too. A paused or failed queue is a failure with Retry (the queue's Resume).
  4. The book check's result toasts ("To do: 2 new items.", "Nothing changed since the last check.") are now the drop's second line instead of toasts, so the result is said once.
  5. Edit pass: the finish toast is gone (the drop says it). The report still opens on its own when the author is still watching the pass in the Edits workspace; anywhere else the pane is left alone and the drop's Open opens it.
  6. A drop leaves on its own once its result shows another way (run opened from the status bar, report open, findings view open) or is gone (run closed, pass deleted), and a new run of the same job clears its earlier drop.
  7. Entering focus mode hides drops already showing (they come back on exit), like the ones that arrive in focus mode after their 6 s.
  8. In the big review dialog Escape and a click outside only hide it ("Back to writing"); the run stays under its status-bar item. In the column Escape still stops or closes the run.
  9. Open on the book check leaves focus mode (the To do list is in the sidebar, which focus mode hides); the scene-card re-read has no Open (nothing to show).
  10. Drops sit under the header (at the window's top in focus mode), 420 px wide, accent left edge; failures have a danger edge and title. The bar's hover strip is 6 px over the header's own bottom padding.
- Alternatives: a linear crawl; keep the To do toasts and the edit pass toast beside the drop; drops that never auto-leave; Escape closing the run in the dialog too; To do opening its review deck over focus mode instead of the sidebar.
- To change it: `src/renderer/features/activity/activityJobs.ts` (`CRAWL_MS`, `outcomeOf`, `noteStale`), `activityStore.ts` (`FOCUS_NOTE_MS`, focus parking), `activityRoutes.ts` (Open, Retry), `src/renderer/features/sideWork/SideWorkFrame.tsx` (the dialog's Escape).

## 2026-10-10 · F-9.19 · Story bible settings: what each part does
- Question: You asked for a Settings tab to edit each category's fields, the write-up style (headings vs paragraphs, order, length), and the default view of new sheets.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed): (1) the page order is the field order you set in the same tab (one order, not two); (2) each field is Heading or Paragraph, one-line fields default to Paragraph and long ones to Heading; the length is per category; (3) fields are saved with a button (renames, reorders, additions and removals together) because a removal moves text into Notes on every sheet of the category, which asks first and names how many sheets; (4) Notes cannot be removed or renamed and stays last (removals need somewhere to go); (5) Threads are not editable here (their fields drive the thread view); (6) the default view applies to the New … dialog and the quick add; sheets the AI or an upload makes keep their own choice.
- Alternatives: a separate page order; one length for the whole bible; instant saves with an Undo instead of a Save button.
- To change it: `src/renderer/features/entities/StoryBibleSettingsTab.tsx`, `src/main/entity/categoryFields.ts`, `src/shared/storyBibleSettings.ts`.
- **Superseded 2026-10-11** by the story memory redesign (F-16; see the 2026-10-11 F-16 entry at the top): closed without action.

## 2026-10-10 · F-9.18 · Sheet sync lands on its own in every chat mode
- Question: Should the sync follow the chat's Auto/Ask/Plan mode (held for Apply at Ask and Plan), or land on its own?
- Decided by the author 2026-10-10: lands on its own in every mode, with Undo. Both directions (the page written up from the fields, and page edits filed into the fields) are applied at once in Auto, Ask, and Plan, each logged in Changes (source "Sheet sync") with an Undo. There is no Apply/Dismiss step; the out-of-date bar (Write up now / File now / Try again, and the AI-off message) stays.

## 2026-10-10 · F-9.18 · How AI-written page text is marked, and why it fits AI rule 1
- Question: The Blank page now holds prose the AI wrote. How is it marked, and is it allowed?
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed): the page is derived from the author's own sheet text (the fields), never from the manuscript, and never enters the manuscript — the same footing as the derived index data rule 1 already allows (summaries, facts), but stored in the sheet because it is the sheet's other view. Each paragraph the AI writes is remembered by hash (`entity.sync.aiParagraphs`); the page shows "Written up by AI from your fields" or "N of M paragraphs written up by AI…". A paragraph the author edits stops matching and counts as theirs. Every write-up is costed in the ledger and has an Undo in Changes. Rule 2 (voice profile, brief, exemplars, fidelity check) is not applied: this is story-bible reference text, not manuscript prose, and the voice block would multiply its cost.
- Alternatives: mark AI paragraphs inline (the page would need a rich-text editor instead of the plain textarea); apply the voice block and fidelity check to write-ups.

## 2026-10-10 · F-9.18 · Existing sheets: what the first sync does
- Question: Sheets written before F-9.18 have fields, a page, or both, and no sync history. What happens to them?
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed): nothing runs on open (no bulk spend). A sheet syncs only after the author edits it (30 s pause) or clicks Write up now / File now. A **Blank page sheet** keeps its page exactly as written: the first sync only fills its fields from it. A sheet with **both** a page and fields keeps its page too (its fields are filled from the page; the page is written up only after a later field edit). A **Structured sheet** with no page gets its page written up. The migration adds two columns and touches no text; F-8.7's backup is taken before it, and every applied sync has an Undo.
- Alternatives: a one-time "sync every sheet" pass after the update (with a cost estimate, like the F-9.14 conversion); write the page of a both-texts sheet up from its fields at once.

## 2026-10-10 · F-9.18 · Safety rules and smaller calls in the sync
- Question: several details the request left open.
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed): (1) an emptied page never empties the fields, and fields with no text never empty the page (both just mark the views as agreeing); (2) an answer that comes back after the author edited the sheet again is dropped, and once a sync has landed the open page sends only what the author edited, so their typing wins in that view and nothing the sync wrote elsewhere is written back; (3) the pause is per sheet (editing another sheet does not hold this one back), and the due list is a settings row so a quit resumes it; (4) the page is plain text: headings are the field labels on a line of their own, laid out by the app (the model writes only prose), shown in the prose font; (5) the write-up length is a ceiling (short ≈ 120, medium ≈ 250, long ≈ 500 words) and never adds facts; (6) the page is marked out of date when the fields' labels, order, the sheet's own fields, or the category's write-up style change, but nothing re-runs until the sheet is edited or Write up now is clicked; (7) a filing sends only the paragraphs removed and added since the views last agreed and gets back compact edits (never whole field values); added text over 3,000 characters is filed in several requests; (8) a write-up over the input budget cuts the longest field values to fit; a filing over it is refused with the budget message (rare); (9) a page addition with no fitting field becomes a field of the sheet's own (at most 12; past that it goes into Notes as "Label: text"); (10) "Write up now" / "File now" / "Try again" skip the 30 s pause.
- Alternatives: per-section incremental write-ups (cheaper on long sheets; listed in the cost registry ideas); a rich-text page with real headings.
- To change it: `src/shared/sheetSync.ts`, `src/main/ai/sheetSync.ts`, `src/main/ai/sheetSyncService.ts`.
- **Superseded 2026-10-11** by the story memory redesign (F-16; see the 2026-10-11 F-16 entry at the top): closed without action.

## 2026-10-10 · F-5.25 · Clear the story bible: what a fresh start forgets and keeps
- Question: You asked for one confirm card with checkboxes, a backup first, one Changes entry, and one Undo. Three details were open: does a clear remember the deleted names (so the background reading and tagging do not make them again, as a one-by-one delete does)? What happens to pictures and uploaded originals? What does "the story bible" tick?
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed): (1) A clear forgets nothing onto the dismissed lists: you are starting fresh, so the readings and your re-upload may make the same sheets and tags again (a dismissed name would also stop the reading from stating facts about a re-uploaded sheet of that name). (2) Pictures and uploaded originals stay on disk so Undo can put the sheets and uploads back; they are not cleaned up afterwards. (3) "The story bible" and "start fresh" tick every sheet category, every tag category, and the Library uploads (so an identical re-upload is not skipped as unchanged); notes only when you name them. (4) Undo refuses whole once something of the same name exists again (after a re-upload) or a cleared document has notes again, and points to the backup taken just before the clear, rather than doubling sheets.
- Alternatives: (1) remember the names as a one-by-one delete does; (2) delete the files at once (Undo then brings sheets back without pictures); (3) leave tags or uploads unticked by default; (4) let Undo double up, or merge into what was made since.
- To change it: `src/main/knowledge/bibleClear.ts` (`clearStoryBible`), `src/main/knowledge/clearSnapshot.ts` (`assertRestorable`), `src/main/ai/prompts/agent.v7.ts` (`AGENT_BULK_RULES`, a new prompt version).
- **Confirmed by the author 2026-10-10:** (1) a clear allows the AI to make the same names again.
- **Superseded 2026-10-11** by the story memory redesign (F-16; see the 2026-10-11 F-16 entry at the top): closed without action.

## 2026-10-08 · F-4.13, F-4.12b, F-9.10 · How the tag rule reads "ordinary word"
- Question: You said tags should be capitalised words or abnormal terms ("memorial fragments"), and that an ordinary word used often in an abnormal way can be a potential tag. How does the app tell a name from an ordinary word?
- Chosen (decided by Claude, unconfirmed): a local rule over your own manuscript, no word list and no prompt change (`src/shared/tagTerms.ts`). A term is a **name** when the text always writes it capitalised (Marta, the Tide Reckoning), a **coined term** when it is a phrase of several words the text repeats 3+ times (memorial fragments), **unusual** when it is an ordinary word the text also capitalises mid-sentence 4+ times ("the Trial" beside "a fair trial"), otherwise **ordinary** (or absent). Background tagging creates new tags only for names and coined terms, in every category, so themes and tones the scene never names ("loneliness", "dread") are no longer made; tags already in your bank are still linked. Unusual words are proposed in the tag bar (F-4.12b now proposes a word that also appears in lower case once it is capitalised mid-sentence 4 times). Organise offers AI-made ordinary-word tags in "Not names — remove?" (removing one also stops the AI making it again), never your own tags, and no longer treats two ordinary words as duplicates.
- Alternatives: ship an English word list (~10k words) to call "skyglass" coined even in lower case (today a single lower-case coined word counts as ordinary); keep AI-made theme and tone tags (only names filtered); a prompt change (`summary.v4`) asking the model for names only.
- To change it: `src/shared/tagTerms.ts` (`classifyTagTerm`, `TERM_PHRASE_MIN_REPEATS`), `src/shared/proposedTags.ts` (`PROPOSED_ORDINARY_MIN_MENTIONS`), `src/main/ai/autoTags.ts`, `src/main/organise/organiseProject.ts` (`ordinaryTags`).
- Confirmed by the author 2026-10-10 with an addition: tags stay names and coined terms, "but also each scene should deduce a general mood and theme so that when it suggests edits it stays in line with that mood and theme." Built as F-5.6's mood and theme (`summary.v5`): two short phrases on the scene card, marked AI-made, never tags; see the entry below.

(6) The rail turns into a dropdown below about 670 px of dialog width.
